# In pursuance of sub Section 5B of Section 18 of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, a copy of the audited accounts and the Report of Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India

NOTIFICATION New Delhi, the 31st July, 2026 No. 1-CA(5)/77/2026.—In pursuance of sub-Section (5B) of Section 18 of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, a copy of the audited accounts and the Report of Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) for the year ended 31st March 2026 is hereby published for general information. 77th Annual Report Transforming Profession: Building Trust, Embracing Innovation, Strengthening Nation Building Trust The story of India's rise as a leading global economy is not merely a story of economic expansion; it is a story of trust, innovation, and institutional strength. At every stage of this journey, the accountancy profession has served as a vital enabler, providing the credibility, transparency, and confidence necessary for sustainable progress. As the nation advances towards the vision of Viksit Bharat, the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) continues to play a transformative role by nurturing a profession that combines integrity with innovation and expertise with public purpose.

Trust remains the cornerstone of economic development. Investors, businesses, governments, and citizens rely on accurate and transparent financial information to make informed decisions. Chartered Accountants have long been the custodians of this trust, ensuring that financial reporting, and governance frameworks operate with the highest standards of professionalism and accountability.

Through their commitment to ethics and public interest, they strengthen the foundations of markets, reinforce confidence in institutions, and contribute to a stable and resilient economic ecosystem.

As economic activities become increasingly interconnected across borders, trust must extend beyond national boundaries. Indian businesses today operate in a global environment where comparability, consistency, and credibility of financial information are indispensable. ICAI has actively facilitated this transition by aligning professional standards with international best practices, promoting global partnerships, and strengthening the international mobility of Indian professionals. These efforts have enhanced the reputation of the Indian accountancy profession and positioned Chartered Accountants as trusted contributors to the global economy.

Embracing Innovation The profession is simultaneously undergoing a profound transformation driven by technology. Artificial intelligence, machine learning, blockchain, data analytics, and automation are redefining how financial information is generated, analysed, and assured. Rather than viewing these developments as disruptions, ICAI has embraced them as opportunities to elevate professional value. By equipping members with future-oriented skills and encouraging the adoption of advanced technologies, the Institute is enabling a shift from traditional compliance-driven functions towards strategic advisory, predictive insights, and value creation.

Innovation within the profession extends beyond technology. It encompasses new approaches to governance, risk management, sustainability, and stakeholder engagement. Modern enterprises are increasingly evaluated not only by financial performance but also by their environmental and social impact. Recognising this evolution, ICAI has been at the forefront of developing professional capabilities in sustainability reporting, ESG assurance, integrated reporting, and responsible business practices. By enhancing the reliability of non-financial disclosures, the profession is helping organisations build longterm stakeholder confidence while contributing to national and global sustainability goals.

A future-ready profession also requires continuous learning and adaptability. ICAI has consistently invested in capacity building through education, research, professional development, and specialised training initiatives. These efforts empower members to navigate emerging areas such as digital finance, cybersecurity, forensic accounting, valuation, public financial management, and sustainability assurance. By fostering a culture of lifelong learning, the Institute ensures that the profession remains relevant in an era of rapid economic and technological change.

Strengthening Nation Beyond corporate enterprises, Chartered Accountants contribute significantly to nation building through their involvement in public finance, tax literacy, financial inclusion, entrepreneurship development, and governance reforms. Their expertise supports efficient utilisation of public resources, strengthens accountability mechanisms, and promotes transparency in both public and private sectors. Whether advising start-ups, supporting MSMEs, facilitating infrastructure development, or contributing to policy implementation, the profession plays an important role in advancing inclusive and sustainable growth.

Global collaboration further amplifies the profession's impact. Through active engagement with international standard-setting bodies, professional organisations, and regulatory institutions, ICAI contributes Indian perspectives to global discussions while bringing international best practices to domestic stakeholders. This exchange of knowledge strengthens professional excellence and reinforces India's emergence as a trusted participant in the global financial architecture.

As the profession transforms, its core values remain unchanged. Ethics, independence, objectivity, and commitment to the public interest continue to define the identity of Chartered Accountants. These principles provide the foundation upon which innovation can flourish responsibly and growth can be sustained with confidence.

The future belongs to professions that can combine trust with technology, expertise with adaptability, and innovation with integrity. Guided by this vision, ICAI is shaping a new generation of professionals equipped to address emerging challenges and seize new opportunities. Through its unwavering commitment to excellence, the Institute continues to strengthen institutions, support economic progress, and contribute meaningfully to India's development journey.

In transforming the profession, ICAI is not merely preparing Chartered Accountants for the future; it is helping shape a future where trust drives progress, innovation creates opportunity, and professional excellence strengthens the nation.

Professional Excellence in Service of Nation Building As the accounting profession navigates an increasingly dynamic global environment, the ICAI continues to advance a vision anchored in trust, professional excellence, innovation, and nation building. Through sustained emphasis on ethical conduct, technological advancement, sustainability, capacity building, and thought leadership, the Institute remains committed to strengthening the profession's relevance and contribution to society.

The Institute established under an Act of Parliament, occupies a unique position in India's economic and institutional framework. As the regulator of the accountancy profession, a premier knowledge institution, a standard-setting body, and a trusted partner in public policy and governance, ICAI continues to contribute significantly towards strengthening India's financial architecture and supporting the nation's journey towards becoming a developed economy.

During the year, the Institute further strengthened its role as a catalyst for professional excellence, economic growth, public accountability, technological innovation, sustainability, and global engagement. Through a combination of regulatory leadership, capacity building, strategic collaborations, policy advocacy, educational reforms, and outreach initiatives, ICAI continued to uphold the highest standards of professionalism while responding proactively to emerging national and global developments.

By encouraging prudent savings habits, informed investment decisions, and greater participation in the formal financial system, ICAI is helping transform individual financial well-being into a powerful driver of national prosperity, exemplifying how a premier professional institution can become an important partner in advancing inclusive economic growth and realizing the vision of Viksit Bharat 2047.

As part of its commitment to financial inclusion, ICAI conducted financial and tax literacy sessions for Northen East States such as self-help groups in the Khasi and Garo regions and tribal communities in remote areas of Meghalaya, and women-led self-help groups in Mizoram and self-help groups Sikkim, thereby promoting financial awareness and empowerment at the grassroots level. ICAI established a World Record by conducting over 190 Financial and Tax Literacy Programs across India within nine months (March–November 2025), recognised by the World Book of Records, London.

Strengthening Financial Reporting, Auditing and Regulatory Frameworks As the statutory regulator of the accountancy profession, ICAI continued to play a pivotal role in strengthening India's financial reporting and regulatory architecture. During the year, the Institute actively engaged with policymakers on several landmark reforms, including the Income-tax Bill, 2025.

Through a dedicated expert group, ICAI submitted extensive recommendations aimed at simplifying the proposed legislation, including rationalisation of provisions and simplification of legal language.

Significantly, more than 90 suggestions submitted by the Institute were subsequently reflected in the final Income-tax Act, 2025, underscoring ICAI's role as a trusted technical advisor in tax policy formulation.

The Institute also continued its contribution to indirect tax reforms by providing recommendations on GST Annual Return (GSTR-9) and Reconciliation Statement (GSTR-9C), facilitating simplification, standardisation and ease of compliance for taxpayers.

The profession's commitment to trust and transparency was reflected in the remarkable achievement of crossing 10 crore Unique Document Identification Numbers (UDINs) during the year. The migration of the UDIN platform to ICAI DigiCA further strengthened security, accessibility and real-time verification capabilities, enhancing confidence in professional certifications across the financial ecosystem.

Transforming Professional Education and Developing Future-Ready Talent ICAI continued to advance and modernise professional education in line with evolving business and societal needs. To further improve the examination process, the facility of exam centre selection was introduced, reducing logistical challenges and enabling students to focus more effectively on their preparation. Additionally, the e-Diary was made mandatory for students commencing practical training on or after 1st January 2026, thereby enhancing transparency, standardisation and real-time monitoring of practical training.

Recognising the global standing of the qualification, ICAI successfully secured international benchmarking through ECCTIS, which assessed the Chartered Accountant qualification as comparable to a Master's degree under several international frameworks. This recognition is expected to substantially enhance global mobility and international career opportunities for Indian Chartered Accountants.

The Institute also demonstrated its commitment to youth development through nationwide skill-building initiatives. World Youth Skills Day 2025 was celebrated across more than 130 cities through over 130 programmes focused on leadership, entrepreneurship, communication and digital competencies.

Additionally, ICAI achieved a Guinness World Record through its Career in Accounting and Finance for Youth programme, which reached over 7,400 students, teachers and principals in a single career guidance session.

Capacity Building and Strengthening Public Financial Management As a key institution supporting governance and public sector reforms, ICAI expanded its contribution to capacity building across government institutions and public sector entities. Pursuant to its Memorandum of Understanding with the Comptroller and Auditor General of India, ICAI successfully conducted a 32-day residential capacity-building programme for 50 C&AG officers at its Centre of Excellence, Hyderabad.

The Institute continued to provide faculty support and technical expertise to various state governments and public institutions, including the Panchayati Raj Department of Bihar, the Treasuries and Accounts Department of Tamil Nadu, and Metropolitan Transport Corporation, Chennai.

ICAI's Accounting Research Foundation further strengthened public sector financial reforms through its continuing partnership with Indian Railways. Since 2014, the Foundation has served as technical partner for railway accounting reforms and has successfully prepared accrual-based financial statements for six consecutive financial years from 2017-18 to 2022-23. Now the project for preparation of Accrual Based Financial Statements of Indian Railways for the financial years 2023-24 and 2024-25 has been awarded to ICAI ARF.

The Institute also expanded its contribution to local governance and urban finance through collaborations with the National Institute of Urban Affairs, Mahatma Gandhi State Institute of Public Administration and Government Accounting Standards Advisory Board, thereby strengthening financial accountability and governance at the grassroots level.

Empowering MSMEs and Driving Entrepreneurial Growth Recognising MSMEs as a cornerstone of India's economic development, ICAI intensified its outreach and support initiatives during the year. The sector currently contributes more than 30 per cent of India's GDP and approximately 35.4 per cent of manufacturing output, making it a critical driver of growth and employment.

To strengthen this ecosystem, ICAI organised MSME Mahotsav and MSME & Startup Conclave 2025, bringing together more than 3,000 stakeholders, including entrepreneurs, policymakers, investors and professionals. Through a nationwide campaign conducted across ICAI's extensive network, the Institute provided free advisory support, Udyam registration assistance, compliance guidance and access to financial institutions.

A landmark achievement was recorded on International MSME Day, when ICAI mentored 19,355 MSMEs across the country in a single day, earning recognition from the World Book of Records, Asia Book of Records and India Book of Records. Building on this momentum, the Institute launched MSME Clinics across 183 branches and regional offices to provide structured professional support in finance, taxation, accounting, compliance, digital transformation and business sustainability.

Strategic partnerships with organisations such as NSIC, NPCI, Startup Tamil Nadu, FITT-IIT Delhi and various state governments further strengthened ICAI's role in promoting entrepreneurship, financial inclusion and business competitiveness.

Leading Digital Transformation The Institute continued to position itself at the forefront of technological transformation. The AI Innovation Summit 2025 attracted leading technology experts, policymakers and academicians to deliberate on the future of accounting, auditing, taxation and governance in an AI-driven world.

The Institute also intensified efforts to integrate emerging areas such as Artificial Intelligence, sustainability, fintech, data analytics and digital transformation into professional development. During the year, over 450 batches of the Artificial Intelligence Certificate Course (AICA Level 1) were conducted, attracting more than 20,000 registrations from Chartered Accountants across the country.

Building on this success, ICAI launched the advanced AICA Level 2 programme and expanded AI learning opportunities to overseas chapters.

ICAI's Digital Accounting and Assurance Board completed 25 years of pioneering work in digital transformation, during which it has trained more than 35,000 professionals in systems audit and over 14,000 professionals in forensic accounting and investigations. Through the development of the Forensic Accounting and Investigation Standards (FAIS) and the Information Systems Auditing Standards (ISAS), ICAI has been at the forefront of establishing global benchmarks and advancing professional excellence in these emerging domains.

The Digital Transformation Finance Summit 2025 witnessed participation from more than 1,300 delegates, while Future Proof Forensics 2025 brought together over 130 regulators, banking leaders and financial institutions to discuss fraud prevention, cyber resilience and digital trust.

Automated Web Based Software for allotment of Audit Assignment designed by ICAI utilized by 6 Public Sector Banks for the year 2025-26 including Bank of Baroda, Punjab National Bank, Canara Bank, Indian Bank, Bank of Maharashtra and Union Bank of India.

Advancing Sustainability and Responsible Business Practices Sustainability remained a major strategic priority for ICAI during the year. Through the Sustainability Reporting Standards Board, the Institute launched Sustainability Month 2025, engaging members, students and communities through awareness campaigns, surveys, tree plantation drives, walkathons and educational initiatives aligned with the Government of India's Lifestyle for Environment (LiFE) mission.

ICAI organised several high-level sustainability platforms, including the National Conference on Responsible Business Conduct and biodiversity-focused initiatives in collaboration with the United Nations Global Compact Network India. More than 200 industry leaders, policymakers, regulators and professionals participated in these deliberations.

A notable achievement was the launch of ICAI's Carbon Emission Calculator, a free digital tool designed to assist organisations and individuals in measuring and managing carbon footprints, thereby supporting India's net-zero ambitions.

Expanding Global Presence and Promoting India as a Global Professional Services Hub ICAI strengthened its international footprint and positioned Indian Chartered Accountants as globally respected professionals. At present, the global network of the Institute is in 47 countries of the world with 54 overseas chapters, 31 representative offices and 85 cities.

Two new overseas chapters were launched in Los Angeles and Atlanta, further deepening ICAI's presence in North America. The Institute also participated in global standard-setting forums, including meetings of XBRL International, IESBA and IAASB, ensuring that Indian perspectives continue to shape international developments in accounting, auditing and ethics. Recognising the growing importance of Global Capability Centres (GCCs), ICAI launched the GCC Summit Series to promote India as a global Accounting GCC hub. Organised across major cities, the summits brought together industry leaders and policymakers to discuss the evolving GCC landscape and the opportunities it presents for Chartered Accountants to take on strategic leadership roles. ICAI also submitted a draft GCC framework to the Government of West Bengal to support structured GCC growth.

As part of its efforts to promote the export of accounting and financial services, ICAI introduced a Certificate Course on Overseas Outsourcing Services to equip professionals with the knowledge and skills required to serve clients in the UK, Australia and Canada markets.

The World Forum of Accountants (WOFA 2.0) emerged as one of the largest global gatherings of the profession, attracting more than 10,000 delegates and over 250 speakers from around the world. The event featured 21 plenary sessions and 42 concurrent sessions, firmly establishing India as a leading centre for professional thought leadership.

Complementing these efforts, ICAI actively contributed to the India-UK Free Trade Agreement, securing provisions relating to professional services, recognition of qualifications and outsourcing opportunities.

These measures are expected to create significant global opportunities for Indian Chartered Accountants and professional firms.

ICAI is moving forward towards Viksit Bharat#2047 making India a developed nation by 2047 , the 100th year of Century of the India's independence.

1.

THE COUNCIL The Twenty-Sixth Council was constituted on 12th February 2025. It comprises of 32 elected members and 8 members nominated by the Central Government. Composition of the 26th Council is disclosed separately. 10 Council meetings were held during financial year 2025-26.

2.

COMMITTEES OF THE COUNCIL The Council in terms of Section 17 of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 constitutes every year various Standing and Non-Standing Committees/Boards and Groups to ensure different activities of the Institute for regulation and development of the profession of Chartered Accountancy. During the financial year ended 31st March 2026, 528 meetings of the said Standing and Non-Standing Committees / Boards and Groups were held.

3.

AUDITORS M/s Jagdish Chand & Co & M/s A S A & Associates, LLP were the joint auditors of the Institute for the financial year 2025-26 appointed from the panel maintained by Comptroller and Auditor General of India. The Council wishes to place on records its appreciation of the services rendered by them.

4.

STANDING COMMITTEES The Council of ICAI constitutes the following Standing Committees as per the Section 17 of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 :

4.1 Executive Committee

4.2 Finance Committee

4.3 Examination Committee

4.1 Executive Committee The functions of this Committee are defined in Regulation 175 of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988 which include matters relating to employees, articled and audit assistants and enrolment, removal, restoration of members from the Register, cancellation of certificate of practice, permission to engage in any other business or occupation other than profession of accountancy.

Executive Committee is also the custodian of the property, assets and funds of the Institute beside maintenance of the Institute's offices. The Executive Committee met 10 times during the year.

4.2 Finance Committee This Finance Committee as per Regulation 176A of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988 controls, implements and supervises the activities related with and incidental, inter alia, to maintenance of true and correct accounts, formulation of annual budget, control of funds, investment of the funds, and disbursements from the funds for expenditure – both Revenue and Capital Nature. The Finance Committee met 3 times during the year.

4.3 Examination Committee The Examination Committee as per Regulation 176 of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988 performs all the functions of the Council relating to the examinations such as holding of examinations, admissions thereto, cancellation of an examination, appointment and selection of examiners, prescription of books for the guidance of candidates, declaration of results, payment of remuneration to examiners and /or assistant examiners, superintendents of the examination and others.

Major achievements of Examination Department during the period are as under:

(III) CONDUCT OF INFORMATION SYSTEM AUDIT – ASSESSMENT TEST [ISA – AT]:The Information Systems Audit- Assessment Test (ISA-AT) which is open to the members of the Institute was held smoothly on 12th July 2025 at 55 exam centres in India, 8th November 2025 at 59 exam centres in India and on 8th February 2026 at 59 exam centres in India respectively through Computer Based Test (CBT) Mode only.

(IV) CONDUCT OF SPECIAL EXAM UNDER MRA / MOU: - The Chartered Accountant Special Examination for Members of Institutions with whom ICAI had entered into Mutual Recognition Agreement (MRAs) / Memorandum of Understanding (MOUs) was held smoothly from June 23rd to 27th, 2025 at Noida.

(V) CONDUCT OF SELF-PACED ONLINE MODULE [SPOM] TESTS: - During the year 2024, ICAI started the Self-Paced Module Tests where students can do online learning through the portal https://lms.icai.org. The candidates who have completed prescribed learning hours for respective Self-Paced Online Modules can book the exam seats at https://spmt.icai.org . Self-Paced Module Test are being conducted on daily basis, except government holidays, across the country and abroad. As on 31st March, 2026, 6,78,507 candidates have already appeared for the exam.

(VI) ASSISTANCE IN CONDUCTING EXAMINATION OF CERTIFICATE COURSES FOR ACCOUNTANTS OF PANCHAYATS AND MUNICIPAL BODIES:ICAI Examination Department has provided all possible assistance to the Board for Local Bodies Accountants Certification (BLoAC) established under ICAI Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF)) to conduct their Examinations of Certificate Courses for Accountants of Panchayats and Municipal Bodies. The Screening Exam was held on 3rd September, 2025 (Except Jammu) & 15th October, 2025 (at Jammu) and Main Exam was held on 6th August 2025.

(VII) DIPLOMA ON MANAGEMENT AND BUSINESS FINANCE - COMPUTER BASED TEST:Diploma on Management and Business Finance - Computer Based Test was held from 9th to 11th February 2026.

(XII) DIGITAL EVALUATION: - Continuing on the path of 100% Digitalization thirty four lakh answer books were digitally evaluated in the year 2025-2026, covering examinations of May 2025, Sept 2025 and Jan 2026 exams. Another major achievement was joint declaration of results of Final, Intermediate and Foundation Exams for the first time in May 2025 exams.

(XIII) DIGITAL WORKSHOP/WEBCAST FOR EXAMINERS & EXAMINATION FUNCTIONARIES:

Continuing on the path of conducting examiners workshop in Digital Mode, Approx 13,553 examiners attended Digital Workshop in the year 2025-2026. Digital Workshop had resulted in huge cost savings as well as ease and convenience for examiners who otherwise had to travel long distance to attend the physical workshop. Programme(s) for enhancing the quality & consistency of evaluation of answer books through webcast for examiners were successfully conducted for May 2025, September 2025 and January 2026 Examinations. This initiative is expected to go a long way in improving the quality of evaluation. Webcast on guidelines for observers, examination centres, Bank of Baroda, Examination Co-ordinators were successfully done for May, September 2025 and January 2026 Examinations for better coordination in the examination conducting machinery.

(XIV) ELIGIBILITY TEST FOR EXISTING AND NEW EXAMINERS: - Mandatory Eligibility test were conducted for new applicant examiners and only those new applicant examiners were allotted examinership assignment who passed the mandatory eligibility test. Similarly, eligibility tests were conducted for existing examiners whose 3 years had lapsed since they passed the eligibility test. Accordingly, 20 Part A test and 19 Part B tests were conducted in the year 2025-26, whereby 1325 new and existing examiners passed the tests and were empaneled as examiners.

(XV) RESULTS ON DIGILOCKER:The result of Foundation, Intermediate and Final Examination can be accessed on DIGILOCKER with effect from May 2025. Result of Information Systems Audit- Assessment Test (ISA-AT) and Diploma on Management and Business Finance (DMBF) can be accessed on DIGILOCKER With effect from Jan

2025.

5.

DISCIPLINARY DIRECTORATE The Disciplinary Directorate, which serves as the regulatory arm of ICAI, has been established to examine cases of alleged professional or other misconduct by its members. Such cases may be initiated either through a formal complaint filed in Form I or through information received under the provisions of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 and/or the Rules framed thereunder.

Under this disciplinary framework, the Directorate is entrusted with the responsibility of investigating any alleged lapses or irregularities committed by members across the country. This ensures that public confidence in the profession is maintained, while also setting clear standards of acceptable professional conduct. Although the majority of members contribute responsibly and diligently to society through their expertise, the disciplinary mechanism exists to take corrective action against the few who deviate from the expected standards.

Following the amendments to the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 in 2006, the disciplinary mechanism underwent significant reforms aimed at improving the efficiency and speed of disposal of disciplinary cases. Currently, the system operates through two quasi-judicial bodies established under the Chartered Accountants (Amendment) Act, 2006, namely the Board of Discipline under Section 21A and the Disciplinary Committee under Section 21B. The structure and processes of this mechanism are designed to ensure transparency, strengthen public trust and provide fair and impartial justice to members facing allegations of misconduct. To further enhance accountability among members and firms, and to ensure timely delivery of justice, the Act was amended again in 2022; however, the effective date for the implementation of the revised disciplinary provisions is yet to be notified.

During the Council Year 2025-26, four Benches of the Disciplinary Committee, i.e. Bench I, Bench II, Bench III (including MNAF cases) and Bench IV, and one Bench of the Board of Discipline were constituted for the expeditious disposal of cases under enquiry, apart from the consideration of the Prima Facie Opinions formed by the Director (Discipline). In addition, the Disciplinary Committee under Section 21D, headed by the President, ICAI, was also constituted to look into any residual old cases that are/may be referred back.

The disciplinary mechanism of ICAI has evolved into a comprehensive and robust regulatory framework capable of addressing professional misconduct in an increasingly complex financial ecosystem. The transition from a Council-centric model to a multi-tiered, independent and structured system reflects a deliberate and progressive reform process. The evolving disciplinary architecture stands as a critical pillar in preserving the integrity, credibility, and accountability of the profession, ensuring that it remains aligned with both domestic expectations and global standards.

Salient Initiatives/Achievements • Successful conduct of e-hearings with the effective participation of the members of the Board of Discipline/Disciplinary Committee. Besides being time-saving and cost-effective, e-hearings have ensured that parties to a case can appear before the Board of Discipline/Disciplinary Committee.

The effectiveness of e-hearings is clearly reflected in the volume of cases disposed of in the meetings of the Board of Discipline and the Disciplinary Committee, as stated in the subsequent paragraphs. • To enhance operational efficiency and keep abreast of recent developments in various laws and regulations, regular training sessions covering critical topics such as Corporate Laws, reporting under CARO and Ind AS are being conducted for the officials and staff of the Disciplinary Directorate. • As on date, all residual cases under the old disciplinary mechanism (under Section 21D) stand heard and concluded by the Disciplinary Committee. Similarly, no case is pending before the Council for consideration.

Board of Discipline under Section 21A of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 The Board of Discipline has been constituted by the Council of ICAI under Section 21A of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 to look into matters of professional and/or other misconduct by members falling under the First Schedule to the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 and/or cases wherein members are held prima facie NOT guilty of any misconduct by the Director (Discipline).

During the period from 1st April, 2025 to 30th April, 2026, the Board of Discipline held 45 meetings at various places in in-person/video-conference mode. In these meetings, the Board concluded its enquiry in 64 cases, including cases which had been referred to it in previous years. The statistical break-up of the cases decided by the Board of Discipline is given below:

Sl.

No.

Particulars Figure a) Number of meetings of the Board of Discipline held during the period b) Number of Complaint/Information cases considered by the Board of Discipline (under Section 21A) wherein the Prima Facie Opinion of the Director (Discipline) was formed (including cases considered under Rule 6/Rule 12) c) Number of cases (Complaint/Information) in which enquiry was completed by the Board of Discipline (including cases referred to the Board of Discipline during earlier years) d) Number of cases (Complaint/Information) in which punishment was awarded by the Board of Discipline (including cases referred to the Board of Discipline during earlier years) e) Number of miscellaneous matters considered (under Rule 5(6), jurisdiction issues, etc.) Disciplinary Committee under Section 21B of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 The Disciplinary Committee has been constituted by the Council of ICAI under Section 21B of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 to look into matters of professional and/or Other misconduct by members which fall within the purview of the Second Schedule, or both the First and Second Schedules, to the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949.

During the period from 1st April 2025 to 30th April 2026, the Disciplinary Committee (all Benches) held 116 meetings in in-person/video-conference mode. During the course of the aforesaid meetings, the Committee concluded its enquiry in 213 cases. The statistical break-up of the cases decided by the Disciplinary Committee is given below:

6. TECHNICAL COMMITTEES/BOARDS

6.1 ACCOUNTING STANDARDS BOARD (ASB) The Accounting Standards Board (ASB), established by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India

(ICAI) in 1977, is entrusted with formulating Accounting Standards to ensure a robust, reliable and high-quality accounting and financial reporting system in India. Its primary objectives are to harmonise accounting policies and practices across the country and to align Indian standards with the International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS) issued by the International Accounting Standards Board (IASB).

The year under report also marked the commencement of the Golden Jubilee Year of the Board.

The following are the major activities undertaken by the ASB during the period under report:

(I) Financial Reporting Standards Amendments to Ind AS and AS notified by MCA: • Lack of Exchangeability – Amendments to Ind AS 21 • Classification of Liabilities as Current or Non-current and Non-current Liabilities with Covenants – Amendments to Ind AS 1 (one amendment is applicable w.e.f. 1st April, 2026) • International Tax Reform (Pillar Two Model Rules) – Amendments to Ind AS 12 • Supplier Finance Arrangements – Amendments to Ind AS 7 and Ind AS 107 • Editorial corrections • Amendments to AS 22, relating to the Pillar Two Model.

Amendments to Ind AS and AS on the following subjects- recommended to NFRA during the period: • Annual Improvements to Ind AS (2024). • Amendments to Ind AS 109 and Ind AS 107 – Classification and Measurement of Financial Instruments. • Ind AS 118, Presentation and Disclosure in Financial Statements, along with consequential amendments. • Amendments to Ind AS 109 and Ind AS 107 – Contracts Referencing Nature-dependent Electricity. • Accounting Standard for LLPs.

Ongoing important projects related to Ind AS: • Formulation of Ind AS 119, Subsidiaries without Public Accountability. • Amendments to Ind AS 21 – Translation to a Hyperinflationary Presentation Currency • Editorial changes in Ind AS • Incorporating the requirements of the Income Computation and Disclosure Standards (ICDS) into Indian Accounting Standards (Ind AS) • Formulating an Accounting Standard on Cash Basis of Accounting • Changes required in Schedule III to the Companies Act, 2013 and updates to SEBI's circulars or guidance in light of the proposed Ind AS 118 • Implementation support on Ind AS 117, Insurance Contracts Exposure Drafts (ED)/Tentative Agenda Decision (TAD) issued by IASB/IFRIC for comments: • Exposure Draft on Risk Mitigation Accounting – proposed amendments to IFRS 9 and IFRS 7 • TAD on Reassessment of Control (IFRS 10)

(II) International Initiatives – Forging Long-lasting Partnership In order to play an active role in standard setting at the global level, comments were submitted on 9 Consultative Documents, consisting of Post-implementation Review (PIR) of IFRS 16 and Tentative Agenda Decisions (TADs).

ICAI representatives also actively participated in meetings of various international standard-setting forums, such as the Emerging Economies Group (EEG), the International Forum of Accounting Standard Setters (IFASS), the Accounting Standards Advisory Forum (ASAF) and the Asian-Oceanian StandardSetters Group (AOSSG) and presented relevant agenda items raising Indian concerns. The details of participation during the period are as under:

Forum Date Location/Mode IFASS March 12-14, 2025 Naples, Italy AOSSG-CAC March 19, 2025 Virtual IFASS September 30 – October 1, 2025 London, UK ASAF (PIR of IFRS 16) March 31, 2026 (PIR of IFRS 16) Virtual IFASS April 28–30, 2026 Melbourne, Australia EEG May 7–8, 2025 Virtual

(III) Building Robust Relationships with Government and Regulatory Bodies In addition to its regular engagement with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA), the ASB actively supported and collaborated with various other regulatory bodies, including the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI), the Ministry of Power, the Employees' Provident Fund Organisation (EPFO), the Reserve Bank of India (RBI), the Department of Telecommunications and the Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India (IRDAI).

Specialised Training Programmes for Regulators and Public Sector Entities: • Office of the Comptroller & Auditor General (C&AG) of India: Under ICAI's Memorandum of Understanding with the C&AG of India, the ASB actively contributed to capacity-building initiatives for C&AG officers in the area of financial reporting standards. To date, 8 residential training programmes (three for entry-level officers and four advanced programmes for senior officers), each spanning 12–16 days, have been conducted, supported by comprehensive background material developed by the ASB. Further, e-learning modules on Ind AS and Accounting Standards, developed in the form of concise video lectures supplemented with MCQbased self-assessment tests, have been shared with the Office of the C&AG. • SEBI – Corporate Fraud Investigation Division (CFID): Two batches of a specialised programme titled "Accounting Intelligence for Corporate Finance Investigations" were conducted at Hyderabad. • Himachal Pradesh State Electricity Board (HPSEB) Ltd.: Two intensive six-day training programmes were organised at Shimla. • National Securities Depository Limited (NSDL): A three-day customised training programme on select Indian Accounting Standards was conducted at NSDL's Mumbai office.

(IV) Knowledge Dissemination and Capacity Building Initiatives • More than 100 CPE events, including seminars, webinars, certificate courses on Ind AS and refresher courses, were organised, either in collaboration with various Branches and Regional Councils or otherwise, to raise awareness about recent amendments in AS and Ind AS.

(V) Other Initiatives • E-learning Modules: 72 e-learning modules, comprising 44 modules on Ind AS and 28 modules on AS, have been developed in the form of concise video lectures supplemented with MCQ-based self-assessment tests. • Weekly Accounting Quiz: An online weekly Accounting Quiz has been introduced and is conducted every week as an educational tool to develop stakeholders' understanding of Accounting Standards; 55 quizzes have been launched so far. • Podcasts: 22 podcasts on Ind AS and AS have been developed and hosted for finance professionals to educate them on the basic outlines of the standards.

Distinct Events Organised: • Independent Directors' Meets were organised on the theme "Guardians of Transparency:

Independent Directors in Financial Oversight" at Pune, New Delhi, Mumbai and Ahmedabad. • Accounting Standards Day was celebrated at the commencement of the Golden Jubilee Year of the Accounting Standards Board at multiple locations, including Delhi NCR (Gurugram), Mumbai and Pune (WIRC), Kolkata (EIRC) and Chennai (SIRC). The celebration highlighted the critical role that robust accounting standards play in fostering transparency, consistency and credibility in financial reporting. The occasion was graced by distinguished speakers and regulators, including Deputy C&AG; Full-time Member, NFRA; Member (F&I), IRDAI; President, Institute of Actuaries of India (IAI); and Secretary and Council Member, IAI. The event witnessed the participation of around 1,500 members across various locations.

(VI) Publications Released • Compendium of Indian Accounting Standards (Indian Accounting Standards as on April 1, 2024) – Volumes I and II. • Compendium of Indian Accounting Standards (Indian Accounting Standards as on April 1, 2025) – Volumes I and II. • Educational Material on Ind AS 36, Impairment of Assets. • Educational Material on Ind AS 24, Related Party Disclosures. • Accounting Standards: Quick Referencer (as on April 1, 2025). • Compendium of Accounting Standards (Accounting Standards as on April 1, 2025). • FAQs on Guidance Note on Financial Statements of Non-Corporate Entities. • FAQs on accounting implications arising from the implementation of the new Labour Codes in India.

6.2 AUDITING AND ASSURANCE STANDARDS BOARD (AASB) Audit plays a critical role in protecting public interest by enhancing accountability and fostering trust in financial reporting, thereby supporting economic growth. Increasing business complexity and expanding global operations have enhanced stakeholder expectations of the auditing profession. The ICAI's Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (AASB) develops high-quality Standards on Auditing, Review, Assurance, Quality Control/Quality Management, and Related Services, which set best practices and serve as benchmarks for auditor performance.

The Board also issues Guidance Notes on general and industry-specific auditing issues to provide practical support to auditors. These are released under the ICAI Council's authority, except for auditing standards relating to companies and Limited Liability Partnerships (LLPs), where the Central Government prescribes standards based on ICAI's recommendations, in consultation with the National Financial Reporting Authority (NFRA). ICAI's auditing standards are harmonised with those of the International Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (IAASB) to maintain global consistency. To aid effective implementation, the Board publishes Technical Guides, Practice Manuals, Studies and Implementation Guides, offering members practical tools to navigate complex audit scenarios confidently.

The following is a comprehensive overview of the important achievements of the Board:

(I) Representations/Suggestions to Ministries and Regulators ICAI's Recommendations on Standards on Auditing for Audit of LLPs In August 2024, the Board submitted ICAI's recommendations on the Standards on Auditing for audit of LLPs ("SAs for LLPs") to NFRA for notification under Section 34A of the LLP Act, 2008, recommending that the SAs recommended by ICAI to NFRA for audit of companies should apply mutatis mutandis to the audit of LLPs. Subsequently, the MCA directed ICAI to develop a separate set of SAs for LLPs. In response, the Board developed a separate set of SAs for LLPs, which was considered and approved by the Council of ICAI. ICAI's final recommendations on SAs for LLPs were submitted to NFRA vide letter dated 24th June, 2025.

Development of Digital Balance Confirmation Portal (DBCP) ICAI has entered into a tri-party MoU with the Indian Banks' Association (IBA) and PSB Alliance Private Limited to develop the Digital Balance Confirmation Portal (DBCP), a platform enabling auditors to obtain digitally signed balance confirmations directly from banks. The portal underwent multiple demonstrations in 2024, followed by testing sessions in January 2025. Phase I (Beta) was launched with Canara Bank on 2nd February 2025 during the World Forum of Accountants, and further testing took place in June 2025 with ICAI, PSB Alliance and NeSL. The portal is currently live with 9 banks, viz.

Canara Bank, UCO Bank, Punjab National Bank, Bank of Maharashtra, Indian Overseas Bank, Punjab & Sind Bank, Bank of Baroda, Bank of India and Union Bank of India. A user guidance manual has been developed, and the platform will gradually be extended to more banks, co-operative banks and financial institutions. Overall, the DBCP represents a major step towards digital transformation in audit confirmations, improving transparency, efficiency and reliability.

Capacity Building Programme for Officers of the C&AG ICAI, in collaboration with the Office of the Comptroller & Auditor General (C&AG) of India, organised a Capacity Building Programme for officers of the C&AG engaged in the audit of PSUs at ICAI's Centre of Excellence, Hyderabad. Phase I was conducted from 5th May, 2025 to 4th June 2025, Phase II from 27th October, 2025 to 18th November, 2025 and Phase III from 9th March, 2026 to 7th April, 2026, all at the same venue. The programme covered a wide range of topics, including Indian Accounting Standards (Ind AS), Standards on Auditing (SAs), sector-specific audit areas and information technology-related aspects. The AASB supported the auditing-related sessions by developing background material and arranging faculty with the requisite subject-matter expertise.

Checklist for Safeguards and Risk Management in respect of Trusts managing PostRetirement Funds of Companies under C&AG Audit At the request of the Office of the C&AG, the Board developed a Checklist for Safeguards and Risk Management in respect of Trusts managing post-retirement funds of companies under C&AG audit. The checklist was submitted to the Office of the C&AG vide email dated 3rd February, 2026.

Audit Report Format for PF Trusts The Employees' Provident Fund Organisation (EPFO) requested ICAI to review and finalise the financial statement formats of PF Trusts of EPF Exempted Establishments covered under the Employees' Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act, 1952, and to develop the audit report format for such PF Trusts. The Board developed the audit report format for PF Trusts and submitted it to the EPFO.

(II) Initiatives for the Members • Seminars/Webinars organised by AASB: The Board organised various seminars, conferences, workshops and webcasts on Engagement and Quality Control Standards, bank audit and other auditing aspects for the awareness and professional enhancement of members. During the period from 1st April, 2025 to 30th April, 2026, 185 programmes were organised by the Board. • Bank Audit Expert Panel: The Board constituted an online panel of experts to address members' queries relating to bank branch audit for the financial year 2025-26. The Panel addressed queries from 1st April, 2026 to 15th April, 2026. • Statutory Audit Expert Panel: The Board constituted an online panel of experts to address queries relating to auditing aspects of statutory audit for the financial year 2024-25. The Panel addressed queries from 11th July, 2025 to 30th September, 2025. • Refresher Course on Standards on Auditing: Recognising the need to strengthen members' understanding and practical application of the Standards on Auditing (SAs) in the context of evolving audit practices and a complex business environment, the AASB developed a comprehensive Refresher Course covering all 35 Standards on Auditing along with a module on Artificial Intelligence. So far, 15 batches of the Refresher Course have been conducted/scheduled.

(III) Contribution at International Platforms • The Chairperson, AASB attended the IESBA, IAASB and Joint JSS meetings held on 7th- 9th May, 2025 at New York, USA. • The Chairperson, AASB attended the IESBA-JSS Meeting and the IAASB-IESBA-JSS Meeting held on 3rd – 4th November, 2025 through virtual mode. • The Vice-Chairman, AASB attended the IESBA, IAASB and Joint JSS meetings held on 29th April– 1st May, 2026 at Madrid, Spain.

(IV) Publications Issued The Board issued the following publications for the benefit of members at large: • Guidance Note on Audit of Banks (2026 Edition). • Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs) on Management Representation Letter. • Technical Guide on Disclosure and Reporting of Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) in Offer Documents (Revised 2025). • Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs) on Guidance Note on Financial Statements of Non-Corporate Entities (issued jointly by the ASB and the AASB). • Guidance on New Labour Codes. • Technical Guide on Revised Directions issued by the C&AG under Section 143(5) of the Companies Act, 2013. • Practitioner's Guide on Drafting of Modified Opinions in Independent Auditor's Reports.

6.3 Financial Reporting Review Board (FRRB) The FRRB undertakes the review of the general-purpose financial statements of various enterprises with a view to determine compliances with the generally accepted accounting principles in preparation and presentation of financial statements, disclosure requirements prescribed by regulatory bodies, statutes/rules and regulations relevant to the enterprise and reporting obligations of the enterprise as well as the auditor. The Board comprises of members of the Central Council of the ICAI including Government of India nominee. The Board also had the privilege of the representations from the office of the Comptroller and Auditor General of India (C&AG), Central Board of Direct taxes (CBDT), National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development (NABARD), Niti Aayog and Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India (IRDAI) during this period.

Partner in Nation Building – Supporting Regulators • Undertook review of 4 cases referred by the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI). • Referred 11 cases to Regulators where significant non-compliances affecting the true and fair view of financial statements were identified. • Contributed to the C&AG-ICAI Training Programme for Audit Officers in Public Sector Undertakings (PSUs) by providing 75 case studies covering 30 Indian Accounting Standards (Ind ASs).

Review of General-Purpose Financial Statements During the period under review, the Board considered and finalised 91 cases, of which 13 cases were filed. Of these, 11 cases were referred to the Director (Discipline) of ICAI for further action on account of serious non-compliances, and the same 11 cases were also referred to Regulators (MCA, SEBI, RBI), as these categories are mutually inclusive. Advisories were issued to auditors in 67 cases.

Cases referred to Cases referred to Advisories issued Cases considered and finalised by the Board to Auditors Director (Discipline) Regulators (MCA/SEBI/RBI) Outcomes of Review of General-Purpose Financial Statements during 2025–26 (out of 91 cases, 13 were filed; figures in columns 2 and 3 are mutually inclusive) Highest Number of Observations Ever Considered During the year, the Board reviewed the highest-ever volume of observations, having considered 2,805 observations across 80 financial statements – a significant milestone that strengthens the Board's monitoring of financial reporting quality across the corporate sector.

Launch of FRRB INSIGHTS – Common Non-Compliances Search Engine FRRB launched 'FRRB INSIGHTS', a dedicated search engine providing members and stakeholders access to 438 observations drawn from FRRB's publications. The tool offers volume-wise, chapter-wise, and keyword-based search, along with search aligned to technical requirements, and presents the data through interactive pie charts and bar graphs by Ind AS, enabling users to quickly identify common non-compliances across financial statement elements.

Spreading Awareness among Members • X Handle – FRRB: The Board continued its 'Did You Know?' series on its X (formerly Twitter) account, providing regular updates on non-compliances observed by FRRB to spread awareness among members. • Articles in Journal: Articles on 'Non-Compliance with Various Reporting Obligations' were published in the ICAI Journal in the November 2025 and December 2025 editions, covering noncompliances pertaining to Equity and Liabilities, and Assets in the Balance Sheet, respectively. • Webinar Series: FRRB organised a series of webinars on 'Commonly Found Non-Compliances Observed in Financial Statements and Auditor's Report', facilitating Pan-India participation. The webinars received an overwhelming response, with approximately 3,000 participants in each session.

Awareness Programmes on Financial Reporting Practices FRRB organised 24 awareness programmes on 'Financial Reporting Practices – Shaping the Future of Audit Excellence' across various regions and cities, including Hyderabad, Mumbai, Nagpur, Kolkata, Bhubaneswar, Vadodara, Ahmedabad, Rajkot, Thrissur, Ernakulam, Kozhikode, Nashik, Rourkela, Navsari, Thiruvananthapuram, Thane, Mangalore, Jammu & Kashmir, Kanpur, Bhopal, Gwalior, Delhi, Ludhiana, and Amritsar, witnessing more than 3,700 participants in aggregate.

Participation in Awareness Programmes on Financial Reporting Practices across Regions/Cities

6.4 INTERNAL AUDIT STANDARDS BOARD (IASB) The Internal Audit Standards Board (IASB) of ICAI plays a crucial role in regulating and enhancing the quality of internal auditing practices in India. The primary objective of the Board is to formulate, issue and revise Standards on Internal Audit (SIAs) and other guidance material. These Standards provide a comprehensive framework for internal auditors to perform their duties effectively, ensuring consistency, objectivity and professionalism in their engagements. By setting benchmarks for internal audit processes, reporting and ethical conduct, the Board contributes significantly to strengthening corporate governance, risk management and internal control systems within organisations across various sectors.

The key activities of the Board during the period under report are as under:

(I) Certificate Course on Concurrent Audit of Banks The Board conducts the Certificate Course on Concurrent Audit of Banks to enable members to supplement the efforts of banks in carrying out internal checks of transactions and other verifications and compliance with the procedures laid down, improve the effectiveness of the concurrent audit system in banks, improve the quality and coverage of concurrent audit reports and understand the intricacies of concurrent audit of banks. During the period, the Board organised 28 batches of the Course in physical mode, and around 1,606 participants successfully completed the Course.

(II) Certificate Course on Internal Audit The Certificate Course on Internal Audit has been completely revamped by the Board with new topics and a strong information technology component, and launched in a contemporary format to serve the needs of all stakeholders. The Course has been designed to enhance the expertise of Chartered Accountants in Internal Audit, build Governance, Risk and Compliance as one of their core competencies and help certified members distinguish themselves from others conducting internal audits. During the period, the Board organised 1 batch of the Course in physical mode, and around 213 participants successfully completed the Course.

(III) Post Qualification Course – Diploma on Management and Business Finance (DMBF) The Board runs the Post Qualification Course – Diploma on Management and Business Finance (DMBF) to enable members to gain acumen, expertise and in-depth knowledge in the areas of Management and Business Finance. The Board conducted the 5th batch of the Course in online mode, and as of now, 84 members have successfully completed the Course.

Courses at a Glance:

Course Batches Participants Certificate Course on Concurrent Audit of Banks (physical mode) 1,606 completed Certificate Course on Internal Audit (physical mode) 213 completed Diploma on Management and Business Finance (online mode) 5th batch 84 completed

(IV) Programmes, Seminars, Conferences and Webinars on Internal Audit Awareness With a view to providing a platform for dissemination of knowledge among members, the Board organised 42 webinars on various topics relating to Internal Audit in virtual mode during the period. In addition, the Board conducted six (6) physical programmes, namely, a Seminar on Internal Audit at Ahmedabad, a Residential Refresher Course at Pushkar, a One-Day Seminar on Internal Audit at Ernakulam, a Full-Day Seminar on Internal Audit at the Centre of Excellence, ICAI Kolkata, a Full-Day Seminar on Internal Audit of Cooperatives at ICAI Bhawan, Bibwewadi, Pune, and a One-Day Workshop on Internal Audit at ICAI Bhawan, Sivakasi.

(V) Training Programme on Internal Audit A Training Programme on Internal Audit for audit officials under the Office of the Director General of Audit was held at SIRC of ICAI from 8th to 12th December 2025.

(VI) Publications Issued (revised 2026 Edition) • Manual on Concurrent Audit of Banks • Technical Guide on Internal Audit of BPO Industry • Technical Guide on Internal Audit of Hotel Industry • Technical Guide on Internal Audit of Stock Brokers • Technical Guide on Risk-Based Internal Audit • E-Newsletter – "ICAI Internal Audit Spectrum" (1st Edition)

6.5 TAXATION AUDITS QUALITY REVIEW BOARD (TAQRB) The Taxation Audits Quality Review Board (TAQRB) was constituted by the Institute in 2018 to improve reporting compliance under various taxation laws – both Direct and Indirect. The reviews carried out by the Board ensure that members exercise greater diligence while certifying the various reports prescribed under direct and indirect taxation, improving the overall quality of reporting and certification in the long run. The key activities of the Board during the period from 1st April, 2025 to 31st March, 2026 are as under:

(I) Review of Tax Audit Reports During the Council Year 2024-25, 123 reports were selected for review, of which 23 pertained to members to whom advisories had already been issued up to 31st March 2023. During the Council Year 2025-26, 1,000 reports were selected for review. Reports are reviewed in three tiers – by Technical Reviewers (TR), then by the Taxation Audits Quality Review Groups (TAQRG), and finally by the Board.

During 2025-26, the Board reviewed 203 reports – the highest number since the Board's inception. For comparison, the number of reports reviewed in the preceding Council Years were as follows:  Council Years 2024–25: 71 reports  Council Years 2023–24: 91 reports  Council Years 2022–23: 80 reports Based on the reviews, the Board: issued advisories to members for compliance; identified suggestions for changes in the e-filing utility of the Tax Audit Report, conveyed to the Direct Taxes Committee of ICAI for onward submission to CBDT; and identified commonly found irregularities for the purpose of creating awareness among members.

(II) Initiatives for Members Seminars/Webinars With a view to disseminating knowledge and enhancing professional competence in the area of Tax Audit, the Board organised 12 seminars in association with the Regional Councils and Branches of ICAI.

These comprised seminars on Tax Audit, Tax Audit of NGO/Trust and Non-Corporate Entities, GST Compliance in Tax Audit & Issues in Charitable Trust/NGO with respect to Tax Audit, Presumptive Taxation & Issues in Tax Audit (Half-Day), Tax Audit (Full-Day), Commonly Found Errors in Tax Audit Reports, Tax Audit 2025, Taxation Audits with AI Audit, and Tax Audit (Half-Day).

The Board organised two webinars on Changes and Issues in Tax Audit and Changes in Business Income & Key Provisions on Exemptions with respect to Tax Audit, covering recent developments, key provisions, and practical aspects of tax audit. The webinar on Changes and Issues in Tax Audit witnessed participation from approximately 2,000 members, making it a notable success.

(III) Training Programme for Technical Reviewers A three-day Training Programme for Technical Reviewers was organised from 1st to 3rd August, 2025 at SCOPE Complex, Lodhi Road, New Delhi. The programme covered sessions on tax audit, the role of Technical Reviewers, the TAQRB web portal and the checklist for review of Tax Audit Reports. All queries raised by the Technical Reviewers were addressed, and the programme was a grand success.

(IV) TAQRB Web Portal The Board has developed a dedicated web portal (taqrb.icai.org) that serves as a centralised repository for TAQRB reports, minutes and case follow-ups, ensuring seamless documentation and accessibility.

The portal automates key workflows across all three review tiers (TR, TAQRG and TAQRB), including case allocation, report submissions and real-time tracking of progress, enhancing transparency, improving coordination and accelerating decision-making.

6.6 SUSTAINABILITY REPORTING STANDARDS BOARD (SRSB) The Sustainability Reporting Standards Board (SRSB) has been actively contributing towards advancing sustainability reporting and embedding sustainable thinking into mainstream business practices across both public and private sectors. The Board proactively identifies and develops emerging opportunities for Chartered Accountants in the sustainability domain, while simultaneously enhancing their knowledge and capabilities through workshops, seminars, certificate courses and technical publications. The Board also promotes compliance with existing sustainability-related disclosure requirements and strengthens the assurance framework, supporting the transition towards a greener, low-carbon economy.

The key initiatives of the Board during the period under report are as under:

(I) ICAI Sustainability Reporting Awards 2024-25 The ICAI Sustainability Reporting Awards 2024-25 were held on 1st February 2026 at the India Expo Centre & Mart, Greater Noida, recognising excellence in Business Responsibility and Sustainability Reporting (BRSR) across Large-Cap, Mid-Cap, Small-Cap and Voluntary Disclosure categories, segmented by Manufacturing and Service sectors. The nomination process commenced in October 2025 and received 180 submissions, with 174 eligible entries reviewed by the Shield Panel Committee. The top nominations were evaluated by a distinguished Jury during a meeting held on 21st January 2026 at BKC, Mumbai. The awardees in the various categories are as under:

Category Sub-Category Manufacturing Sector Large Cap Service Sector Manufacturing Sector Mid Cap Service Sector Manufacturing Sector Small Cap Service Sector Manufacturing Sector Voluntary Disclosures Service Sector

(II) Programmes Organised • 3rd National Conference on Responsible Business Conduct (NCRBC) 2025: Organised on 2nd–3rd July 2025 at Hotel Taj Palace, New Delhi, in association with IICA under the Ministry of Corporate Affairs and ICAI. • National Conference on Sustainability, Digital Assurance & Valuation: Organised on 30th November 2025 at Mumbai, by the Vasai Branch of WIRC in association with SRSB, covering BRSR, Social Stock Exchange, Digital Assurance, Sustainability and Valuation. • Seminars and Webinars: The Board conducted various seminars and webinars, including sessions on "Biodiversity & Business in Harmony: Role and Opportunities for Chartered Accountants", an ICAI-hosted Consultation Workshop on Workforce Nutrition Reporting,

"Demystifying Sustainability, SSE, Social Audits & Opportunities for CAs", "Navigating the Net Zero Journey", a Residential Refresher Course on "Sustainability Leadership", and various webinars on the theme "Demystifying Sustainability, Catalyzing Growth". • ICAI Sustainability Month: A nationwide initiative with participation from 5 Regional Councils and 183 Branches, anchored on the 5Ps of Sustainability and aligned with the LiFE mission and UN SDGs. Activities included tree plantations, walkathons, yoga sessions, awareness surveys and a sustainability reel competition.

(III) Certificate Course on Sustainability and BRSR The Board has been actively conducting both physical and online batches of the Certificate Course on Sustainability and BRSR. The 3rd physical batch was held at ICAI, Mumbai, from 29th–30th November and 5th–7th December 2025, with 3 physical batches completed so far. In the online mode, the Board has successfully completed the 30th batch on 11th January 2026, with over 3,000 participants enrolled to date. The online course is conducted on weekends to ensure wider accessibility and participation.

(IV) ICAI Carbon Emission Calculator On 1st July 2025, SRSB launched the ICAI Carbon Emission Calculator – a free, dynamic and userfriendly digital tool designed to assist organisations and individuals in measuring and monitoring their carbon footprint.

(V) Publication of Technical Literature The Board released the Background Material on Sustainability and Business Responsibility & Sustainability Reporting (BRSR), Revised Edition 2025.

6.7 INSOLVENCY BANKRUPTCY & VALUATION STANDARDS BOARD (IB&VSB) The Insolvency & Valuation Standards Board (I&VSB) of ICAI (earlier named as Insolvency & Valuation Standards Board (I&VSB) was constituted in 2024-25 to strengthen the insolvency and valuation profession by promoting transparent, consistent and globally aligned practices. The Board focuses on the formulation, implementation and advancement of the ICAI Valuation Standards, while contributing towards a fair and robust corporate and financial ecosystem.

Significant achievements/Initiatives

(I) Policy Advocacy and Representations • Actively contributed to insolvency law reforms by submitting detailed suggestions to the IBBI, the Parliamentary Standing Committee on Finance and the Hon'ble Select Committee of the Lok Sabha on the IBC (Amendment) Bill, 2025. • Made an oral presentation before the Hon'ble Select Committee of the Lok Sabha on 6th November 2025 regarding the IBC (Amendment) Bill, 2025, and submitted replies to the questions raised at the oral hearing. • Submitted a representation to the CBDT requesting recognition of Registered Valuers under the Companies Act framework for the purposes of the Income-tax Act, 2025, to avoid duplication and ensure uniformity in valuation regulation. • Conducted various Roundtable Discussions jointly with the IIIPI of ICAI to deliberate on the proposed amendments in various Discussion Papers brought out by the IBBI/MCA; the suggestions thus prepared were compiled and submitted to the IBBI.

(II) Standard-Setting Initiatives The Board initiated the process to develop Valuation Standards for the asset classes of Land & Building and Plant & Machinery, in addition to Securities or Financial Assets.

(III) RESOLVE-2025 – International Convention on Insolvency Resolution and Valuation The Board organised the 3rd edition of the International Convention on Insolvency Resolution and Valuation – RESOLVE-2025 – at Mumbai on 1st –2nd December, 2025, focused on the theme "Enabling Resolution, Maximizing Value", with the participation of over 500 professionals and more than 70 eminent speakers across 19 technical sessions.

(IV) Knowledge Dissemination Initiatives • Quarterly e-Newsletter "Valuation Edge": Released 5 e-newsletters for the quarters ending March 2025, June 2025, September 2025, December 2025 and March 2026. • Monthly Knowledge Corner: Released 13 series of the Knowledge Corner during the period. • Monthly Quiz on IBC and Valuation: Released 13 series of the Monthly Quiz during the period. • Announcements: Issued 51 announcements on the I&VSB page of the ICAI website to update stakeholders on the latest amendments, circulars and notifications released by the IBBI. • Online Certificate Course on IBC: Organised 7 batches of the Online Certificate Course on IBC in association with the IBBI, training more than 400 members. • Organised multiple training programmes, workshops, conferences, webinars and seminars to promote and create awareness about the ICAI Valuation Standards 2018, Valuation and Insolvency and Bankruptcy laws.

(V) Publications The Board released 22 technical publications, handbooks, judicial pronouncement series, guidance notes, webcast ATQs and policy-oriented publications covering insolvency, valuation and reforms under the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, including: • White Paper on RESOLVE-2025. • Issues and Recommendations emerging from the deliberations at RESOLVE-2025. • Handbook on Committee of Creditors and Insolvency Professionals Synergy. • Handbook on the IBC as an Instrument for Enabling Revival, Easing Exit and Ensuring Fresh Start for MSMEs. • Handbook on Opportunities for Insolvency Professionals beyond the IBC.

6.8 PEER REVIEW BOARD The Peer Review mechanism was introduced by the Council of ICAI with the setting up of the Peer Review Board in March 2002. The Board has since been progressing satisfactorily and continuously providing guidance to members to enhance the efficiency of assurance services rendered by them. The main objective of Peer Review is to ensure that in carrying out an assurance service assignment, the member (a) complies with Technical, Professional and Ethical Standards as applicable, including other regulatory requirements; and (b) has in place proper systems, including documentation thereof, to amply demonstrate the quality of the assurance services. The Peer Review is conducted of a Practice Unit by an independent evaluator known as a Reviewer.

(I) Recognition of Peer Review by Regulators • SEBI has made it mandatory, with effect from 1st April 2010, for the limited review/statutory audit reports submitted to stock exchanges by listed entities to be given only by auditors who have subjected themselves to the Peer Review process and hold a valid certificate issued by the Peer Review Board of the Institute. • The Comptroller and Auditor General of India has revised the Policy of Empanelment of CA Firms/LLPs and appointment of auditors of companies under Sections 139(5) and 139(7) of the Companies Act, 2013, and of statutory corporations/autonomous bodies. A maximum of 25 points are allotted to firms holding a valid Peer Review Certificate on the date of making an application for empanelment.

(II) Peer Review of Practice Units The Peer Review Board considered and issued 5,226 Peer Review Certificates to Practice Units during the period from 1st April, 2025 to 30th April, 2026. As on 30th April 2026, a total of 10,281 Practice Units held a valid Peer Review Certificate. The Board also considered 2,510 cases of AQMM Review till 30th April 2026, the AQMM level-wise break-up of which is as under:

AQMM Level of Practice Units Number of Practice Units Level 1 Level 2 Level 3 1,251 Level 4 Not Eligible Total 2,510

(III) Peer Review Mandate – Roll-out Implementation • Phase III: The third phase of the mandate was earlier applicable w.e.f 1st April, 2024 for Practice Units proposing to undertake the Statutory Audit of entities which have raised funds from the public, banks or financial institutions of over ₹50 crore during the review period, or of any body corporate/trust covered under public interest entities, or Practice Units rendering attestation services and having 4 or more partners – was deferred initially by nine months (effective January 2025) and further extended by six months, to be made applicable with effect from 1st July, 2025. • Phase IV: The fourth phase of the mandate was earlier applicable w.e.f 1st April, 2025 for Practice Units proposing to undertake audits of branches of Public Sector Banks, or Practice Units rendering attestation services and having 3 or more partners – was deferred initially by nine months (effective January 2026) and further extended by twelve months, to be made applicable with effect from 31st December, 2026.

(IV) Training and Empanelment of Peer Reviewers • Training Programmes: During the period from 1st April, 2025 to 30th April, 2026, the Board organised 117 Peer Review training programmes through physical mode at various Branches and Regional Councils across the country, and 4 webinars on various topics related to Peer Review. • Online Tests for Empanelment: Final Assessment Tests and Mock Tests are organised every alternate Friday (twice per Friday – morning and afternoon). Accordingly, 52 Final Tests and 52 Mock Tests were organised during the period. A total of 2,314 members cleared the Final Assessment Tests, bringing the total number of empanelled Peer Reviewers with the Board to 4,925 as on 30th April, 2026.

Region wise break-up of Number of Trained Peer Reviewers with the Peer Review Board:

Region No. of Reviewers WIRC SIRC EIRC CIRC NIRC Total

(V) Peer Review Portal The Peer Review Board is developing a dedicated web portal designed to automate, streamline and elevate the Peer Review process. This innovative centralised platform will seamlessly integrate all review activities, from the initial submission of applications to final decision-making, leveraging advanced technology to enhance efficiency, promote transparency and drive greater effectiveness of the Peer Review process.

(VI) Other Initiatives The Board remains steadfast in its continuous efforts to enhance the efficacy of the Peer Review mechanism. A comprehensive, all-encompassing approach has been followed to create an enabling ecosystem, including by developing sufficient and competent resources for review; improving the quality of Peer Reviews; and initiating steps to increase the use of technology in the Peer Review process to effectively overcome capacity constraints.

6.9 Ethical Standards Board (ESB) The Ethical Standards Board of ICAI is the designated standard-setting authority for ethical governance within the profession. The Board develops and issues ethical standards and other pronouncements for Chartered Accountants, and works towards evolving a dynamic and contemporary Code of Ethics that retains the long-cherished ideals of excellence, independence, and integrity, while protecting the dignity and interests of members. The Board takes measures to ensure the independence of auditors, assists in the adoption and implementation of ethical standards by members in practice and in service, and promotes public awareness of the integrity, objectivity, competence, and professionalism of members.

The CA Connect Portal, launched by the Ethical Standards Board in 2021 and redesigned in 2024, continues to provide an effective listing platform that serves as an essential bridge between clients/service receivers and Chartered Accountants.

Significant Achievements: • Global Ethics Day was observed on 15th October 2025 under the theme '#EthicsRe-envisioned', with a webinar organised on 'Contemporary Developments in Professional Ethics'. An Ethics Fortnight was also celebrated from 8th to 23rd October 2025, featuring knowledge-sharing programmes organised by branches under the aegis of the Board, including the publication of articles in newspapers and periodicals and enrolment of members on the CA Connect Portal. • The special edition of the CA Journal for October 2025 was published on the theme 'Internal Audit and Ethics'. • A Quiz on Ethical Issues was conducted for members from 1st to 15th October 2025, encouraging engagement and awareness on ethical matters. • The revised 13th edition of the Code of Ethics is being released. • The Board continued to issue the monthly awareness column 'Know Your Ethics' in the ICAI Journal and maintained an active presence on X (Twitter), sharing creatives on important topics to promote optimum adoption of the Code of Ethics. • The Board clarified various ethical issues raised by members and stakeholders relating to auditor independence, permissible professional services, conflict of interest, engagement in other business/occupation, holding of other designations/positions, and advertisement and solicitation.

A clarification was issued regarding the printing of photographs of members on visiting cards, in response to a query in the 'FAQs on Ethical Issues, 2025 edition' publication. In addition, more than 300 queries from members on professional ethical issues were addressed and resolved by the Secretariat. • The Board provided inputs to IFAC's Member Engagement: Adoption & Implementation Questionnaire, 2025, on 7th May 2025, and contributed inputs for the IESBA-IAASB Joint Standard-Setters (JSS) Meeting held on 7th–9th May 2025 on jurisdictional developments of international relevance.

Seminars, Programmes and Webinars on the Code of Ethics The Ethical Standards Board organised approximately 31 seminars, programmes, and webinars during the year to create awareness on the provisions of the Code of Ethics and ethical issues among members.

These were conducted across locations including Bilaspur, Hyderabad, Chennai, Tirupur, Kerala, Kottayam, Faridabad, Cuttack, Jaipur, Amritsar, Agra, Sonepat, Prayagraj, Rajasthan, Palakkad, Bihar, Jodhpur, Bhilai, Satara, and Kolhapur, covering topics ranging from the Code of Ethics, professional misconduct, disciplinary mechanisms, NOCLAR, audit documentation, and advertisement and solicitation, to professional skepticism in tax audit. Ten dedicated webinars were also held on themes such as significant ethical expectations, disciplinary procedures, advertisement and solicitation, and the exposure draft of the Code of Ethics.

International Engagement The Board participated in meetings of the Committee on Professional Ethics and Independence of the South Asian Federation of Accountants (SAFA) held on 15th September, 2025, 16th January, 2026, and 29th January 2026, and in the IESBA (International Ethics Standards Board for Accountants) – JSS Meeting held on 3rd –4th November 2025 at New York.

6.10 DIGITAL ACCOUNTING AND ASSURANCE BOARD (DAAB) The Digital Accounting and Assurance Board (DAAB) of ICAI continued to strengthen ICAI's leadership in the areas of Information Systems Audit, Forensic Accounting, Cybersecurity, Data Protection, Digital Forensics and Technology-driven Assurance during the year 2025-26. The Board focused on professional capacity building, the development of standards, publications, practical training infrastructure and stakeholder engagement in emerging technology domains.

(I) Standard-Setting in Emerging Technology Domains A significant milestone during the year was the issuance of the Information Systems Audit Standards

(ISAS), making ICAI the first professional accounting body globally to release dedicated standards in the domain of Information Systems Audit. During the year, the Board also updated the Forensic Accounting and Investigation Standards (FAIS), along with the Implementation Guide, to align them with global best practices, emerging technologies and international adoption requirements. The Board further undertook global outreach initiatives for FAIS, which are increasingly being recognised by regulators, enforcement agencies and institutions in India and abroad.
