(II) Professional Education and Skilling The Board undertook major initiatives in professional education and skilling: • DISA 4.0: The Board initiated the upgradation of the DISA Course to DISA 4.0, with an enhanced focus on practical learning, cybersecurity, digital governance and hands-on audit tools. • FAFD to Diploma in Forensic Investigation (DFI): The Certificate Course on Forensic Accounting and Fraud Detection (FAFD) continued successfully, while work progressed towards its conversion into the Diploma in Forensic Investigation (DFI). • Data Protection Compliance & Audit Certification (DPCAC): During the year, the Board launched the DPCAC programme, aligned with the Digital Personal Data Protection framework. • Course on Cybersecurity & Audit Skilling: The specialised course was approved by the Council and shall be launched shortly. • Executive Development Programmes: Programmes on Digital Forensics, Data Analytics and Power BI were also conducted.

(III) Practical Learning Infrastructure The Board strengthened practical learning infrastructure through the initiation of a phased upgradation of Data Analytics/Forensic Labs at ICAI Centres of Excellence, beginning with Hyderabad as a pilot project.

(IV) Conferences, Outreach and Capacity Building The DAAB organised several national-level conferences, seminars, outreach programmes and webinars during the year. Major events included the Digital Transformation Finance Summit (DxFS) 2025 at Hyderabad and Future Proof Forensics 2025 at Mumbai, which witnessed participation from regulators, banks, enforcement agencies, industry leaders and technology experts. The Board also conducted specialised capacity-building programmes for organisations such as the C&AG, NIA, SEBI, ICLS Academy and CID Telangana.

(V) Technical Publications The Board released and updated various technical publications and guidance materials in emerging areas, including Cloud Computing, Blockchain, Internet of Things (IoT) and the ABCD of Technology.

These initiatives have contributed significantly towards strengthening digital trust, technology assurance and professional excellence within the accounting and financial ecosystem.

6.11 DIRECT TAXES COMMITTEE (DTC) The Direct Taxes Committee undertook various initiatives during the period in the areas of policy advocacy, representations to the Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT), publications and capacity building of members in the field of direct taxation. The major activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Inputs/Memoranda Submitted and Interactions with the CBDT/Government Departments • ICAI's inputs on the Draft Income-tax Rules, 2026: ICAI's suggestions on the Draft Income-tax Rules, 2026 were submitted on 27th February, 2026. • Preparation and submission of Pre- and Post-Budget Memoranda: The Pre-Budget Memorandum 2026 was presented on 27th October, 2025; additional pre-Budget suggestions were submitted on 14th November, 2025; and the Post-Budget Memorandum was submitted on 25th February, 2026.

(II) Representations to the CBDT to Address Concerns relating to Tax Matters The Committee made representations on the following matters during the year 2025-26: • Excess tax deducted and deposited by the deductor also to be reflected in Form 26AS of the deductee to give due credit (submitted on 28th April, 2025). • Issues faced by taxpayers while filing online rectification applications and concerns on account of delay in giving effect to rectification orders issued by Jurisdictional Assessing Officers (submitted on 22nd May, 2025). • Request for addressing concerns relating to grant of credit for excess tax deducted under Section 194T (submitted on 10th June, 2025). • Request to address concerns arising in the Income-tax Offline Utility for Form 3CA-3CD and Form 3CB-3CD for Assessment Year 2025-26, and concerns in the notified Form 3CD relating to the requirement contained in clause 22(ii) (submitted on 23rd July, 2025). • Requests to address certain concerns in relation to the return forms for Assessment Year 202526 (submitted on 13th August, 2025 and 20th August, 2025). • Request to clarify/address concerns arising in Form No. 3CD for Assessment Year 2025-26 (submitted on 20th August, 2025). • Request for timely resolution of technical glitches in the Income-tax Portal (submitted on 15th September, 2025). • Request for addressing the concerns arising on account of levy of interest under Section 234C for failure/delay in making advance tax payments due to portal glitches (submitted on 17th September, 2025). • Request for parity to taxpayers in respect of return filing and other due dates, specified date and auto-renewal/deemed renewal in the case of small trusts and institutions whose original period of validity of registration is due to expire on 31st March, 2026 or thereafter, and parity for other trusts whose last date for making an application for renewal fell on 30th September, 2025 (submitted on 17th September, 2025). • Request for addressing the concerns arising on account of the updated utilities and technical glitches in the Income-tax Portal (submitted on 22nd September, 2025). • Enabling reporting under Clause 8A of Form No. 3CD by trusts carrying on business incidental to their objects (submitted on 17th October, 2025). • Request seeking restoration of carry-forward of excess application of income (deficit) in the case of charitable trusts, especially where expenditure is incurred in anticipation of government grants (submitted on 30th December, 2025). • Request to address the concern of issue of notices to companies for furnishing Form No. 29B while processing returns under Section 143(1)(a) (submitted on 31st December, 2025). • Request to amend Rule 15A and Form No. 8 of the Income-tax Rules, 1962 to align with the provisions of Section 158A of the Income-tax Act, 1961 and Rule 194 of the Income-tax Rules, 2026 (submitted on 27th March, 2026). • Request to provide clarification on the applicability of TDS under Section 393(3) [Table Sl. No. 5] of the Income-tax Act, 2025 (corresponding to Section 194N of the Income-tax Act, 1961) (submitted on 30th March, 2026). • Request for clarification as to whether the extended time for filing a revised return under Section 139(5) is applicable from A.Y. 2025-26 or A.Y. 2026-27 (submitted on 15th April, 2026). • Request to resolve the system validation issue in Form No. 26QB under Section 194-IA of the Income-tax Act, 1961, where the date of deduction of tax falls in March 2026 at the time of payment of advance (under the Income-tax Act, 1961 regime) and the date of agreement falls in April 2026 (under the Income-tax Act, 2025 regime) (submitted on 30th April, 2026).

(III) Conferences/Seminars/Workshops/Webinars/Refresher Courses • 103 Seminars and Conferences on direct taxes were organised by the Direct Taxes Committee. • 40 Webinars on direct taxes were organised by the Direct Taxes Committee. • 47 Public Outreach Programmes on direct taxes were organised by the Direct Taxes Committee.

(IV) Other Initiatives Development of a joint mobile application with the Committee on International Taxation to help members active in direct taxation and international taxation matters.

(V) Publications Released by DTC • Guidance Note on Tax Audit under Section 44AB of the Income-tax Act, 1961 (Revised 2025). • Checklist for Preparation of ITR Forms (ITR-1 & ITR-4). • Income-tax Act, 2025 (including Tabular Mapping of Sections vis-à-vis the Income-tax Act, 1961). • Income-tax Act, 2025 (as amended by the Finance Act, 2026), including Tabular Mapping of Sections vis-à-vis the Income-tax Act, 1961. • Income-tax Rules, 2026 – including Tabular Mapping of Rules and Forms vis-à-vis the Incometax Rules, 1962 and Forms.

6.12 INTERNATIONAL TAXATION COMMITTEE The International Taxation Committee (earlier named as Committee on International Committee) undertook various initiatives in the areas of professional education, capacity building, policy representation, stakeholder engagement and knowledge dissemination in the fields of International Taxation and Transfer Pricing. The major activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Representations, Suggestions and Policy Advocacy During the period, the Committee submitted/contributed: • Suggestions for the simplification of Income-tax Rules and Forms aligned with the Income Tax Bill, 2025, pertaining to International Taxation. • Suggestions on International Taxation for the Post-Budget Memoranda 2025-26 and 2026-27. • Representation on issues under the Black Money (Undisclosed Foreign Income and Assets) and Imposition of Tax Act, 2015. • Preparation of FAQs and technical inputs relating to withholding tax provisions and the new Income-tax framework. • Inputs on International Taxation for the preparation of toolkits for MSMEs, Start-ups and other stakeholders. • Representation to the CBDT on practical difficulties in filing Form 41 under the Income-tax Rules, 2026 for claiming DTAA benefits.

(II) Professional Courses and Capacity Building Programmes • Diploma in International Taxation (DIIT): During the period, the Committee conducted/announced five physical batches – the 34th (Delhi), 35th (Chandigarh), 36th (Mumbai), 37th (Hyderabad) and 38th (Vadodara) – and two online batches (13th and 14th). The programmes witnessed participation from members across India and focused on practical and contemporary issues in International Taxation and Transfer Pricing. • Certificate Course on UAE Corporate Tax: During the period, the Committee conducted/announced three physical batches – the 3rd (Pune), 4th (Hyderabad) and 5th (Thrissur) – and three online batches (6th, 7th and 8th). • Certificate Course on Income Tax Law of the USA: The Committee launched the Certificate Course on Income Tax Law of the USA during the period. The first online batch commenced with the participation of 192 members and the second batch with 204 members.

(III) Webinars and Knowledge Sessions The Committee organised 15 webinars during the period on emerging and contemporary issues in International Taxation. Several webinars witnessed participation ranging from 600 to over 2,000 members and were also addressed by senior officials from the Income Tax Department and the CBDT.

(IV) Seminars, Conferences and Workshops The Committee organised/co-organised the following programmes during the period:

With a view to enhancing professional knowledge in the field of International Taxation, the Board organised 15 programmes comprising a Residential Refresher Course–Seminar on Overview of International Tax & Transfer Pricing, a One-Day Seminar on International Taxation, Business Setup & Compliance in the UAE: A New Frontier for Indian CAs, a Full-Day Seminar on International Taxation, Seminars on International Taxation, a seminar on Practical Insights on Cross-Border Transactions & Tax Controversies, a CPE Seminar on International Taxation & Professional Opportunities, International Taxation and Emerging Professional Opportunities, Budget 2026 with Special Regard to International Taxation, a Half-Day International Taxation Seminar, and a seminar on the Income Tax Act, 2025, conducted at Panipat, Ernakulam, Ranchi, Gurugram, Chandigarh, Delhi, Faridabad, Hyderabad, Bhubaneswar, Rishikesh, Dehradun, Amritsar, Kota, Jaipur and Thane.

In addition, one Brainstorming Workshop was held with stakeholders and experts in association with the Comptroller & Auditor General of India (C&AG) on Ease of Doing Business and Direct Taxation perspectives.

(V) Stakeholder Engagement and Institutional Coordination The Committee actively engaged with: • CBDT officials and officers of the International Taxation and Transfer Pricing wings. • C&AG officials and industry stakeholders. • ICAI overseas Chapters and subject experts for collaborative initiatives. • Senior officers of the Income Tax Department through webinars and interactive sessions for member awareness and capacity building.

(VI) Publications and Technical Resources The Committee undertook the development, revision and release of the following professional publications and technical resources: • Basics of International Taxation – A Guide. • Technical Guide on Expatriates Taxation (Revised 2025). • Background Material of the Diploma in International Taxation (Revised 2025). • Cross-Border Transactions and Investments (Revised 2026). • Basics of Permanent Establishment (India Perspective) (Revised 2026). • Background Material of the Certificate Course on Income Tax Law of the United States of America (February 2026). • Taxation of Digital Economy – A Study (Revised 2026).

6.13 GST AND INDIRECT TAXES COMMITTEE The GST & Indirect Taxes Committee of ICAI supports the Government in GST policy making, implementation and knowledge sharing through technical inputs, publications and newsletters. It enhances the skills of members and taxpayers via workshops, courses and seminars, and contributes to the Government's capacity building by conducting customised GST training for tax officers. The Committee is committed to bridging the GST knowledge gap across sectors and stakeholders.

The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Initiatives towards Partner in Nation Building Reinforcing GST policy making, implementation and research – Technical Inputs: • Suggestions on Forms GSTR-9 and GSTR-9C, submitted to the CBIC on 17th April 2025, led to the incorporation of several ICAI recommendations in the amended Forms notified vide Notification No. 13/2025 – Central Tax dated 17th September 2025, followed by further suggestions on the amended Forms. • Suggestions on practical issues faced by taxpayers in GST compliance were submitted to the Government on 25th August 2025 to facilitate smoother GST implementation and address challenges relating to registration, refunds, input tax credit and tax demands. • A compilation of scenarios requiring adjustment of auto-populated tax liability in Form GSTR-3B was submitted to the GST Policy Wing, CBIC on 2nd October 2025, and a similar compilation of scenarios requiring adjustment of auto-populated input tax credit in Form GSTR-3B was submitted on 24th October 2025, in response to its request for stakeholder inputs. • Suggestions on GST registration and refund processes were submitted to the Directorate General of GST on 5th December 2025 to aid the formulation of actionable measures and Standard Operating Procedures. • Suggestions for enhancing the Ease of Doing Business under GST were submitted to the C&AG of India on 20th December 2025, pursuant to its request for inputs in connection with the proposed audit on GST from the Ease of Doing Business perspective. • ICAI representatives shared practical insights on field-level challenges and processes relating to GST refunds during the consultative meeting convened by the GST Policy Wing, CBIC on 17th December 2025. • Post-Budget Suggestions on the indirect tax proposals contained in the Union Budget 2026-27 to the Government on 21st February 2026, after compiling inputs received from members, Branches and Regions. • Suggestions to the GSTAT and GSTN on 13th March 2026 for aligning the GST Appellate Tribunal portal terminology and statistics with the statutory provisions of the CGST Act, 2017. • A representation to the CBIC seeking clarification on jurisdictional issues relating to appeals and revision proceedings under Sections 107 and 108 of the CGST Act, 2017, and suggested measures for smoother implementation and taxpayer convenience. • A Representation seeking extension of the due date for filing Form GSTR-3B for September 2025, in view of the Diwali festival and GST rate revisions, was accepted by the CBIC, extending the due date to 25th October 2025. • A representation before the GSTAT on 26th February 2026 seeking mandatory verification of valid Certificates of Practice of Chartered Accountants appearing as Authorised Representatives.

Memoranda of Understanding with State Governments for Capacity Building:

The Institute, through the GST & Indirect Taxes Committee, entered into Memoranda of Understanding

(MoUs) with the Commissionerate of Taxes & Excise, Government of Tripura, and the Commissionerate of CT & GST, Government of Odisha, for capacity building and extending technical cooperation in research, assistance in policy making, consulting, etc.

Supporting the capacity-building initiatives of the Government: • The Committee organised 45 Capacity Building Programmes on GST during the year for officers of the Central and State Governments and other departments, benefiting more than 2,758 officials. • National GST Symposium: The Committee organised the 4th National GST Symposium at Thiruvananthapuram on 8th–9th January 2026, which was attended by 97 senior officers from 24 States representing various Central and State Government departments. • Outreach Programmes for Trade and Industry: The Committee organised GST Awareness Programmes on the new tax rates, in collaboration with the Government of Tripura, at Agartala

(twice) and Udaipur in October 2025, each attended by over 250 representatives from trade and industry. The awareness programme at Agartala was inaugurated by the Hon'ble Chief Minister of Tripura, and the programme at Udaipur on 10th October 2025 was inaugurated by the Hon'ble Finance Minister of Tripura. • Meetings with functionaries of the Central and State Governments: During the reporting period, the Committee held meetings with 26 senior officers of the Central and State Governments to apprise them of ICAI's GST initiatives and explore avenues for providing proactive support in the area of GST.

(II) GST Knowledge Sharing Initiatives • Strengthened and regularly updated its official website as a comprehensive digital knowledge hub offering free professional resources to members and stakeholders. • Organised 7 physical and 1 virtual batch of the Certificate Course on GST, attended by 421 members; 402 members successfully qualified the online assessment tests. • Conducted 1 batch of the Certificate Course on UAE-VAT in Dubai, in coordination with the ICAI Dubai Office, attended by 24 members. • Re-launched the E-learning on GST on 23rd May 2025, enabling members to access GST learning anytime and anywhere. • Organised 10 National Conferences across the country with the participation of over 4,700 members. • Conducted 58 CPE events benefiting more than 6,300 members. • Organised 26 live webcasts/webinars on contemporary GST topics, attended by over 25,000 delegates. • Published 10 editions of the ICAI Newsletter on GST during the year. • Circulated 12 GST Updates covering key CBIC notifications, circulars, orders and instructions to over 50,000 registered users and through the website.

(III) Publications  Practical Guide to GST Disputes,  Handbook on Applicability of GST on Agricultural Sector  Handbook on E-Commerce under GST.

In addition, sixteen publications were revised to incorporate the latest developments and amendments under the GST law, viz.  GST Act(s) and Rule(s) – Bare Law (revised twice)  Handbook on the Inspection, Search, Seizure and Arrest under GST  Handbook on the Invoicing under GST  Handbook on the Exempted Supplies under GST  Handbook on the Input Service Distributor under GST  Handbook on the Blocked Credit under GST  Handbook on the Registration on GST  Handbook on the Advance Ruling under GST  Handbook on the Interest, Late Fee and Penalties under GST  Composition Scheme under GST  Handbook on Refunds under GST  Returns & Payment under GST  Handbook on Foreign Trade Policy – Incentives, Schemes & Related FAQs  Technical Guide on GST Annual Return (Form GSTR-9)  Technical Guide on GST Reconciliation Statement (Form GSTR-9C)  Significant Judicial and Advance Rulings in GST: A Compilation

6.14 EXPERT ADVISORY COMMITTEE (EAC) In a dynamic economic landscape marked by increasingly complex transactions and the continuous evolution of financial reporting requirements, accounting professionals are often required to address intricate and emerging issues. Such situations demand professional judgement supported by wellreasoned and authoritative guidance to maintain the credibility and quality of financial reporting. The Expert Advisory Committee (EAC) serves as a key technical body in this regard by providing independent and well-deliberated opinions on intricate accounting matters. Over the years, the Committee has established itself as a valuable and trusted source of technical guidance, assisting members across industries, as well as government and regulatory institutions, in addressing practical accounting challenges.

(I) Expert Opinions The Committee responds to queries from members strictly in line with the Advisory Service Rules. Its scope is confined to issues relating to accounting and auditing principles; accordingly, issues involving the legal interpretation of statutory enactments and/or cases of professional misconduct are not answered by it. Further, as per the Rules, matters pending before the Board of Discipline or the Disciplinary Committee of the Institute, or before an appropriate department of the Government or the Income-tax authorities, are not answered by the Committee. The Advisory Service Rules are accessible on the ICAI website(https://www.icai.org/post/495) The opinions issued by the Expert Advisory Committee are based on the specific facts and circumstances provided by the querist, taking into account the relevant laws, statutes and applicable accounting and auditing principles. Each opinion clearly states the date of its finalisation and should be read considering the developments and/or amendments to laws or standards that may have occurred after that date.

(II) Opinions Finalised During the period from 1st April, 2025 to 30th April, 2026, the Committee finalised forty-three (43) opinions on a wide range of queries raised by members involving issues related to accounting principles.

(III) Information Technology (IT) Initiatives Opinions issued by the Committee are made available through an online Search Application on the ICAI website, enabling easy access. The EAC has upgraded its Search Application with more refined search features, enabling next-level search to deliver more precise results and provide members with a seamless and efficient research experience.

The salient features of the Application (https://eacopinion.icai.org/ ) are: • An extensive repository of around 1,700 opinions, empowering users to explore the Opinions issued by the EAC so far. • Search facility based on keyword(s), date of opinion, volume of the Compendium of Opinions, etc. • A 'Recent Opinions' tab containing the latest opinions issued by the Committee, updated regularly to provide up-to-date information to members. • An advanced and upgraded search facility that enables users to locate EAC opinions using keywords, combinations of keywords and/or the date or period of issuance, with enhanced features delivering more precise results and significantly improving ease of access and usability.

(IV) Dissemination of Knowledge • Webinars: As part of its outreach initiatives, the Committee organised a webinar on the theme

"Recent EAC Opinions under Ind AS Framework" on 16th December, 2025, with a view to disseminating knowledge and creating awareness amongst members and other stakeholders. • Publishing of Opinions in the Journal: Selected opinions of general relevance are also published in the Institute's journal, 'The Chartered Accountant'.

(V) Publications – Compendium of Opinions In order to make the technical guidance contained in the Opinions available to accounting professionals and other stakeholders, the Committee periodically compiles and publishes its opinions as the Compendium of Opinions. To date, forty-four (44) volumes have been released, serving as a valuable reference resource for professionals. The Compendium of Opinions is accessible on the ICAI website

6.15 CORPORATE LAW COMMITTEE (CLC) The Corporate Law Committee (CLC) (Earlier named as Corporate Laws and Corporate Governance Committee (CL&CGC) works towards strengthening the corporate regulatory framework through policy advocacy, stakeholder engagement and collaboration with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA). The Committee also promotes knowledge enhancement and professional excellence in the areas of corporate laws and governance.

The key highlights of the year are as under:

(I) Policy Contributions and Recommendations • Submitted comprehensive recommendations for the operationalisation of Section 129A of the Companies Act, 2013 (periodic financial reporting by specified unlisted companies), covering proportionate thresholds, half-yearly reporting aligned with SEBI practices, mandatory Board approval, practical timelines, flexibility between audit and limited review, and simplified e-forms to reduce the compliance burden. • Submitted detailed suggestions on SEBI's Consultation Paper on Appointment of Auditors and Related Party Transactions of listed entities, aimed at strengthening governance and transparency while ensuring operational feasibility, ease of compliance and balanced regulatory implementation. • Submitted a representation seeking exemption from dematerialisation for Section 8 companies, considering their non-profit nature and limited shareholder activity, and provided technical inputs to SEBI on secretarial compliance, auditor appointments and related party transactions to ensure a balance between regulatory strength and practical implementation. • Supported the Prime Minister Internship Scheme (PMIS) by facilitating the placement of trained interns to enhance youth employability and recommended treating vocational training as CSR expenditure and post-employment costs as normal business expenses, in line with Section 135 and Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 2013.

(II) Regulatory and Governance Advisory • Examined requests from CPSEs under the Ministry of Heavy Industries for relaxation in strike-off provisions involving unresolved liabilities and legal proceedings, and recommended a balanced approach ensuring administrative efficiency while safeguarding stakeholder interests and upholding governance and accountability standards. • Reviewed the representation from Tata Power Renewable Energy Limited (TPREL) seeking exemption from appointing Independent Directors in certain captive SPVs, and advised against such exemption, citing inconsistency with sound corporate governance and potential dilution of the intent of Section 149 of the Companies Act, 2013.

(III) Support to Government Initiatives • Collaborated with the MCA on the simplification of corporate laws through nationwide stakeholder consultations, and contributed to the rollout of MCA21 Version 3 through technical support, grievance redressal and facilitation, while initiating consultations for MCA21 Version 4. • Continued engagement through meetings with MCA officials, participation in user acceptance testing and joint outreach programmes, along with awareness initiatives, webinars and training sessions to support compliance readiness, especially for filings such as AOC-4 and MGT-7.

(IV) Capacity Building and Outreach Programmes • Undertook national outreach initiatives, including participation in "Vision Rajasthan 2026" at Sirohi, Rajasthan, and promotion of LLP adoption in under-penetrated regions, along with capacity building through conferences, conclaves and certificate courses on CSR Reporting. • Conducted extensive training programmes, seminars, webinars and outreach initiatives to enhance professional competency, strengthen compliance awareness and promote good governance practices across the country.

(V) Publications • Technical Guide on CSR Expenditure. • Handbook on Compliances and Exemptions for Private Companies. • FAQs on Section 8 Companies.

6.16 COMMERCIAL LAWS & ECONOMIC ADVISORY COMMITTEE The Commercial Laws & Economic Advisory Committee (Earlier named as Committee on Commercial Laws and Economic Advisory & NPO Cooperative) plays a vital role in equipping Chartered Accountants with in-depth knowledge and practical expertise across commercial laws, economic advisory and dispute resolution, while contributing to nation building through proactive engagement with regulators and policymakers.

The key initiatives undertaken by the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Initiatives towards Partner in Nation Building • Launch of Section 8 Company – ICAI International ADR Centre (IIAC): The launch of the ICAI International ADR Centre at Le Meridien, New Delhi on 30th July 2025 marked a significant milestone. The Chief Guest, Hon'ble Minister of State, Ministry of Law and Justice, appreciated ICAI's initiative to strengthen institutional dispute resolution, while Hon'ble Justice, Delhi High Court, emphasised the importance of ADR in enhancing the ease of doing business.

Esteemed panellists shared valuable insights. The unveiling of the IIAC logo and booklet further strengthened ICAI's commitment to promoting India's vision of becoming a global ADR hub. • Quick Legal Form Utility: The Committee launched a Quick Legal Form Utility offering easy access to drafts of key legal documents, including gift deeds, partnership deeds, powers of attorney, rent and lease agreements, wills and writs, which achieved over 14,000 downloads on the launch day itself. The Utility is readily accessible on the ICAI website at • Inputs for Uniform Audit Format for Multi-State Co-operative Societies: The Committee actively contributed to drafting MSCARO 2025 as part of the Sub-Committee constituted by the Central Registrar of Cooperative Societies (CRCS). In February 2026, the final draft was submitted to the Central Registrar's Main Committee, supporting standardisation in the audits of Multi-State Co-operative Societies.

(II) Knowledge Enhancement through Webinars The "Know Your Laws" series during 2025-26 reached 135 webinars and over 350 structured CPE hours, addressed to more than 10,000 participants, reflecting the Committee's sustained efforts towards professional development and knowledge dissemination.

(III) Celebration of International Days As part of its outreach initiatives, the Committee observed International Literacy Day, World Environment Day, World Intellectual Property Day, International Labour Day and RERA Day, among others, promoting awareness of global issues and encouraging meaningful member participation.

(IV) Meetings and Institutional Engagements • A meeting was held on 24th September, 2025 with Chairman, MahaRERA, to explore collaboration in the real estate sector. Interactions were also held with officials from the Ministry of Home Affairs and the Ministry of Finance, including Director, FATF Cell, Department of Revenue, Ministry of Finance, regarding the National Risk Assessment. • Engagements with academic and professional institutions, and interactions with experts such as Hon'ble Justice, Delhi High Court, Two Senior Advocates, Senior Attorney, Global Arbitrator and strengthened the dialogue on arbitration and mediation.

(V) Flagship National Cooperative Programme – "Cooperative Chintan Shivir" The Committee successfully organised its flagship programme, "Cooperative Chintan Shivir", on 12th January 2026 at New Delhi, with the participation of over 800 members. The programme served as a significant platform for thought leadership and dialogue on strengthening India's cooperative sector through transparency, professional management and institutional reforms. The event was graced by Hon'ble Union Minister of Environment, Forest and Climate Change and Labour & Employment; Hon'ble Minister of State, Ministry of Cooperation; and Former Union Minister and Chancellor, Rishihood University, whose presence and insights greatly enriched the deliberations. The programme featured insightful technical sessions focusing on governance, inclusive growth and digital transformation in the cooperative sector, and also marked the launch of a Concept Paper on strengthening the cooperative ecosystem, contributing to meaningful discussions and future-oriented policy perspectives.

(VI) Representations/Inputs  Representations by contributing research on strengthening RERA, promoting professional opportunities in the cooperative sector and  Providing technical inputs to India's National Risk Assessment 2025 in collaboration with FIUIndia.

(VII) A Year of Learning, Leadership and Engagement During the year, the Committee conducted 18+ Certificate Courses, 40+ Seminars, 3+ Leadership Development Programmes (LDPs) and 3+ Residential Refresher Courses (RRCs), engaging over 6,500 members and strengthening professional learning and capacity building.

(VIII) Publications: • Online Dispute Resolution (ODR): An International Perspective. • Professional Opportunities for Chartered Accountants under Foreign Exchange Management Act

(FEMA) Laws in India. • Professional Opportunities for Chartered Accountants under the Real Estate (Regulation and Development) Act, 2016 in India. • Professional Opportunities for Chartered Accountants in the Cooperative Sector. • Background Material for the Certificate Course on FEMA. • Study on Strengthening the Real Estate (Regulation and Development) Act (RERA) in India for the Future: Best Practices and Recommendations. • Strengthening India's Cooperative Framework: The Role of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI). • Revised Background Material for the Certificate Course on Anti-Money Laundering Laws (AML Specialist). • Revised Background Material for the Certificate Course on Arbitration.

6.17 COOPERATION AND NPO COMMITTEE The cooperative sector, comprising more than 8.5 lakh cooperatives across the country, plays a pivotal role in India's socio-economic development by promoting inclusive and sustainable growth. Recognising the significance of this sector, the Government of India established the dedicated Ministry of Cooperation in 2021 with the vision of achieving the mission of "Sahkar Se Samriddhi / ".

In alignment with this national vision, ICAI has constituted the Cooperation and NPO Committee (Earlier part of Committee on Commercial Laws and Economic Advisory & NPO Cooperative) to contribute towards the strengthening and development of the cooperative and NPO sectors in the country.

The Committee engaged with key policy-making and regulatory authorities for the holistic development of the cooperative sector. In this regard, the Committee closely coordinated with the Central Registrar of Cooperative Societies in the drafting of the Multi-State Co-operative (Auditor's Report) Order (MSCARO), 2026.

Further, the Committee coordinated with the Ministry of Cooperation, the National Council for Cooperative Training (NCCT) and Tribhuvan Sahkari University (TSU) towards implementing transformational reforms in the cooperative sector. Additionally, the Committee coordinated with the Cooperative Departments of various States and Union Territories to strengthen capacity-building initiatives for stakeholders in the cooperative ecosystem across the country.

6.18 CENTRE FOR AUDIT QUALITY COMMITTEE (CAQC) The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India established the Centre for Audit Quality Directorate

(CAQD) in 2020 to focus on enhancing the quality of audit practices in India. In 2026, the Directorate was reconstituted as the Centre for Audit Quality Committee (CAQC), strengthening its role and governance framework. The CAQC primarily focuses on the qualitative aspects of the audit function, ensuring continuous improvement in audit quality and contributing to enhancing the global standing of Indian audit firms.

In furtherance of its mandate, the Committee undertakes a broad spectrum of initiatives aimed at strengthening audit and assurance quality. These include developing a framework for audit and assurance quality and formulating the Audit Quality Maturity Model (AQMM) based on Audit Quality Indicators; conducting certificate courses and awareness programmes to enable members to perform audits in compliance with auditing principles; developing publications and guidance materials to assist members in improving their professional practices and establishing robust systems and processes; and facilitating the development and adoption of technological resources for efficient audit practices, besides undertaking functions assigned by the Council or the President from time to time. The activities undertaken during the period under report are as under:

(I) Utilities Developed and Updated by CAQC The CAQC develops and provides technological resources, tools and utilities to facilitate the smooth conduct of quality audits and assurance services, while supporting audit firms in enhancing audit quality, operational efficiency and regulatory compliance. During the year, the following utilities were • Checklist for Certificates issued by Chartered Accountants: The CAQC developed a Checklist for Certificates issued by Chartered Accountants, in alignment with the Guidance Note on Reports or Certificates for Special Purposes (Revised 2016). It is an easy-to-understand and easy-to-implement tool that assists members in ensuring compliance with the various aspects of the Guidance Note while issuing certificates. • Utility for Bank Branch Audit – 2026 Edition (updated): To help members in the smooth conduct of bank branch audits, the CAQC has developed an Excel utility based on the Guidance Note on Audit of Banks and the Technical Guide on Long Form Audit Report issued by the AASB.

The Utility has been updated, incorporating the changes in the Guidance Note on Audit of Banks (2026 Edition). • Utility for Review and Monitoring Tool on Standards on Auditing Compliance (updated):

The tool provides comprehensive checklists for ensuring compliance with the Standards on Auditing, tracks audit progress via a work status dashboard and documents team activities, including the time spent on each audit. The tool has been updated with the addition of a 'Whether Applicable' column in the Dashboard, indicating the applicability of each Standard on Auditing

(SA); if a particular SA is not applicable, the user can select 'No', upon which the corresponding SA sheet displays a 'No' status and restricts any further inputs. • Utility for Determining Materiality (updated): The utility assists in calculating materiality thresholds for statutory audits, categorises risk assessment (low/medium/high) and aids in forming audit opinions based on material misstatements. It has been enhanced by adding a column 'Reason for choosing the level of risk' in the materiality calculation sheet, enabling users to document their rationale for selecting the appropriate level of risk.

In previous years, the Committee had successfully introduced the following utilities: • Utility for Self-evaluation under AQMM v 2.0: The utility assists firms in self-evaluating their maturity level on the basis of the Audit Quality Indicators defined under AQMM v 2.0, enabling them to identify areas where competencies are strong or lacking and to develop a roadmap for achieving a higher level of audit maturity. • Utility on Engagement Letters: The utility has been designed to simplify and streamline the process of generating engagement letters for assurance clients, enabling auditors and audit firms to generate multiple engagement letters with a single click, saving time and eliminating the complexities involved in their preparation.

Status of download of the utilities as at 08.05.2026 is as under:

(II) Videos on Tools and Utilities Developed by CAQC The CAQC has developed instructional videos on the Utility for Determining Materiality, the Review & Monitoring Tool for SA Compliance, the Utility on Engagement Letters, the Utility for Bank Branch Audit and CAQC Initiatives. In addition, videos have been developed on the process of downloading the utilities and enabling macros. These videos, hosted on the CAQC page as well as on YouTube, aim to provide members with a better understanding of the purpose and functioning of these tools and utilities, while also offering step-by-step guidance on their use.

(III) Widening the Scope of Mandatory Applicability of AQMM v 2.0 The scope of mandatory applicability of AQMM v 2.0 has been widened to cover more firms, and accordingly AQMM v 2.0 has been made mandatory in a phased manner for the following categories of firms:

Date of Applicability S.

No.

Category of Firms (Peer Review conducted on or after) Mandatory applicability of AQMM – Existing criteria

1.

April 1, 2023 Firms auditing the following entities: (a) a listed entity; (b) banks other than co-operative banks (except multi-state cooperative banks); and (c) insurance companies. However, firms conducting only branch audits are not covered.

Mandatory applicability of AQMM – Widened scope April 1, 2026

2.

Firms which are subject to Peer Review and audit the Holding/Subsidiary/Associate/Joint Venture entities of the entities stated under S. No. 1 above.

3.

April 1, 2026 Firms which propose to undertake the statutory audit of unlisted public companies having paid-up capital of not less than ₹500 crore, or annual turnover of not less than ₹1,000 crore, or, in aggregate, outstanding loans, debentures and deposits of not less than ₹500 crore as on 31st March of the immediately preceding financial year.

4.

April 1, 2027 Firms which propose to undertake the statutory audit of entities which have raised funds from the public or banks or financial institutions of over ₹50 crore during the period under review, or of any body corporate, including trusts, covered under public interest entities.

(IV) Programmes/Webinars Organised by CAQC • Joint Webinars with the Peer Review Board: The CAQC, jointly with the Peer Review Board

(PRB), organised two webinars to educate members on AQMM v 2.0 on 20th June 2025 and 10th April 2026, which were attended by approximately 1,900 participants. • Quality Café Sessions – a virtual series on audit quality: The CAQC organises the Quality Café series in virtual mode, comprising two-hour interactive panel discussions by audit experts on the elements that contribute to audit quality. During the period, 6 Quality Café sessions were organised, attended by approximately 4,365 participants. • Certificate Course – "Executive Master Program – New Age Auditors": During the period, 3 virtual batches of the Certificate Course were conducted, attended by approximately 227 members. Examinations for 2 batches were conducted, wherein 156 members successfully qualified and were awarded the Certificate. • Other Programmes: The CAQC organised 36 programmes on audit quality aspects, hosted by various Branches and Regional Councils. These programmes imparted knowledge on the Audit Quality Maturity Model (AQMM), the various tools and utilities developed by the CAQC, and the enhancement of audit practices for non-corporate entities, and were attended by approximately 4,460 participants. The region-wise participation of members is as under:

Region WIRC CIRC NIRC EIRC SIRC Participants (Nos.) 1,552 1,031

(V) Other Initiatives A separate 'X' (Twitter) handle has been created for the CAQC to connect with members, enhance awareness on audit quality and share updates on initiatives, webinars, certificate courses, and new utilities and publications.

(VI) Publications Released by CAQC • Handbook on Certificates by Chartered Accountants: Comprehensive Checklist & Formats: The publication serves as a practical guide for members in issuing various certificates with due professional care. It provides detailed checklists, FAQs and illustrative formats to promote uniformity and enhance the quality of certification work undertaken by Chartered Accountants, and is aligned with ICAI's Guidance Note on Reports and Certificates for Special Purposes (Revised 2016). • Bridging the Expectation Gap – Audit vs. Forensic: The publication explains the fundamental differences between an audit and a forensic engagement – two professional activities that are often perceived as similar but serve distinctly different purposes.

7. PROFESSIONAL DEVELOPMENT COMMITTEES

7.1 AI COMMITTEE The AI Committee (constituted as the "AI in ICAI" Committee in the Council Year 2024-25) spearheads the integration of Artificial Intelligence across the profession and the Institute's operations, enhancing services for members, students and employees. The Committee focuses on building AI capability through structured certifications and continuous learning, developing AI-powered tools and platforms, creating knowledge infrastructure, fostering innovation and forging strategic collaborations with the Government, industry and global technology leaders. The key initiatives undertaken during the period under report are outlined below:

(I) Capacity Building and Learning Initiatives • AICA – AI Certificate Course for Chartered Accountants: A flagship structured certification programme comprising Level 1, a three-day physical programme covering foundational AI concepts and tools, and Level 2, a five-day blended-format programme covering advanced applications with practical use cases. Over 750 batches have been conducted and 42,000+ members trained at Level 1, while 40+ batches have been conducted and 1,500+ members trained at Level 2. • AI Refresher Series: Weekly virtual sessions on Level 1, launched in April 2025, ensure continuous learning and updates on emerging AI trends and tools; the Level 2 series commenced in April 2026. Both series are free of cost for eligible members. • Faculty Identification Programme (FIP) on AI: A structured initiative to build a strong pool of AI-trained faculty across India for scaling learning initiatives; 6 FIPs have been conducted till date, with the 7th scheduled on 9th –10th May, 2026.

(II) Technology and AI Tools Development A total of 131 GPTs have been developed by the Committee, including the following: • ICAI CA GPT (multi-phase rollout): AI-powered assistants for members covering multiple domains, including taxation, auditing and regulatory areas. • ICAI CA Student GPT: A dedicated AI assistant tailored for students to support learning and examination preparation. • ICAI AI Agent (multi-modal platform): An advanced AI-driven solution integrating text, document and analytical capabilities for professional use. • Industry Forum GPT: An AI tool built on the data of 5,000+ listed companies, enabling industry benchmarking and analysis.

(III) Knowledge Infrastructure • AI Knowledge Hub: A centralised repository of AI resources, including articles, videos, use cases and technical materials. • AI Hub for Capstone Projects (Level 2): A platform to showcase innovative AI-based projects developed by participants.

(IV) Innovation and Engagement Platforms • ICAI AI Hackathon: A season-based national innovation platform encouraging members to develop AI-driven solutions for real-world professional challenges; 4 seasons have been completed and the 5th season is currently underway. • AI Innovation Challenge: A focused initiative to recognise and promote innovative AI use cases within the profession. • Prompt Engineering Competition: A competition across multiple categories to build practical AI usage skills among members.

(V) Flagship Events and Global Platforms • ICAI AI Innovation Summit: An annual global summit bringing together international experts, policymakers and professionals (Hyderabad 2024, Pune 2025 and New Delhi 2026). • Participation in global platforms such as the IndiaAI Impact Summit and the World Forum of Accountants (WOFA), showcasing ICAI's AI initiatives on international stages.

(VI) Strategic Collaborations and MoUs • MoU with IndiaAI (MeitY, Government of India) for collaborative training, AI ecosystem development and research initiatives. • MoU with Google India for advancing AI research and capacity building. • Engagements with regulators and institutions for AI-driven initiatives, including standards, governance and implementation frameworks.

(VII) Future-Focused Initiatives • Expansion of AI certifications and global alignment of the curriculum. • Development of international collaborations under the MoUs. • Creation of AI research labs and advanced innovation platforms. • Strengthening AI adoption across practice, industry and academia.

(VIII) Thought Leadership and Publications ICAI has developed a strong body of knowledge through its AI publication series, comprising: • Ethics in AI; Privacy in AI; and Impact of AI on Indian Economy. • AI Law & the Chartered Accountant: Governance, Assurance, and Practice in India and Beyond. • AI Prompt Engineering for Chartered Accountancy. • AI and the DPDP Era: A Chartered Accountant's Guide to Responsible Data Governance. • AI Essentials for Chartered Accountants and Python Essentials for Chartered Accountants (under development).

7.2 MEMBERS IN INDUSTRY & BUSINESS COMMITTEE Members in Industry & Business Committee of ICAI (Earlier named as Committee For Members in Industry and Business) continues to drive professional excellence through a diverse portfolio of strategic initiatives. The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Campus Placement Programmes A cornerstone of the Committee's efforts is the Campus Placement Programme, which facilitated career transitions for thousands of newly qualified CAs in 2025-26. During the 61st edition (February–March 2025), 3,729 offers were made, with a top salary of ₹29.00 lakh per annum. This momentum was sustained in the 62nd edition (August–September 2025), which saw 3,785 employment offers and a highest CTC of ₹26.60 lakh per annum. Preparations for the 63rd Campus Placement Programme (February–March 2026) were geared towards qualifying candidates from the November 2025 examinations, with the registration process commencing in January 2026 to continue this career trajectory.

To ensure candidates are "corporate-ready", the Committee also conducted intensive 1–2-day Orientation Programmes focusing on interview techniques and soft skills for the 10,653 candidates registered in the latter session.

(II) Professional Networking and Continuous Learning Beyond initial placements, the Committee fosters lifelong learning and professional networking through dedicated forums and continuous education modules. Major initiatives include the CFO and CHRO Forums, which serve as high-level networking platforms for senior professionals. The "Growth Gears" talk-show series and various industry-specific webinars have been instrumental in providing members with contemporary insights into the start-up ecosystem, real estate and digital transformation.

Furthermore, the Committee maintains an active schedule of Seminars, Workshops and Residential Refresher Courses (RRCs) to help members meet their Continuing Professional Education (CPE) requirements while staying updated on global business trends.

(III) Recognition Programmes and Leadership Development The Committee's commitment to excellence is also reflected in its recognition programmes and targeted development for high achievers. These include the ICAI Awards for CA achievers and the specialised

"40 Under 40 – CA Business Leader Award". For academic standouts, the Management Development Programmes (MDPs) for rank holders provide advanced leadership training in collaboration with premier business schools. Collectively, these initiatives ensure that the CMI&B remains a vital link between the accounting profession and the evolving needs of global industry.

7.3 AGGREGATION OF CA FIRMS COMMITTEE (ACAFC) The Aggregation of CA Firms Committee (ACAFC), constituted in 2024-25, is driving a transformational journey from "Ease to Excellence in Practice" for CA firms in India. With a clear strategic mandate, the ACAFC is undertaking a comprehensive review and revision of existing guidelines to ensure they are relevant, adaptive and aligned with the evolving professional and regulatory landscape. Through its initiatives, the ACAFC empowers CA firms to expand expertise, scale operations, embrace digital solutions and navigate the dynamic professional ecosystem with agility. By streamlining processes, reducing procedural barriers and fostering a culture of resilience and excellence, the Committee cultivates an environment where firms can thrive. Leveraging stakeholder engagement, structured capacity building and continuous feedback, the ACAFC ensures that CA firms are not only equipped to meet domestic challenges but are also poised to compete and collaborate effectively in an increasingly global market.

Key Initiatives of ACAFC • FAQs and the Model Merger Deed on the ICAI (Merger & Demerger of CA Firms) Guidelines, 2024: This publication comprises a comprehensive set of Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs) designed to address commonly encountered issues and provide practical, actionable guidance to members. The FAQs serve as an important reference tool, offering clarity on interpretational and procedural aspects to enable smooth and efficient adoption of the revised Guidelines. To further support firms in operationalising mergers, a Model Merger Deed has been annexed, outlining essential provisions including the terms of merger, governance and management structure, dispute resolution mechanisms, client transition processes and confidentiality safeguards, thereby facilitating a seamless and legally compliant merger process for CA firms. • FAQs on the ICAI (Aggregation of LLPs) Guidelines, 2024: To assist stakeholders in understanding and implementing the Aggregation framework for LLPs, the ACAFC developed a detailed set of FAQs addressing key aspects of the Guidelines. These FAQs provide clarity on eligibility criteria, procedural requirements, compliance obligations and practical implementation considerations, enabling members to navigate the Aggregation of LLPs framework with confidence. • CA-Setu – Networking Portal for CAs: During the year, the ACAFC envisaged a strategic digital networking portal aimed at fostering collaboration, connectivity and growth opportunities amongst Chartered Accountants and CA firms across India. The Request for Proposal (RFP) for the development of the portal was floated and the process for finalisation of the vendor was undertaken. Further, the homepage of the portal was soft-launched during the 448th meeting of the Council held on 6th–7th February 2026. • Outreach, Capacity Building & Stakeholder Sensitisation: During the year, the Committee undertook structured outreach initiatives and strategic consultative interactions on a pan-India basis to educate and sensitise stakeholders on emerging opportunities relating to growth, networking, collaboration and aggregation of CA firms. Through workshops, webinars, mentorship sessions and one-on-one consultative interactions on "Big Indian Firms", practical insights and guidance were provided to strengthen the capabilities of CA firms, address practical challenges and align the profession with evolving global best practices. • Webinars/Seminars/Workshops/Programmes The Committee organized a series of 17 programmes across the country to promote the aggregation and growth of Chartered Accountant firms. These included webinars, seminars, workshops, outreach programmes, consultative interactions, a residential refresher course, and a half-day conference on themes such as aggregation of CA firms, networking and capacity building, scaling up professional firms, opportunities for CAs as independent directors, and the

"Big Indian Firm: The Aggregation Blueprint" initiative. The programmes were conducted in both virtual and physical modes at various locations including Mumbai, Jaipur, Surat, Vadodara, Prayagraj, Rishikesh, Guwahati, Hisar, New Delhi, Faridabad, and other cities, facilitating stakeholder engagement and fostering discussions on strengthening and expanding the professional practice landscape in India.

7.4 MEMBERS IN PRACTICE COMMITTEE (MPC) The Members in Practice Committee (MPC) (Earlier named as Committee for Members in Practice), a non-standing Committee of ICAI, was established with the primary objective of revitalising the practice portfolios of practitioners and firms through a suite of targeted initiatives. Aligned with ICAI's overarching mission to cultivate globally competent professionals, the CMP undertakes multifaceted initiatives aimed at the capacity building of Chartered Accountants in practice, and regularly organises refresher courses, workshops, conferences, seminars, certificate courses, brainstorming sessions and interactive forums to strengthen the practice portfolio of members and enhance their visibility.

(I) Capacity Building Measures in Identified Thrust Areas As part of the approved Action Plan for 2025-26, the Committee identified five emerging thrust areas for developing new avenues of professional growth for practising Chartered Accountants – GST Litigation, Real Estate Consultancy, Customs & Foreign Trade Policy (FTP), MSME Advisory and Accounting Outsourcing – and conceptualised structured workshop formats under each area. These formats were also popularised amongst the Branches of ICAI to encourage them to conduct the workshops for the benefit of members in practice. The workshops conducted under these formats include: • 6-Day Workshop on GST Dispute Mechanism (Strategies & Advocacy): Conducted jointly with the GST & Indirect Taxes Committee, this non-residential workshop is a focused training programme for Chartered Accountants engaged in advising taxpayers, drafting responses to departmental notices and representing clients in adjudication and appellate proceedings, offering hands-on learning through technical sessions, case studies, drafting exercises and mock tribunal hearings. Six such workshops were successfully organised. • Two-Day Workshop on "Communication Business Skills for Global Outsourcing": A forward-looking programme equipping Chartered Accountants with the communication and interpersonal skills required to excel in global outsourcing, shared services and virtual work settings, integrating practical learning, case studies, real-life simulations and AI-driven applications. A physical batch was conducted at the Ahmedabad Branch of WIRC on 1st–2nd November 2025. • Webinar Series – "Bridging the Mindset Gap": A thought-driven webinar initiative going beyond technical mastery to strengthen the inner foundation of professional success – attitude, ethics and values – nurturing a growth-oriented mindset, ethical leadership and value-driven decision-making. The Committee successfully organised 27 such webinars (Mondays, 4:00–6:00 p.m.). • Six-Day Workshop on Real Estate Advisory and Practice Setup: Developed jointly with the Committee on Commercial Laws, Economic Advisory & NPO Cooperative, the workshop covers the pre-development, development and post-development phases of consultancy opportunities, offering an integrated understanding of RERA compliance, taxation (GST and Income-tax), due diligence, project financing, accounting and audit of real estate entities, Joint Development Agreements, legal documentation, valuation methodologies and revenue recognition under Ind AS 115. Two such workshops were successfully organised. • Two-Day Workshop on "Practice Opportunities in Customs & Foreign Trade Policy

(FTP)": Provides a comprehensive and practical understanding of the customs and international trade framework governing India's import-export ecosystem, including legal provisions, procedures, valuation mechanisms and benefit structures under the Customs Act and the FTP, enabling members to translate conceptual knowledge into strategic advisory capability. • Four-Day Workshop on "Practice Opportunities in MSME": A comprehensive learning initiative on the legal, financial and compliance ecosystem governing MSMEs, building professional capacity in advisory, consultancy and financing functions while familiarising members with Government schemes, incentives and policy frameworks for MSME growth. • Two-Day Workshop on "CA's Toolkit: Practical Guide to Appeals & Representation":

Equips members with practical skills in drafting, representation and management of tax and legal proceedings across Direct and Indirect Tax laws and allied legislations – including search/seizure procedures, compliance under the Black Money, Benami and PMLA Acts, and representation before appellate forums and tribunals. A workshop was organised at the Hyderabad Branch of SIRC on 20th–21st December 2025.

(II) Other Capacity Building Initiatives • Practice ki Paathshala – Flagship Initiative: The flagship programme, designed to enhance professional competencies in practice management, technology integration, and networking and growth, was revamped from the earlier 3-day residential format to a more flexible 4-day residential/non-residential refresher format exclusively for practising Chartered Accountants, blending practice management, technology adoption (including AI and automation training), personal development and leadership skills. Nine batches of the programme were successfully organised. • "Grow Your Practice" Webinar Series: A series equipping Chartered Accountants with the latest tools, strategies and insights to expand their practices, covering business development, digital transformation, client management and emerging opportunities; seven webinars were successfully conducted. • Career Readiness Programme for New CAs: Organised jointly with the Committee for Members in Industry & Business, this 6-day virtual programme (18 hours) empowers newly qualified Chartered Accountants with informed career decision-making, with a focused deep-dive into industry and independent practice. An online batch was organised from 24th to 27th December 2025. • Pathway to Practice – 7-Day Residential Training Programme for COP Applicants: The Committee revamped the structure of this comprehensive residential programme, tailored for first-time Certificate of Practice applicants, covering practice management, regulatory compliance, client engagement, technology adoption and ethical standards, conducted at the ICAI Centres of Excellence at Jaipur and Hyderabad. • One-Day Workshop on "Reimagining the CA Profession: Emerging Practice Opportunities in a Globalised Economy": Conceptualised to sensitise members, particularly SMPs, to emerging high-growth domains – including GCCs, GIFT City, international taxation and cross-border advisory – and to facilitate a mindset shift from compliance-oriented practice to value-driven advisory services. Two such workshops were organised. • Two-Day Workshop on "Grow Your Practice 2026": A thought-leadership initiative empowering SMPs to evolve into future-ready, high-value advisory and entrepreneurial practices, anchored in innovation, ethics, digital transformation and AI-enabled workflows. Three such workshops were organised.

(III) Certificate Courses The Committee revamped its two Certificate Courses:

(a) the Certificate Course on Preparation of Appeals, Drafting of Deeds & Documents, and Representation before Appellate Authorities and Statutory Bodies (revamped as the

"Certificate Course on Legal Drafting and Representation before Authorities", 30 CPE hours), designed to equip Chartered Accountants with advanced practical skills in drafting legal documents, handling appeals and representing clients before statutory authorities and appellate tribunals; and

(b) the Certificate Course on Wealth Management and Financial Planning (30 CPE hours), covering financial planning, behavioural finance, equity and bond investment strategies, portfolio management, AI applications in wealth management and alternative investment avenues, supported by simulations and practical exercises. Two online batches of the revamped Certificate Course on Wealth Management and Financial Planning were successfully organised.

(IV) Member Benefit Arrangements During the year 2025-26, the Committee entered into various arrangements with service providers to extend exclusive benefits to members, including Apollo Diagnostic Services (discounts on health checkup packages); AccuReco (GST ITC reconciliation tool); CIBIL by TransUnion CIBIL Ltd.; Global Assure (roadside assistance); Medanta Hospital (renewal of the existing tie-up); HDFC Bank (term loan and dropline overdraft facilities); AU Small Finance Bank (personal loans for Chartered Accountants);

GovEVA (digital ESG SaaS platform); and CLAonline.

(V) Revamped Website of the Committee As part of its strategic action plan, the Committee undertook a comprehensive initiative to revamp its official website and successfully launched it under the new domain https://cmp.icai.org/ positioning it as a dynamic, member-centric digital platform. The revamped website serves as a centralised knowledge and engagement hub, featuring dedicated sections on capacity-building initiatives, memberbenefit arrangements, learning resources and practitioner-oriented tools, and events. It offers extensive information on workshops, webinars, certificate courses and other programmes, along with access to publications, background materials and post-event content (including recorded sessions), serving as an effective communication and outreach medium through regular updates and announcements.

(VI) Publications • GIFT IFSC: New Opportunities for Chartered Accountants • How to Grow Your Practice • Guide on Setting up Practice of Real Estate Consultancy: Compliance, Advisory and Evolving Frontiers

7.5 MSME & START-UP COMMITTEE Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises and Start-ups are the backbone of the Indian economy and key drivers of employment, innovation, and inclusive growth on the path to Viksit Bharat. The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) has consistently aligned its initiatives with the nation's priorities and constituted a dedicated MSME & Startup committee to act as a dedicated enabler for small businesses and new-age enterprises. MSME & Startup Committee is focused on empowering MSMEs and start-ups and remains committed to strengthening India's entrepreneurial ecosystem by supporting MSMEs and startups through knowledge dissemination, capacity building, policy advocacy, and professional guidance. In the year 2025-26, the Committee has undertaken a wide range of initiatives e.g. publications, seminars, conferences, webinars, outreach programmes, and stakeholder engagement to promote excellence, foster innovation, and contribute meaningfully to the sustainable growth and competitiveness of India's MSME and startup ecosystem.

The key activities and achievements of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Flagship Events • ICAI MSME Mahotsav 2025: Conducted on 27th June 2025 across 140+ ICAI Branches nationwide, supporting nearly 20,000 MSMEs through 400+ advisory helpdesks, with the participation of Ministers and senior officials. The initiative earned recognition from the World, Asia and India Books of Records for the "Maximum MSME Mentorship Felicitations in a Day". • ICAI MSME & Startup Conclave 2025: Held on 26th–27th June 2025 at Mumbai to mark International MSME Day, with 3,000+ delegates, 19+ sessions, 60+ speakers and 38+ exhibitors. 65 start-up pitches were made, with 3 start-ups securing funding. The event was supported by the Ministry of MSME, NSIC and CAIT, and the ICAI MSME Helpdesk offered on-the-spot mentoring. • ICAI MSME Connect – Stakeholders Strategic Meet 2025: Held on 24th October 2025 at New Delhi with 80+ participants and representation from 60+ ministries, regulators, financial institutions and incubators, focusing on MSME and start-up challenges, digital transformation and innovation, and exploring strategic collaborations and MoUs to strengthen the MSME ecosystem.

(II) Initiatives towards Partner in Nation Building • Collaboration with the Ministry of MSME: The Committee collaborated with the Ministry of MSME to strengthen MSME capabilities nationwide, including support in conducting Entrepreneurship & Skill Development Programmes (EAP, E-SDP, MDP) with Development Facilitation Offices, leveraging ICAI's network of 185 Branches, 5 Regional Councils and 50+ overseas Chapters, and conducting national seminars, financial management sessions and awareness campaigns at Kolkata, Mandla, Jabalpur and Solan. • SPARK – The 100K Collective Program: A formal association with the Women's Collective Forum was entered into through a Letter of Engagement, with ICAI as knowledge partner of the SPARK – The 100K Collective Program led by Smt. Smriti Irani Ji. The pilot programme was held on 24th September 2025 at New Delhi, including an ICAI expert session on Income-tax and GST, with the participation of over 350 women entrepreneurs, reinforcing support to women-led MSMEs and start-ups. • Vision Rajasthan 2026: The Committee participated in the "Vision Rajasthan 2026" mega exhibition held from 19th to 21st January 2026 at Sirohi, Rajasthan (an Aspirational District identified by NITI Aayog), setting up an exhibition stall to create awareness on corporate governance, MCA21, Ease of Doing Business, MSME formalisation, entrepreneurship and compliance, supporting the outreach initiatives of the Ministry of Corporate Affairs. • Networking Meet: A Networking Meet on "Digitalising the Procure-to-Pay Process for MSMEs:

Role of Chartered Accountants" was organised on 17th January 2026 at ICAI Tower, BKC, Mumbai, featuring industry experts from NPCI Bharat BillPay Limited and Tally, providing practical insights into the digitalisation of the procure-to-pay ecosystem and highlighting the critical role of Chartered Accountants in advising MSMEs on digital adoption, regulatory compliance and internal controls.

(III) Memoranda of Understanding The Committee entered into MoUs with prominent institutions, State Governments and incubation centres, including NPCI Bharat BillPay Limited; the Foundation for Innovation and Technology Transfer, IIT Delhi; the Department of Micro, Small & Medium Enterprises, Uttar Pradesh; Tamil Nadu Startup & Innovation Mission (Startup TN); the National Small Industries Corporation (NSIC); BIL-Ryerson Technology Startup Incubator Foundation; Gujarat Student Startup and Innovation Hub (i-Hub); IIM Lucknow Enterprise Incubation Centre; AIC Mahamana Foundation for Innovation and Entrepreneurship, IM BHU, Varanasi; AIC Nalanda Institute of Technology Foundation, Bhubaneswar;

IIM Sambalpur; Software Technology Parks of India (STPI); Startup Odisha; the Government of Odisha; and MeitY Startup Hub.

(IV) NEXTLEAP Program The flagship 8-week start-up accelerator programme, hosted by the ICAI Singapore Chapter and Beyond Seed, celebrated 60 years of India–Singapore diplomatic relations. 44 start-ups participated, including 38 from India, with the top 3 start-ups being Aeronero Solutions, eMSME and Seamless XR (2 from India). A physical Demo Day was held at Singapore, providing selected start-ups the opportunity to pitch before global investors, enabling cross-border mentorship, investor access and global market readiness.

(V) ICAI MSME Clinic – Nationwide Launch Announced on 24th October 2025 and launched nationwide on 10th December 2025 in the presence of officials from the Ministry of MSME, GeM, NSIC, SIDBI, UP MSME and ICAI Branches/Regional Offices, the ICAI MSME Clinic provides pro-bono weekly advisory support to MSMEs across India. Hosted at ICAI Branches and Regional Offices, it offers expert guidance on financial literacy, compliance, technology adoption and business resilience, with walk-in advisory available every Friday for 2 hours at all ICAI Branches, and comprehensive support focused on finance, accounting, technology, human resources, operations and marketing.

(VI) Capacity Building, Seminars and Outreach • Certificate Courses: 19 batches of the Certificate Course on MSME were attended by over 1,900 members, and 8 batches of the Certificate Course on Startup involved around 900 participants. The weekly "Pathshala Series" knowledge initiative, along with refresher courses and publications, further strengthened member capacity. The ICAI Start-up Portal (startup.icai.org) and ICAI MSME Portal (msme.icai.org) continued to facilitate collaboration amongst start-ups, MSMEs, Chartered Accountants, mentors, investors and incubation centres. • Residential Refresher Courses: The 4th and 5th batches of the RRC on "Startups & the Role of CAs as Investment Catalysts" were conducted at i-Hub, Ahmedabad (July–September 2025), hosted by the Ahmedabad Branch of WIRC; a two-day RRC was organised on 10th–11th January 2026 at Madai, Madhya Pradesh, covering tax planning for start-ups, the Startup India Scheme and secured/unsecured lending for MSMEs; and a two-day RRC was organised by the Kishangarh Branch on 23rd–24th April 2025 at Udaipur (12 CPE hours). • Seminars and Workshops at Branches: The Committee organised/supported around 19 seminars, conferences and workshops hosted by Branches across the country during the year – including the Conference on Startup and MSME at Ahmedabad on 2nd August 2025 on the theme

"Idea to Implementation", graced by Hon'ble Shri Jagdish Vishwakarma, Minister of State, Government of Gujarat, with CA. Sunil Talati as Guest of Honour; full-day and half-day seminars at Varanasi, Patna (273 members), Jaipur (496 participants), Kota, Bhilai, Faridabad, Raipur, Jalandhar, Coimbatore, Bilaspur, Raigarh, Dehradun, and Jammu & Kashmir – covering MSME schemes and subsidies, Section 43B(h), GSTR-9 & 9C, SME IPOs, direct taxation, succession planning, cyber safety, the new Income Tax Bill and digital enablement of MSMEs. • National Outreach: ICAI MSME & Startup stalls/pavilions engaged stakeholders at the Startup Mahakumbh 2025, New Delhi (500+ start-ups); the Global Investors Summit 2025, Bhopal (showcased to 10,000+ global investors); the Uttar Pradesh International Trade Show 2025, Greater Noida (earning recognition from the Government of Uttar Pradesh); the IICA Northeast Conclave 2025, Shillong; the Global Fintech Fest 2025, Mumbai (7th–9th October 2025, with a keynote on "Bharat Connect for Business"); and the Tamil Nadu Global Startup Summit 2025, Coimbatore (9th–10th October 2025), under the Government of India Pavilion.

The MSME & Startup Committee continues to play a pivotal role in supporting MSMEs and start-ups by creating valuable opportunities, fostering collaborations and empowering stakeholders with the knowledge and resources they need for sustainable growth and transformation, and remains committed to policy advocacy, capacity building and strengthening the MSME ecosystem across India.

(VII) Publications and Knowledge Resources • Publication on State-Specific MSME Policies • Resource Handbook for MSME, Startup, OPC and Others • Resource Handbook for ICAI MSME Clinic • Newsletters and Knowledge Series

7.6 COMMITTEE ON PROMOTING WORK LIFE BALANCE The Committee on Promoting Work–Life Balance (now merged in Beneficiary Schemes Directorate), in the year 2025-26, undertook a series of focused and impactful initiatives aimed at integrating wellness, resilience, and sustainable professional practices within the Chartered Accountants fraternity. Guided by the belief that a healthy professional is a productive professional, the Committee worked towards embedding wellbeing as a routine and non-negotiable part of professional life, rather than an occasional afterthought.

Various Initiatives undertaken by the Committee

(I) Year-Round Wellness Initiatives and Activities Through the observance of global wellness days such as World Laughter Day, World Bicycle Day, the 11th International Yoga Day 2025, and World Meditation Day 2025, the Committee underscored that physical, mental, and emotional wellness are essential pillars of long-term professional excellence. These observances were designed to spark reflection among members and students on the importance of pausing, recharging, and reconnecting with themselves amid demanding professional schedules. Notably, the Committee's efforts were recognized and ICAI's name was recorded in Asia Book of Records, 2025, for organizing yoga sessions across the maximum number of branches on the occasion of International Yoga Day ,2025. This recognition stands as a testament to extensive participation of members and students and the Committee's success in mobilizing the CA fraternity nationwide.

(II) Fit CA Movement In alignment with the Hon'ble Prime Minister's Fit India Movement, the Committee launched the 'Fit CA Movement' initiative, offering daily virtual Yoga and Zumba sessions, accessible at the click of a button. Simple in design yet impactful in its reach, this initiative aimed to encourage Chartered Accountants to integrate fitness into their daily routines despite demanding professional commitments.

By promoting a culture of health and wellbeing, the initiative contributed to the broader vision of a Viksit Bharat.

(III) Weekly Webinar Series By adopting a digital-first approach, the Committee organized impactful virtual webinars on a weekly basis, led by domain experts, enabling nationwide participation and accessibility regardless of geographical location. These webinars covered diverse dimensions of work-life balance, with the objective of encouraging Chartered Accountants to adopt sustainable lifestyle habits, manage stress effectively, set healthy professional boundaries, and cultivate resilience while continuing to excel in their careers.

(IV) Memorandum of Understanding with The Art of Living The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), through its Committee on Promoting Work-Life Balance, entered into a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) with The Art of Living on 10 February,

2026. This partnership aims to promote the holistic development of members and students by enhancing their life skills, emotional resilience, ethical values, and overall wellbeing, thereby contributing to the continued advancement of the profession. The MoU underscores ICAI's commitment to nurturing professionals who are not only technically proficient but also guided by integrity, accountability, and a strong sense of social responsibility. Through this partnership, the ICAI seeks to encourage initiatives focused on overall well-being, stress management, and inner development, while further strengthening the ethical foundation essential for the accounting profession and nationbuilding.

Through these collective efforts, the Committee encouraged the CA fraternity to prioritise wellness, health, and personal passions alongside professional excellence, positioning work–life balance as a cornerstone of resilient and future-ready professionals.

7.7 GLOBAL TRADE & SERVICES COMMITTEE The Global Trade & Services Committee (erstwhile Development of International Trade, Services & WTO Directorate) of ICAI is committed to strengthening India's presence in global services trade by promoting international collaboration, enhancing professional opportunities and positioning accounting and finance as key export services in the evolving global economy. The Committee works closely with government bodies, industry stakeholders and international institutions to facilitate trade integration, policy engagement and global professional mobility. The Committee's action plan for the Council Year 2026-27 focuses on five key areas: Trade & Market Integration; Global Services & Professional Mobility;

Policy & Government Affairs; GCC & Emerging Global Ecosystems; and Capacity Building.
