(I) Initiatives towards Partner in Nation Building • Extension of Most Favoured Nation (MFN) treatment to New Zealand: The Council, at its 447th Meeting held on 10th–11th December 2025, considered the request received from the Ministry of Commerce and Industry regarding the extension of MFN treatment to New Zealand under the ongoing India–New Zealand Free Trade Agreement (FTA) negotiations, insofar as professional accounting services are concerned. • ITEC Training Programmes under the aegis of the Ministry of External Affairs: The 2nd ITEC Training Programme, on the theme "Frontiers of Global Accounting: Mastering Digital Transformation", was conducted from 15th to 26th September 2025 with 29 delegates from 17 countries, including Bhutan, Botswana, Fiji, Ghana, the Kingdom of Eswatini, Laos, Liberia, Mali, Mauritius, Nepal, Rwanda, Sierra Leone, South Africa, Sri Lanka, South Sudan, Tanzania and Uzbekistan. Further strengthening global engagement, the 3rd ITEC Training Programme, themed "Engaging Public Accountants in the 21st Century", was held from 12th to 23rd January 2026 and attended by 18 participants from 11 countries. These initiatives reflect ICAI's commitment to fostering knowledge exchange, strengthening capacity building and promoting international cooperation in accounting and auditing. • Chintan Shivir 2025 on Professional Services: ICAI, in collaboration with the Ministry of Commerce and Industry and the Services Export Promotion Council (SEPC), organised a Chintan Shivir on Professional Services titled "Expanding Global Horizons: Opportunities for Indian Professionals" on 23rd December 2025, witnessing the participation of over 100 delegates from industry, the professional fraternity and Ministries. Four focused sessions covered making professionals globally ready; strengthening international mobility through MRAs and MoUs; developing global networks through professional chapters abroad; and leveraging FTAs to boost exports of professional services.

(II) Participation at International Platforms • Participation at Accountex London: ICAI showcased its activities and initiatives in the field of accounting and finance at Accountex, held on 14th–15th May 2025 in London, through a dedicated 18 sq.m. stall. The stall was visited by Mr. Malcolm Bacchus, President, ICAEW, and Ms. Ami Copeland, CEO, Institute of Certified Bookkeepers, reflecting strong international interest in ICAI's initiatives. • ICAI represents India at the WTO, Geneva: ICAI was represented at the Council for Trade in Services (CTS) Thematic Session on Recognition of Professional Qualifications at the World Trade Organization, Geneva, on 2nd October 2025. The session witnessed participation from over 150 delegates across 120+ countries, highlighting the global emphasis on professional mobility and mutual recognition. • ICAI Global Week 2025: Held online on the theme "Navigating Global Opportunities" from 29th September to 7th October 2025, the event aimed to strengthen global professional linkages, promote cross-border collaboration and explore emerging business and professional opportunities worldwide. It was inaugurated by Mr. Jean Bouquot, President, IFAC, as Guest of Honour, in the presence of CA. Charanjot Singh Nanda, President, ICAI; CA. Prasanna Kumar D, Vice-President, ICAI; CA. Abhay Chhajed, Convenor; and Shri Darpan Jain, Joint Secretary, Department of Commerce, Government of India.

(III) Participation at the National Level • Participation in the IFSCA Chintan Shivir 2025 roundtable interaction held on 11th September 2025 at GIFT City, Gandhinagar. • ICAI delegation visit to GIFT City–IFSCA on 28th August 2025: As part of its MoU with the IFSCA, ICAI organised a delegation visit with 63 selected members, covering NSE IX, Bank of Baroda IBU, DevX and a GIFT City tour, preceding the GCC Summit. • Two batches of the 3-Day Residential Hands-on Workshop on Overseas Outsourcing Services

(US) were conducted: from 30th May to 1st June 2025 at the Centre of Excellence, Jaipur (35 members), and on 17th–19th November 2025 at the Centre of Excellence, Hyderabad. • MoU Renewal with SEPC: ICAI renewed its MoU with the Services Export Promotion Council on 1st July 2025 to further enhance collaboration in promoting India's services exports. • Accounting Process Outsourcing Portal: A dynamic platform fostering collaboration between foreign firms and Indian CA firms, connecting foreign firms with a vast network of skilled Indian CA firms renowned for their expertise in local regulations, taxation, auditing and financial consulting. The Portal, launched at WOFA 2025, is now ready for Indian CA firms and foreign clients to register through the institutional framework.

(IV) Supporting the Government During the year, meetings were held with senior Government functionaries, including Shri Viraj Singh, IFS, Additional Secretary, Ministry of External Affairs; CA. Mahaveer Singhvi, IFS, Joint Secretary

(NEST), Ministry of External Affairs; Shri Thiru V. Arun Roy, IAS, Secretary, Industries, Investment Promotion & Commerce Department, Government of Tamil Nadu (regarding the State's GCC policy framework); Shri Darpan Jain, IAS, Joint Secretary, Ministry of Commerce & Industry (on 10th September 2025 and 19th November 2025, regarding MFN treatment under the India–New Zealand FTA negotiations); Ms. Rupanjali Karthik, Director, Ministry of Commerce & Industry (3rd September 2025); Ms. Anoopa Nair, Director (Services SMD), Department of Commerce (14th November 2025);

Shri Babul Supriyo (on GCC opportunities in West Bengal, 7th August 2025); and Shri Alok V.

Chaturvedi, IAS (Retd.), Director General, EPCES (28th October 2025, on MoU deliverables and future collaboration). A virtual meeting on Trade in Services between India and New Zealand was also attended on 27th August 2025.

Representations/Technical Inputs to the Government (other than taxation areas):

The Committee provided more than 25 technical inputs and representations to the Government during the year, broadly covering: • FTA/CEPA negotiations: Inputs on MFN treatment and draft Market Access offers for accounting, auditing, bookkeeping and taxation services under the India–New Zealand FTA; mandate on MFN offers under the India–EU FTA; India–Chile FTA/CEPA (market access and mobility of professionals); India–UK FTA (India's offer on accounting and bookkeeping); impact assessment of the India–UAE CEPA (services trade); and collaboration under the India–Japan CEPA. • Bilateral trade fora: Inputs for the 2nd India–Indonesia WGTI Meeting (including action-taken status), the 3rd India–Brunei Darussalam Joint Trade Committee, the 6th India–Vietnam Joint Trade Sub-Commission, the Second Session of the India–Mauritius High-Powered Joint Trade Committee, the 14th India–Philippines JWGTI, the India–Singapore JWGTI and the HCIM meeting with the Indonesian counterpart. • Multilateral and other inputs: Inputs on key events for India's BRICS Presidency 2026; the OECD STRI Database update (nationality/citizenship requirements); the draft Ministerial Outcome Document of UNCTAD; preparation of the Roadmap for India's Services Exports (Business Sector); barriers encountered by India's services industry while exporting services to Germany; and the waiver of the requirement of 3,000 hours of practical experience for auditor registration in Australia.

(V) Promoting India as an Accounting GCC – GCC Summit Series The Group to Promote India as an Accounting Global Capability Centre (GCC), functioning under the erstwhile DITS & WTO Directorate, organised a series of GCC Summits across major Indian cities under the theme "From Ledgers to Global Leadership: CAs Shaping GCCs" to position India as a preferred global hub for Accounting and Finance GCCs. The Summit Series commenced in New Delhi (38 distinguished speakers and 11+ curated sessions, inaugurated by Joint Secretary, Ministry of External Affairs, followed by GIFT City, Gujarat (38+ eminent speakers, inaugurated by Chairperson, IFSCA) and Hyderabad (34 eminent speakers across 10+ sessions, inaugurated by Former Minister, Government of Telangana).

The Pune Summit emerged as a flagship two-day event with 500+ delegates, 11+ curated sessions and 15+ eminent speakers; the Bengaluru Summit recorded 1,000+ delegates with 12+ sessions and 25+ eminent speakers, with strong support from KDEM and IFSCA; while the Jaipur and Kolkata editions each hosted 250+ delegates. The initiative received strong support from key government bodies and ecosystem partners, including MeitY, the Ministry of Commerce & Industry, IFSCA, SEPC, Invest India, NSDC, IIM Sambalpur, NFSU, RRU and leading industry partners, and highlighted the growing role of Chartered Accountants in the GCC ecosystem.

(VI) Initiatives for Members/Students • Online Certificate Courses on Overseas Outsourcing Services: In the series of Online Certificate Courses on Overseas Outsourcing Services (UK), (US) and (Australia), 11 batches of US, 2 batches of UK and 1 batch of Australia have been organised so far, in which 1,600 candidates have registered. In addition, a Three-Day Residential Workshop on Overseas Outsourcing Services (US Market), designed for members who had successfully passed the certificate course, was held from 30th May to 1st June 2025 at the Centre of Excellence, Jaipur, with 35 participants. • Global Udaan Webinar Series: Launched in April 2025 as part of the Committee's capacitybuilding action plan, 39 webinars were organised in the series during the period from 1st April 2025 to 30th April 2026, covering diverse themes such as GCCs in India; FTAs and India's trade policies; opportunities in GIFT City/IFSC; outsourcing for the US, UK and Australian markets; US GAAP and global audit practices; fintech and AI-powered finance; cross-border succession, business setup and compliance; opportunities in Egypt, Kuwait, Qatar and Singapore; Virtual CFO services; and building a global practice. • Promoting Foreign Languages amongst Members: ICAI has tie-ups with the official cultural language centres of foreign embassies in India for the delivery of online foreign language courses for members and students, in order to enhance their access to foreign opportunities. The status of courses undertaken since inception is as under:

Language Course Partner Institution Batches Enrolments Spanish (Levels A1.1 to A2.2) Instituto Cervantes French Alliance Française Japanese (Levels A1 to A2/B1) Japan Foundation Business English British Council German Goethe-Institut Publications:  The Future of Work: Outsourcing, Technology & Trade in Services  Paper to Port – A Chartered Accountant's Handbook on Export Documentation -  GIFT IFSC : New Opportunities for Chartered Accountants  Four quarterly editions of "Global Gateway", the e-newsletter of the erstwhile Development of International Trade, Services & WTO Directorate.

7.8 PROFESSIONAL DEVELOPMENT COMMITTEE (PDC) The Professional Development Committee (PDC), established in 1962 with a mission to enhance the skill sets of members of the Institute in existing and new areas, actively engages with the Government and regulatory authorities to promote the professional opportunities of Chartered Accountants across diverse sectors.

The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Meetings with Regulators and Other Bodies • Reserve Bank of India: Four meetings were held with the Executive Director, Department of Supervision, RBI – on 1st April 2025 (Mumbai: appointment of SCAs/SBAs, rotation policy, audit coverage); 8th April 2025 (Delhi: Governance Framework of Audit in Public Sector Banks); and 15th April 2026 (Mumbai: strengthening audit framework in PSBs, technology in assurance, ICAI certifications for banking assignments). A meeting was also held with the CGM, Department of Supervision, RBI on 29th May 2025 (structured training for Audit Committee Chairmen, regular ICAI–RBI meetings, NBFC compliances). More than 45 State Level Coordination Committee

(SLCC) meetings convened by RBI across India were attended by ICAI representatives during the year. • NABARD: Three meetings were held with the Chief General Manager, Institutional Development Department, NABARD – on 31st July 2025 (audit of Cooperative Banks and RRBs); 27th February 2026 (audit of StCBs, CtCBs and RRBs, interactive meets for auditors); and a follow-up online meeting on 17th April 2026 (allotment issues for SAs post-merger of RRBs). • Ministry of Cooperation / CRCS: Three meetings were held with the CRCS and Ministry of Cooperation – on 30th April 2025 (challenges in Concurrent Audit assignments); 13th May 2025 (formulation of auditing/accounting norms for Multi-State Co-operative Societies, jointly with C&AG, RBI and NCUI); and 18th March 2026 (empanelment of firms for MSCS concurrent audit). • Office of C&AG: A meeting was held on 10th November 2025 with the Deputy CAG (Commercial & RC) regarding feedback on the existing empanelment policy and suggestions for new norms following the notification of the Aggregation of LLPs Guidelines, 2024. Majority of ICAI's submissions were accepted and incorporated in the 2026-27 policy. • SEBI: A meeting was held with the Executive Director, SEBI at SEBI Bhawan, Mumbai on 26th November 2025 to address issues arising from the Notice Inviting Applications for Empanelment of Forensic Auditors issued on 14th November 2025. • IRDAI: A meeting was held with Member (Finance & Investment), IRDAI on 12th March 2026 at Hyderabad to deliberate on empanelment of audit firms, cybersecurity and compliance audits, standardisation of regulatory certifications by Chartered Accountants and organisation of an Insurance Summit for statutory auditors. • Other institutions: Meetings were held with IBA (29th May 2025: standardisation of bankrelated certificates, certification responsibilities of concurrent auditors); IAGES (23rd March 2026: parameters for empanelment, awareness programmes, standardisation of assessment fee); IIM Jammu (13th April 2026: joint programmes, seminars and training under the MoU); and NITI Aayog (17th April 2026: renewal of the Statement of Intent with WEP and related matters).

(II) Major Achievements • Concept Paper on Governance Framework of Audit in Public Sector Banks: Submitted to RBI following the 8th April 2025 meeting; a draft structure for a 2-day training programme for Audit Committee of the Board (ACB) members of the banking industry was also submitted. • "OA" Category Panel submitted to IBA and other banks: PDC introduced the "OA" Category last year and submitted the panel with this category to IBA and other banks for allotment of assignments other than statutory bank branch audits. • ICAI MEF Panel accepted by the Office of CRCS: After persistent coordination and followup, the Multipurpose Panel prepared by ICAI was accepted by the Office of the Central Registrar of Cooperative Societies for conducting concurrent audits of Multi-State Co-operative Societies. • Regional Professional Development Research Cells established: PDC established regionwise PD Research Cells across North, South, East, West and Central India to foster localised professional development and ensure that opportunities can be accessed across the country. • MoU with IAGES (Indian Association of Gold Excellence and Standards): PDC entered into an MoU with IAGES, created awareness among members about professional opportunities arising from it, supplied a panel of CA firms and organised interactive meets in Pune, Ahmedabad, Hyderabad, Chennai, Bengaluru, Kolkata and Delhi. • MoU with IIM Jammu: PDC entered into an MoU with IIM Jammu to provide specialised management courses exclusively designed for Chartered Accountants. • MoU with NITI Aayog (WEP): Various actions were undertaken for empowering women under the Statement of Intent between NITI Aayog and ICAI, including reviewing and vetting content, contributing articles and providing experts for capacity-building sessions. • Dissemination of RBI's asset classification divergence Write-Up: Information on typical reasons observed by RBI for divergence in asset classification during supervisory cycles for UCBs and NBFCs was disseminated to all Statutory Central Auditors/Statutory Auditors of UCBs and NBFCs.

(III) Panel Submissions to Authorities The Multipurpose Panel of ICAI, developed through the Multipurpose Empanelment Form (MEF), serves as an authoritative, comprehensive and reliable database of Chartered Accountants and audit firms fulfilling prescribed eligibility criteria. During the period from 1st April 2025 to 30th April 2026, 24 panel submissions were made to various authorities/agencies, including the Reserve Bank of India (Bank Branch Auditors Panel 2025-26); NABARD (RRB Auditors Panel 2025-26); SEBI; CRCS (Ministry of Cooperation); NABARD (General Manager); National Pension System Trust; Indian Bank, Federal Bank, Tamil Nadu Grama Bank (Inspection & Audit); IAGES; Food Corporation of India (SILO Division);

Gujarat Technological University; Superintendent of Police, Directorate of Vigilance & Anti-Corruption, Tamil Nadu; CBI, Mumbai; The Institution of Engineers (India), Kolkata; FA & CAO, RD & PR Department, Chennai; Deputy Conservator of Forests, Goa; and others.

(IV) Representations Submitted • NABARD: Representations were submitted on 4th September 2025 (eligibility criteria for SAs of Cooperative Banks); 18th October 2025 (revision of audit fees for SAs of RRBs, based on both deposits and advances); and 15th December 2025 (audit-related matters for Cooperative Banks and RRBs). • Office of C&AG: Submissions were made on C&AG's draft empanelment policy for 2026-27; on ICAI's recommendations for empanelment of CA firms setting up LLPs in IFSC (GIFT City) (26th December 2025); on the Expression of Willingness for audit of Autonomous/Local/other Bodies (July 2025–March 2027 and April 2026–March 2027, with ICAI's submission accepted and the deadline extended to 29th March 2026). • CRCS, Ministry of Cooperation: A representation was submitted regarding issues arising from the Order dated 3rd October 2024 for the panel of auditors for FY 2024-25. • Delhi Cooperative Societies: A representation was submitted highlighting that there had been no revision in the audit fees structure since 2010, despite considerable increases in operational costs and inflation. • J&K Bank: A representation was submitted on 22nd July 2025 regarding restrictions on FCMAs from undertaking Concurrent Audit assignments. • Government of Himachal Pradesh: A representation was submitted on 24th April 2026 regarding empanelment of auditors under the Cooperative Societies Rules, 1971.

(V) Programmes and Interactive Meets • Interactive Meet on Strengthening Governance in UCBs, Mumbai, 31st July 2025:

Around 60 delegates (CFOs, Heads of Accounts/Internal Audit and Audit Committee Chairman/Members of UCBs) attended, with a session addressed by the GM, Department of Supervision, RBI on "Governance in UCBs and Functioning of Audit Committees". • Interactive Meet for CA Directors, Audit Committee Chairmen and CFOs of the Banking Sector, Mumbai, 9th January 2026: Held on the theme "Shaping the Future of Banking and Finance: Evolving and Versatile Role of CAs", graced by Shri Satish Kashinath Marathe, Director, RBI and NABARD, as Guest of Honour. The CGM, Department of Regulation, RBI addressed on "Shaping the Future of Banking with Resilience in the Digitalisation Era". More than 40 delegates attended in person. • Statutory Central Auditors' Meet 2026-27, Mumbai, 24th March 2026: Held in hybrid mode and attended by more than 130 Statutory Central Auditors of Public Sector and Private Sector Banks. The Guest of Honour, Executive Director, RBI, emphasised professional scepticism, timely risk identification and the importance of high audit quality. Technical sessions covered RBI expectations, treasury audits, NPA identification, data analytics and audit reporting (LFAR and IFCoFR). • Three-Day Residential Programme on "Insurance and Investment Audit", Hyderabad, 18th–20th December 2025: Attended by 35 participants, with a keynote by Member (Actuary), IRDAI, on the expectations of IRDAI from auditors. Discussions were also held on the standardisation of certificates issued by CAs under IRDAI regulations and utilisation of the MEF panel of ICAI.

In addition, the Committee organised 36+ seminars, workshops and Residential Refresher Courses across India, including National CA Conferences at Cuttack, Vijayawada, Lucknow, Udupi, Kolhapur and Meerut; Residential Refresher Courses at Alappuzha, Mangalore, Vapi, Rajkot, Bhavnagar, Kolhapur and Goa; and single/two-day seminars and workshops at Gandhinagar, Ranchi, Bhilwara, Surat, Anand, Bhilai, Guntur, Udaipur, Nashik, Nagpur, Kota, Bhubaneswar, Varanasi, Goa, Solapur, Meerut, Guwahati, Bhopal, Alwar, Cuttack and Indore.

RBI-nominated seminars/workshops were also attended/facilitated throughout the year: a Workshop for Statutory Auditors of UCBs in Andhra Pradesh (Vijayawada, 21st April 2025), a Workshop for SAs of UCBs in Punjab & Haryana (Chandigarh, 9th September 2025), two programmes on "Introduction to Accounting Standards" and "Bank Balance Sheet & Ratio Analysis" (September 2025), and a Conference for CEOs and SAs of select UCBs at the Nagpur Regional Office (27th October 2025). A Workshop on

"Revision of Statutory Auditor's Report and Financial Statements of PSUs" was also supported at the C&AG office, New Delhi (5th December 2025).

(VI) PDC's DNA – Discover New Avenues Webinar Series The Professional Development Committee (PDC) continued its "PDC's DNA – Discover New Avenues" webinar series to create awareness about emerging professional opportunities for Chartered Accountants. During the period, five editions of the series were organised on topics including alternative modes of finance, the NBFC sector, accreditation in the gold industry, and the application of data analysis and Artificial Intelligence in forensic investigations, with the webinars attracting participation of 900+ and 1,000+ attendees, respectively.

(VII) Diploma and Certificate Courses • Certificate Course on Project Financing: Two batches were conducted. The 1st Online Batch (9th May –1st June 2025) had 88 registered members; the Assessment Test was held on 3rd August 2025 and results declared on 13th August 2025. The 2nd Online Batch (10th April–3rd May 2026) had 200 registered participants; the Assessment Test was held on 25th May 2026. • Diploma in Insurance and Risk Management (DIRM): The DIRM Course has three phases.

A total of 5,618 members have registered since inception, of whom 1,059 have qualified the Course and 77 have passed the Technical Examination but are yet to undergo the Orientation Programme. The region-wise registration is: Central – 1,207; Eastern – 674; Northern – 850;

Southern – 1,578; Western – 1,309. A two-day DIRM Orientation Programme for members who cleared the Technical Examination was conducted from 27th to 30th May 2025 through ICAI's Digital Learning Hub; the programme structure was revised to align with current industry trends and professional requirements.

7.9 PROFESSIONAL SKILL ENRICHMENT COMMITTEE (PSEC) The Professional Skills Enrichment Committee (now merged in members in practice committee) focused on empowering Chartered Accountants with contemporary professional, behavioural, managerial and leadership skills required to excel in a dynamic and evolving professional environment. Through a wide range of initiatives encompassing skill enhancement, structured learning, self-assessment, digital learning, wellness and professional development, the Committee promoted continuous learning and holistic professional growth, enabling members to remain future-ready and aligned with emerging business and professional requirements.

Key Initiatives

(I) Awards, Recognitions and Record Achievements Recognising the importance of continuous skill development and holistic professional growth, the Committee undertook several large-scale initiatives which received national and international recognition: Received Silver Medal under the category "Exceptional Vocational & Skill Development Platform" at the ET Education Excellence Awards 2025.  The Work-Life Balance initiative completed 100 sessions and was recognised by the Asia Book of Records and India Book of Records.  Received recognition from the Asia Book of Records and India Book of Records for conducting the maximum number of Skills Enrichment Programmes during 12–19 July 2025.  Received recognition from the Asia Book of Records and India Book of Records for conducting the maximum number of Online Yoga Sessions.

(II) Assessment and Communication Skill Initiatives Recognising that communication, critical thinking and behavioural competencies are essential for modern-day professionals, the Committee introduced various assessment and skill enhancement initiatives: Launched ICAI SkillsX – National Debate & Oratory Competition through Branch, Regional and National level rounds to promote communication, articulation, analytical thinking and leadership skills.  Continued ICAI CAlibre, a periodic MCQ-based assessment initiative on Vocabulary and Grammar; completed 25 series and launched the 26th series.  Continued ICAI CAvalry Psychometric Assessment Series; completed the first series on Branding & Communication Skills and launched the second series on Critical & Design Thinking Skills.

(III) Certificate Courses and Structured Learning Programmes With a view to strengthening professional competencies and addressing emerging skill requirements, the Committee conducted structured learning programmes and specialised certificate courses: Conducted multiple batches of the Certificate Course on Skills Enrichment to strengthen communication, interpersonal, leadership, teamwork, ethics, branding, networking, technology and other contemporary professional skills.  Launched the Certificate Course on Business Consulting Skills to enhance consulting, advisory and strategic decision-making capabilities of members.  Launched the Certificate Course on Business Analytical Skills focusing on analytical reasoning, business analysis, data interpretation and problem-solving skills.

(IV) Digital Learning Initiatives To leverage technology-enabled learning and facilitate wider dissemination of knowledge, the Committee continued to strengthen digital learning platforms for members: Launched ICAI Skillscast, a podcast series featuring eminent industry leaders and professionals on leadership and contemporary professional skills; the inaugural episode featured Founder, IIFL Group.  Conducted Webcast Series on communication, leadership, financial advisory, emotional intelligence, practice management and other professional skills and achieved the milestone of completing 100 webcasts during the year.

(V) Workshops, Seminars and Conferences To provide interactive and experience-based learning opportunities, the Committee organised various programmes across the ICAI network: Conducted interactive workshop series focusing on communication, presentation, leadership and interpersonal effectiveness.  Organised seminars, workshops and conferences across Branches and Regional Councils for skill enhancement, knowledge sharing and professional networking.  Facilitated practical, application-oriented learning through expert-led workshops, seminars and conferences across Branches and Regional Councils.

(VI) Nationwide Skill Development Initiatives In line with its objective of promoting professional excellence and continuous learning, the Committee organised nationwide skill development initiatives: Celebrated World Youth Skills Day through 132 programmes across Branches and Regional Councils during 12–19 July 2025, with the main programme held at Ahmedabad.  Observed Skills Enrichment Month during November 2025 through workshops, webinars, masterclasses, seminars and training programmes.  Promoted continuous learning and adoption of future-ready skills through nationwide learning and outreach initiatives.

(VII) Wellness and Work-Life Balance Initiatives Recognising the importance of physical and mental well-being in professional success, the Committee continued its wellness and work-life balance initiatives: Continued Work-Life Balance programmes for members across the country, with the initiative completing 100 sessions during the year and receiving recognition from the Asia Book of Records and India Book of Records.  Conducted virtual Yoga Sessions aimed at promoting physical fitness, mental wellness and stress management.  Successfully completed 557 Yoga Sessions up to 4 February 2026.

(VIII) Publications and Digital Outreach To support continuous learning and enhance member engagement, the Committee strengthened its knowledge dissemination and digital outreach initiatives: Maintained active outreach through the dedicated PSEC website and social media platforms.  Published books on Work-Life Balance, Active Listening, Critical Thinking & Decision Making, Emotional Intelligence for Finance Professionals, Public Speaking, Negotiation Skills, Entrepreneurship Skills, Wealth Management & Financial Planning Skills, Leadership & Team Management Skills, Productivity Skills for Busy Professionals and Inclusive Workplace Practices.  Published books on Communication & Stakeholder Management Skills, Business Analytical Skills, Business Model Financial Advisory Skills and Executive Insight Skills.  Continued dissemination of learning resources, programme updates and professional development opportunities through digital platforms.

7.10 WOMEN & YOUNG MEMBERS EXCELLENCE COMMITTEE (WYMEC) The Women & Young Members Excellence Committee (WYMEC) – erstwhile Women Members Excellence Committee – is a non-standing Committee of ICAI, established to promote the fulfilment of women and young members' potential through capacity-building initiatives, skill development activities and professional and employment opportunities.

The key activities of the Committee during the period are as under:

(I) Flagship Events • 3rd CA Women Excellence Awards: The Committee organised the 3rd CA Women Excellence Awards to acknowledge and appreciate the contribution of women members who are at leadership positions or have made substantial progress in corporates, organisations or practice, or have contributed significantly to society. Awards were given in 5 distinguished categories; 131 women members were nominated. A befitting award ceremony was held at the India EXPO Centre, Greater Noida, coinciding with WOFA 2.0, graced by Shri Arjun Ram Meghwal, Hon'ble Minister of Law and Justice; Shri Raj Bhushan Chaudhary, Hon'ble Minister of State, Jal Shakti; the President, ICAI; the Vice-President, ICAI; and Chairperson and Vice-Chairman, WYMEC, along with other Council Members and the Secretary, ICAI. • CA Women Leadership Conclave – Altair: Soar. Lead. Shine: Organised on 8th November 2025 at Yogi Sabhagruha Hall, Mumbai, the Conclave was attended by more than 500 Women Chartered Accountants. Smt. Vijaya Kishore Rahatkar, Chairperson, National Commission for Women, graced the Conclave as Chief Guest. Sessions covered topics including Independent Directors: The Cornerstone of Corporate Governance; Rising Beyond: Stories of Struggles & Success; Leading with Purpose; and EmpowerHER: Opportunities and the Way Forward. • ICAI's InnoFest 2025 / YUVA – An Ascent of Excellence (International Youth Day):

Under the theme "YUVA – An Ascent of Excellence", Regional Councils and Branches organised programmes during August 2025 to commemorate International Youth Day. The Committee also organised a programme on 2nd August 2025 at NSE Auditorium, BKC, Mumbai, covering Global Opportunities, Ethics, Career Paths, Industry, Consulting, Entrepreneurship, Artificial Intelligence, Capital Markets, Investment Banking and Motivation for young members. • International Women's Day – "Rise. Lead. Transform – Beyond Boundaries": More than 100 Regional Councils/Branches conducted Women Empowerment programmes under the aegis of WYMEC during March 2026, in response to the Committee's mass communication.

(II) Webinar Series • Yashasivini – An IDOL (Inclusivity | Diversity | Outstanding | Leadership) Series: The Committee organised 94 webinars under this Wednesday Series, addressed by eminent speakers on pertinent technical and non-technical topics, providing both professional knowledge and a platform for women members to develop their verbal and communication skills. • SkillUp – Leadership | Innovation | Inclusivity | Growth Series: The Committee organised webinars covering topics such as Independent Directors: Roles, Responsibilities and Opportunities; Digital Skills and Technology Adoption for Future-Ready Professionals; Social Entrepreneurship in India; Significance of SQC and Audit Quality; Breaking Barriers – Unconventional Career Paths; and Leadership Stories, among others. • MentHer Help Desk (25th June 2025): A live session to address the concerns of women members, with expert panels covering Career Growth & Restarting Your Career, Prioritisation & Time Management, and Opportunities in the Profession.

(III) Placement and Mentorship • Placement Programme for Women CAs: Organised jointly with the Committee for Members in Industry & Business (CMI&B), addressing the need for Flexi-Time/Part-Time/Work-from-Home opportunities. 5,087 women members and 47 organisations participated, with 559 flexi and parttime vacancies offered. An Orientation Programme was also conducted to help registered women members prepare for interview panels. • Flexi & Part-Time Portal: The Women Portal for Flexi & Part-time work options enables both employers and job seekers to register, facilitating better job matching and networking. More than 800 employers and approximately 12,000 women members have registered on the portal. • ICAI Mentorship Odyssey – MentorMate: Introduced under the theme "MentorMate – Wisdom Guides Aspirations", the initiative connects young and women Chartered Accountants with experienced professionals for one-on-one mentorship, enabling mentees to sharpen skills, gain career direction and take informed professional decisions.

(IV) Residential Refresher Courses and Capacity Building Programmes • CA Women RRC – Revive & Rise (13th–15th June 2025, ICAI COE, Hyderabad): A blend of technical and non-technical sessions covering Career Pathways for CAs, Growth Mindset, AIPowered Productivity, Networking, and Celebrating Indian Culture. • RRC hosted by Raipur Branch, CIRC (28th–29th June 2025, Champaran, Chhattisgarh): Sessions on Leadership Development, Stress Management, Work-Life Balance, Time Management and the Impact of AI. • RRC hosted by Ranchi Branch, CIRC (18th–19th March 2026, Goa): Sessions on Professional Opportunities for Women CAs, GST Appeals, Personal Development & Leadership Skills, and AI Tools for CAs. • Branch Capacity Building Programmes: Programmes organised by the Kota Branch (3rd July 2025), Ludhiana Branch (27th July 2025), Surat Branch (4th October 2025, including a visit to a Lab Grown Diamond Factory), Raipur Branch (11th January 2026), Brahmapur Branch (11th January 2026), and Bhuj Branch (14th January 2026), covering topics including emerging areas for women CAs, women leadership, succession planning, forensic audit, ESG and risk advisory.

(V) Government Interaction The Committee interacted with Smt. Raksha Khadse, Hon'ble Minister of State for Youth Affairs and Sports, to collaborate in enhancing youth employability and leadership development across the country by leveraging ICAI's strong network of skilled mentors, comprehensive digital and physical infrastructure, and expertise in professional capacity building.

8. OTHER COMMITTEES/BOARDS

8.1 AUDIT COMMITTEE The Audit Committee of the Institute was established in April 2001 by the Council as a non-standing committee with a clearly defined mandate. Since 2009, it has been chaired exclusively by a Government Nominee to ensure greater independence. To further enhance its autonomy, members of the Executive Committee and the Finance Committee are not permitted to serve on the Central Audit Committee.

Additionally, five Regional Audit Committees – Southern, Western, Central, Northern and Eastern – support the Committee's work, ensuring regional representation and effective oversight.

The Committee is responsible for overseeing the financial reporting process and ensuring the accuracy, integrity and transparency of the Institute's financial disclosures. It reviews audit reports, discusses observations with the auditors, recommends corrective actions and strives to strengthen the internal control environment. The Committee remains committed to good governance and continuous improvement in audit practices.

Statutory Audit • Appointment of Statutory Auditors: In accordance with Section 18(5) of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, the statutory auditors for ICAI's Branches, Regional Councils, Decentralised Offices, Centre of Excellence and Head Office are appointed from a panel of two firms recommended by the Comptroller and Auditor General (C&AG) of India, effective from FY 2022-23. This system ensures continuity and avoids audit delays. A status report on these appointments is regularly shared with the C&AG. • Audit Reporting Framework: The statutory auditors' reporting framework is governed by Regulations 196 and 197 of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988. • Joint Statutory Auditors: The accounts of the ICAI Head Office are audited by two joint statutory auditors, bringing diverse expertise and ensuring comprehensive review and accountability.

Internal Audit • Internal Audit Coverage: A robust internal audit mechanism is in place for all major units, including branches with over a thousand members, Regional Councils, Decentralised Offices, the Centre of Excellence and the Head Office. The internal audit is conducted under the supervision of the Audit Committee, which ensures transparency and auditor independence. • Appointment through EoI: Internal auditors are appointed through a transparent process involving an open invitation for Expressions of Interest (EoI) from CA firms. Selection is based on an objective evaluation and ranking system. Audits are conducted biannually and cover both financial and compliance aspects, as per the prescribed scope and reporting format. Audit reports are reviewed by the respective Regional or Central Audit Committees for follow-up action.

Annual Accounts • Financial Statement Format: ICAI prepares its financial statements in accordance with the illustrative format for Not-for-Profit Organisations, as per the Technical Guide issued by the Institute. The format is customised to enhance relevance and clarity for stakeholders. • Approval Process: The approval of audited financial statements follows a structured and statutory process under Section 18(5A) of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, and Regulation 200 of the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988, as depicted below:

8.2 MEMBERS IN PUBLIC SERVICE COMMITTEE The Members in Public Service Committee (MPSC) (earlier named as Committee for Members in Entrepreneurship and Public Services) serves as a dedicated platform for ICAI members engaged in public service or entrepreneurship. With a focus on promoting public service and entrepreneurship as career options for CAs, the Committee undertakes various initiatives to inspire and empower members.

Many Chartered Accountants currently serve in senior roles across government departments, ministries, legislatures, the judiciary, regulatory bodies and entrepreneurial ventures, actively contributing to governance, economic growth and the nation's development. The Committee organises Residential/Interactive Meets of ICAI members in public service to solicit their suggestions on matters of national importance and continuously explores the opportunities that lie in entrepreneurship for CA members through mentorship sessions, orientation and training programmes, webinars, seminars and regional meets.

The key activities during the period are as under:

(I) Interactive and Mentorship Programmes • "Charting New Territories: From CA to Civil Services or Entrepreneurship": An interactive meet held on 9th June 2025 at New Delhi, attended by 125 members and featuring distinguished speakers including CA. Navneet Kumar Sehgal, IAS (Retd.), Chairman, Prasar Bharati; CA. K. Jegadesan, IPS, Special Commissioner of Police (Traffic); and Shri Ankur Aggarwal on careers in the Civil Services, along with CA. (Dr.) Nitin Soni on entrepreneurship. The event was graced by the President, ICAI, the Vice-President, ICAI, the Secretary, ICAI and CA. Hans Raj Chugh, CCM. • "Chartered Minds in Public Service: Inspiring the Next Generation" – Jaipur: An Awareness & Mentorship Programme held on 21st March 2026 at Jaipur, featuring an esteemed panel from diverse domains of public service – CA. Gaurav Sharma, IPS, Inspector General of Police, Kolkata; CA. Prasoon Kabra, IRS, Commissioner of Income Tax (Appeals), Hyderabad; CA.

Shivani Maheshwari, ICoAS, Joint Director, DPE, New Delhi; and CA. Gaurav Gupta, Assistant Manager, SEBI, Mumbai – followed by a panel discussion with three Chartered Accountants who cleared the UPSC Civil Services Examination 2025: CA. Sakshi Jain (AIR 37), CA. Pankaj Soni (AIR 130) and CA. Anjani Mishra (AIR 274). • "Chartered Minds in Public Service: Inspiring the Next Generation" – Udaipur: A similar Awareness & Mentorship Programme was held on 18th April 2026 at Udaipur, graced by CA.

Namit Mehta, IAS, Excise Commissioner, Rajasthan; CA. Akshay Kabra, IRS, Deputy Commissioner of Income Tax, Udaipur; CA. Tushar Saraswat, IRTS, Director, Ministry of Railways;

CA. Parvesh Sharma, Deputy Director, SFIO; and CA. Kapil Goyal, ICoAS, Assistant Director, MCA/IICA.

(II) ICAI's InnoFest 2.0 ICAI's InnoFest 2.0, themed "Empowering CA Entrepreneurs: Igniting Ideas, Building Futures", was organised on the occasion of World Entrepreneurs' Day on 20th August 2025 at New Delhi, with the participation of 211 members. The event was addressed by eminent speakers – CA. Anuruddh Tiwari, Entrepreneur; CA. Dilip Chauhan, Founder, Diviam Aahar; and CA. Robin Jha, Founder & COO, Nirog Street – who shared insights and an overview of the various schemes available to entrepreneurs, and was graced by the President, ICAI, CA. Hans Raj Chugh, CCM, the Secretary, ICAI and the Chairman, NIRC.

(III) Webinar Series The Committee organised 16 webinars during the period, broadly across two streams. For CA Civil Service aspirants, webinars covered opportunities in UKPSC, BPSC and MPSC examinations; UPSC 2026 preparation strategy; optional-subject strategy ("Why Commerce & Accountancy Works"); "From Balance Sheets to Bharat Sewa"; "From Chartered Accountant to Civil Servant: Bridging Finance and Governance"; "Unlocking Opportunities in UPSC and Beyond"; and roadmap-and-guidance sessions by experts including retired IPS/IRS officers and leading academy faculty. For aspiring entrepreneurs, webinars covered social entrepreneurship and impact-driven ventures; sharpening entrepreneurial skills; building a strong entrepreneurial mindset; entrepreneurship opportunities, roadmaps and risks;

"Empowering CAs in the Entrepreneurial Revolution"; and leveraging the Government e-Marketplace

(GeM) for entrepreneurial opportunities. Individual webinars registered between 140 and 490+ members each.

(IV) Residential Meets of CA Members in Public Service • Goa: A Residential Meet on "Chartered Accountants' Role in Realising the Vision of Viksit Bharat @ 2047" was held on 12th October 2025 at Goa, attended by 15 Members in Public Service from the judiciary, various ministries, services and administrative bodies, and graced by the President, Vice-President and Secretary, ICAI. The Meet ended on a positive note, with members expressing strong commitment to nation building. • Amritsar: A Residential Meet on the same theme was held on 3rd–4th January 2026 at Amritsar, presided over by the leadership of ICAI and bringing together eminent Chartered Accountants serving in Group A services such as the IRS, IAAS, IRAS, IRTS, IRPS and ICoAS, SEBI, regulatory bodies and tribunals. Over 86 Members in Public Service attended, and the interaction fostered a closer connect between ICAI and its members serving in governance.

(V) Publications • "Handbook for Chartered Accountants Aspiring for Civil Services" – • "SANMUKH – A Handbook of Motivation & Guidance for CAs Aspiring for Civil Services"

8.3 FINANCIAL MARKETS AND INVESTORS' AWARENESS COMMITTEE (FM&IAC) The Committee on Financial Markets and Investors' Awareness provides suggestions on Bills, Notifications, Circulars, Consultation Papers, and other documents relating to financial markets to the Government and Regulators. The Committee regularly interacts with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs, the Reserve Bank of India, the Securities and Exchange Board of India, the Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority, the Department of Non-Banking Finance Companies of RBI, and Stock Exchanges on matters relating to financial markets, the role of Chartered Accountants, and investor protection.

Partner in Nation Building To create awareness among the public and ensure investor protection, the Committee conducted 100 Investor Awareness Programmes through Programme Organising Units of ICAI, including Branches, Regional Councils, and Study Circles, to enhance investors' understanding of developments in financial markets under the aegis of the Investor Education and Protection Fund Authority (IEPFA), Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA).

Comments and Suggestions on SEBI Consultation Papers As a Partner in Nation Building, the Committee offers comments and suggestions on consultation papers published by the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI). The status of the Committee's engagement with SEBI consultation papers since 2023–24 is summarised below.

Particulars 2023–24 2024–25 2025–26 No. of Consultation Papers Published by SEBI Committee View in Agreement with SEBI Consultation Paper Committee View Submitted before SEBI Strategic MoUs and Institutional Collaborations During the year, the Committee entered into three Memoranda of Understanding (MoUs) with the Indian Venture and Alternate Capital Association (IVCA), NSE IFSC Limited (NSE IX), and India International Exchange (IFSC) Limited (India INX), strengthening institutional collaboration on matters relating to financial markets and capital market development.

Capacity Building of Members To enhance the skills of members and empower them suitably, the Committee conducts 8 Certificate Courses: Forex and Treasury Management (FXTM); Derivatives; Fundamental & Technical Analysis of Stocks including Equity Research (FATA); Financial Markets and Securities Laws (FMSL); Compliance of Capital Markets Intermediaries; Fund Management at IFSC; Investment Banking; and Non-Banking Financial Companies. During the year, the Committee successfully conducted 2 physical batches and 10 online batches of these Certificate Courses through the Digital Learning Hub, with a total of 942 members enrolling.

Conferences, Seminars and Professional Enhancement Programmes The Committee disseminated knowledge among members through 16 CPE Meetings and Virtual Webinars. It conducted 4 National Conferences and 26 Seminars, including the flagship series 'Investment ki Pathshala – CA: The Investment Guru', through the Regional Councils and Branches of the Institute, reaching members across the country with practical insights into financial markets and investment practices.

8.4 PUBLIC AND GOVERNMENT FINANCIAL MANAGEMENT COMMITTEE (PGFMC) The Committee on Public and Government Financial Management (PGFMC) continued its dedicated efforts towards strengthening public financial management, accounting reforms, financial reporting and capacity building across all tiers of government in India during the period under report. A total of 32 meetings of the Committee were held during the year. The key activities are as under:

(I) Institutional Collaboration and Capacity Building • ICAI–C&AG Memorandum of Understanding: ICAI and the Office of the Comptroller & Auditor General of India signed an MoU on 21st April 2025 to support capacity development within the C&AG institution. Pursuant to the MoU, eight residential training programmes on Accounting, Auditing and IT were organised, aimed at enhancing professional competencies and institutional effectiveness. • Local Governance Synergy Conclave 2.0: Organised on 12th January 2026 at New Delhi, the Conclave was inaugurated by Shri K. Sanjay Murthy, Hon'ble C&AG of India, and brought together senior representatives from Ministries, State Governments and audit institutions. • Certificate Course for Accountants of Panchayats and Municipal Bodies: A joint initiative of ICAI and the Office of the C&AG of India implemented through BLoAC under ICAI-ARF, the Course continued to receive an encouraging response, with over 5,200 registrations and 356 successful candidates till date. • Certificate Course on Public Finance and Government Accounting: Batches from the 33rd to the 38th were conducted in online mode for ICAI members and officials of Government Departments and Autonomous Bodies during the year, with six online examinations also successfully organised.

(II) Standard-Setting Contributions • Government Accounting Standards Advisory Board (GASAB): The Committee submitted technical comments on various drafts, suggesting detailed line-by-line changes in two draft standards, several of which were incorporated in the Indian Government Accounting Standards

(IGASs) and Guidance Notes. The Committee also participated in GASAB meetings and workshops, and contributed to deliberations relating to the revision of the National Municipal Accounts Manual (NMAM 2.0). • International Public Sector Accounting Standards Board (IPSASB): The Committee actively contributed inputs on technical documents of the IPSASB.

(III) Multilateral Collaborations The Committee strengthened collaborations with multilateral institutions such as the Asian Development Bank (ADB) and the World Bank with the objective of improving the quality of audit of Externally Aided Projects (EAPs) in India. Several training programmes, seminars, webinars and faculty development initiatives were organised for Government officials, auditors, finance officers and ICAI members.

(IV) New MoUs and Institutional Linkages The Committee strengthened institutional collaborations through MoUs with the Andhra Pradesh Police Department (Training Wing), the National Institute of Urban Affairs (NIUA) and the Mahatma Gandhi State Institute of Public Administration (MGSIPA), Punjab.

(V) Recognition Initiative – ICAI Awards for Promotion of Accounting Reforms ICAI Awards for Promotion of Accounting Reforms in Local Bodies 2025-26 were organised to recognise excellence in accounting reforms and financial reporting practices among Urban Local Bodies and Panchayati Raj Institutions.

(VI) Publications • Transparency and Accountability in Government and Public Finance: Pillars of Good Governance • Internal Audit of Panchayati Raj Institutions (PRIs) and Urban Local Bodies (ULBs) in India

8.5 EDITORIAL BOARD The Editorial Board is a specialised non-standing committee of ICAI entrusted with enriching the professional knowledge ecosystem of the Institute through its flagship publication, The Chartered Accountant. Widely regarded as one of the most respected professional journals in the country, the publication reaches more than 400,000 readers through its print and digital editions. As the voice of ICAI's thought leadership, the journal serves as a dynamic platform for disseminating contemporary developments, expert insights, research-driven perspectives, and emerging trends shaping the accounting and financial profession, reflecting the Institute's commitment to professional excellence, continuous learning, and intellectual advancement.

Under the guidance of the Editorial Board, the publication strengthens ICAI's engagement with members, students, regulators, and stakeholders, while reinforcing the Institute's brand and leadership in the global professional arena. With relevant, insightful, and future-oriented content, the Board plays a pivotal role in connecting the profession with evolving practices in finance, governance, sustainability, technology, and regulatory frameworks.

(I) Enabling India's Growth Through Professional Knowledge Through the monthly journal The Chartered Accountant, the Editorial Board has consistently contributed to India's development journey by fostering a culture of knowledge, innovation, and professional excellence within the accounting fraternity. By bringing forward contemporary issues, emerging global trends, regulatory developments, and transformative ideas, the Board equips ICAI members to navigate an evolving economic landscape with competence and vision. The journal serves as a powerful medium for promoting informed decision-making, ethical practices, and forward-looking professional thought, strengthening the role of Chartered Accountants as key contributors to economic progress, governance, and nation building.

(II) Advancing Professional Excellence During the period, The Chartered Accountant journal showcased a rich spectrum of articles addressing diverse and contemporary issues relevant to the accounting profession. Alongside its regular professional features, the journal brought out special editions focused on significant and emerging areas such as MSMEs, Leadership, GST, Technology, Sustainability, Startups, and Capital Markets, among other key technical subjects. These thematic issues were curated to enhance members' professional knowledge, broaden perspectives, and equip them to remain future-ready in a rapidly evolving environment.

(III) Member and Student Initiatives – Inclusions in the Journal During the period, 207 articles on various themes and professional topics were included in the ICAI Journal, in addition to regular features covering updates on the Institute's activities in coordination with various ICAI Committees. The July 2025 edition featured a diverse range of special write-ups and knowledge-driven articles focused on ethics, sustainability, artificial intelligence, digital transformation, leadership, wellness, financial innovation, women empowerment, and nation building, with contributions from eminent professionals, policymakers, and global leaders. The issue also included sections such as Practice to Policy, Global Footprint, In Conversation, and Lifestyle and Wellness, and carried inspirational CA Day messages from 39 eminent dignitaries, including the Prime Minister of India, Governors, Union Ministers, and Chief Ministers.

(IV) Digital Versions of The Chartered Accountant – Upgraded for Convenience • Journal in PDF/Flipbook Format: The journal continues to be hosted in PDF/Flipbook format, with digital archives available on the ICAI website from July 2002 onwards. • Journal on Digital Learning Hub: The electronic version of the journal is available as a userfriendly e-magazine on the ICAI website and the Digital Learning Hub, supporting the ICAI Learning Management System and the Institute's sustainability drive. • Journal Highlight Emailers: Highlights of every issue, along with the President's Message, are mass-emailed to all members in capsule form. • Journal on Mobile: The e-journal is accessible on iOS and Android devices through the ICAI website and the ICAI Mobile App.

8.6 INFRASTRUCTURE DEVELOPMENT COMMITTEE (IDC) The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) has continuously expanded and strengthened its infrastructure since the inauguration of its Headquarters at Indraprastha Marg, New Delhi, in 1954.

To ensure systematic planning, monitoring and standardisation of infrastructure development across its Headquarters, Regional Councils, Branches, Centres of Excellence, Decentralised Offices and other establishments, the Infrastructure Development Committee (IDC) was constituted in 2014 as a Special Purpose Non-Standing Committee under the authority of the Council. The Council also approved the Infrastructure Policy for Branches and Regional Councils in 2014, providing a comprehensive framework for infrastructure planning and development. Since then, the IDC has been responsible for assessing infrastructure requirements, formulating and reviewing policies, examining proposals for land acquisition, construction, renovation and redevelopment, monitoring project implementation, and advising the Council on all infrastructure-related matters, thereby ensuring planned, transparent and sustainable infrastructure development across the Institute.

Since the introduction of the Infrastructure Policy, the Institute has initiated the following projects:

Purchase of New Infrastructure Construction Proposals Approved Kannur, Jalandhar, Jabalpur, Goa, Gurugram, Moradabad, Pali, Agra, Gorakhpur, Karnal, Kishangarh, Latur, Patiala, Ujjain, Ratlam, Chengalpattu, Ahmedabad, Kota, Ernakulam, Himachal Pradesh, Jamnagar, Tirupati, Raipur, Bengaluru (Gifted Property), ICOE Chennai (1.19 acres), COE Guwahati, Kalyan-Dombivli, Ongole, Kozhikode, Hyderabad, Karimnagar, Sikar, and built-up space at GIFT City, Gandhinagar Ajmer, Surat, Bhopal, Rajamahendravaram, Centre of Excellence Jaipur, Bathinda, Bareilly, Jodhpur, Raipur, Kannur, Ghaziabad, Goa, Moradabad, Guntur, Agra, Gurugram, Rohini, Ratlam, Patiala, Kishangarh, Ujjain, Pali, COE Kolkata, Bengaluru (Gifted Property), Ahmedabad, Chengalpattu, Ernakulam, Rohtak, Siliguri, Patna, Jabalpur, Jalandhar, ICOE Chennai (Camp Office), Nagpur, Tirupati, Himachal Pradesh, Hubli (Auditorium), Latur, Kota, renovation of the HO Building at C-1, Sector-1, Noida, Gorakhpur, Gwalior and COE Guwahati Out of the total 186 Branches set up by ICAI so far, 106 Branches have their own premises. 13 Branches (presently functioning from rented premises) have procured land on which construction is either yet to commence or is under way, and 10 Branches (functioning from their own premises) have procured land where construction is either yet to start or is under way. 67 Branches own neither land nor a building.
