8.7 INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS COMMITTEE During the 2025-26 period, the International Affairs Committee of ICAI significantly expanded the Institute's global footprint and strengthened international professional mobility through strategic agreements, new Chapters and Representative Offices, and high-level global dialogues. The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Global Mobility and Institutional Frameworks • Global Mobility and Collaborative Agreements: ICAI maintained 8 Qualification Reciprocity Agreements and 16 Collaboration Agreements worldwide to facilitate professional mobility and technical cooperation. • Benchmarking Success: An independent study by ECCTIS evaluated ICAI qualifications, resulting in the Chartered Accountant designation being deemed equivalent to a Master's degree in frameworks across the UK, US, Canada, Australia, UAE and Europe.

(II) Global Footprint: New Chapters and Offices • United States Expansion: ICAI launched its 53rd Chapter in Los Angeles (1st June 2025) and 54th Chapter in Atlanta (4th June 2025). • Representative Offices (ROs): Five new ROs were inaugurated virtually in Kansas, Louisiana, Minnesota, Tennessee and San Diego through the "Unite in America" initiative (9th February 2025). • The Institute's network grew to 54 Chapters and 31 Representative Offices spanning 47 countries and 85 cities.

(III) International Strategic Engagements • ICAI is affiliated with 15 international accounting bodies/forums to strengthen its footprint globally. • At various IFAC Connect events (Mexico City, Riyadh and Jakarta), ICAI presented on the impact of Artificial Intelligence on audit and assurance, emphasising the need for technological literacy and independent oversight of AI systems. • ICAI presented to the IESBA and the IAASB on auditing challenges for less complex entities and sustainability ethics. • ICAI representatives regularly attended events organised by ICAI Chapters globally, addressed the fraternity and discussed the issues being faced by the Chapters. • The Premier of the Cayman Islands and Minister for Financial Services & Commerce, Hon'ble André M. Ebanks, visited ICAI on 2nd December 2025 to discuss areas of mutual interest.

(IV) World Forum of Accountants (WOFA 2.0) ICAI organised the World Forum of Accountants (WOFA 2.0) on the theme "Nation Building to Global Collaboration: Strengthening Trust, Enabling Growth" from 30th January 2026 to 1st February 2026 at the India Expo Centre & Mart, Greater Noida. The event hosted over 10,000 participants from over 25 countries, with over 800 delegates attending virtually, and featured 250+ speakers, along with the participation of 100 corporate sponsors and 30 CA firms.

WOFA 2.0 was inaugurated on 30th January 2026 in the august presence of CA. Piyush Goyal, Hon'ble Minister of Commerce and Industry, as Chief Guest, and Shri Kumar Mangalam Birla, Chairman, Aditya Birla Group, as Guest of Honour. The Forum featured focused concurrent tracks designed to enable indepth engagement across key domains, including the Technology Track (Artificial Intelligence & Digital Finance), Global Edge Track (Global Capability Centres & Outsourcing), Marketsphere Track (Capital Market), Aatmanirbhar Bharat Track (MSME & Start-up), Eco Rise Track (Sustainability) and the Governance & Assurance Track.

On the sidelines of WOFA, ICAI hosted the Strategy Meeting of the SAFA Board and SAFA Committee Meetings. The ICAI Awards and the ICAI Annual Day were also organised on the sidelines of WOFA

2.0, and the ICAI Best Chapter Overseas Awards were conferred at the ICAI Annual Day.

8.8 MANAGEMENT COMMITTEE Management Committee, constituted in 2015 as non-standing Committee of the Council, is mandated to consider matters pertaining to formation of Branches, setting up of Chapters abroad, MoUs/ MRAs with national/ international bodies, appointment of central auditors of ICAI, matters referred by the Central Government and other regulatory bodies, proposals for amendments in the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, Rules and Regulations framed thereunder, Regional Councils and Branches matters, Members/CA firms/LLPs/mergers/demergers/networking related matters and proposals received from other committees/departments of the Institute having administrative and policy implications and making its recommendations to the Council wherever required.

8.9 PUBLIC RELATIONS COMMITTEE (PRC) The Public Relations Committee has been working to develop, strengthen and enhance the image of the ICAI as a premier accountancy body and the sole regulatory authority for the profession of Chartered Accountancy in India & globally through various ways and means, as considered appropriate within framework of the CA Act. The Committee also endeavours to foster strong relationships with the media and other stakeholders, in coordination with various Committees of ICAI, to facilitate effective communication, disseminate information and enhance the visibility of the Institute. Recognising the media as a strategic platform for communicating with the public, policymakers and key stakeholders, ICAI has proactively leveraged it to effectively convey its views and decisions. The Public Relations Committee has not only expanded ICAI's media footprint in terms of reach and volume but has also built enduring relationships with key national media.

The Public Relations Committee undertook extensive communication, media engagement, branding and outreach initiatives during the period to enhance the visibility of ICAI's initiatives and strengthen public awareness of the accounting profession.

The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Celebration of the 11th International Day of Yoga 2025 ICAI celebrated the 11th International Day of Yoga in a grand manner on 21st June 2025 through its wide network of 5 Regional Councils, 179 Branches, 54 Overseas Chapters and 31 Representative Offices. A grand event was organised at Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, under the theme "Yoga for One Earth, One Health", with the enthusiastic participation of over 2,000 Chartered Accountants and students. The programme was inaugurated by Shri Vijender Gupta, Hon'ble Speaker, Delhi Legislative Assembly; Shri Raja Iqbal Singh, Hon'ble Mayor, MCD, Delhi; Shri Sandeep Kapoor, Hon'ble Councillor & Ex-Chairman, Standing Committee; and Dr. Sandeep Marwah, Chancellor, AAFT University of Media & Arts. It was streamed live across 5 Regional Councils and 179 Branches, ensuring wide outreach across ICAI's 14.5 lakh+ members and students. Over 5 Regional Offices, 130 Branches and various Overseas Chapters actively participated. These initiatives together earned ICAI recognition from the Asia Book of Records for the maximum number of branches of a single organisation conducting Yoga simultaneously.

A five-day countdown campaign under the theme "Healthy CAs, Wealthy Economy", supported by competitions, digital outreach, uniform branding and extensive pre- and post-event media publicity, ensured wide awareness, engagement and sustained visibility.

(II) ICAI's Visit to Pahalgam In a historic gesture on the occasion of the 79th Independence Day, ICAI convened its 445th Council Meeting in Pahalgam from 12th–14th August 2025, becoming the first national institution to host such a high-level meeting in the Valley after the tragic Baisaran incident of 22nd April 2025. ICAI's visit to Pahalgam was a powerful reaffirmation that the nation stands united, resolute and unwavering in its belief that Kashmir is and will always remain an integral part of India. Amplifying this message, the PRC organised a Press Meet and issued a Press Release that was covered widely across India.

(III) Media Engagement and Press Coverage • Press Conferences: ICAI organised over 16 press conferences across New Delhi and various cities nationwide, serving as an effective platform to highlight the Institute's key initiatives, policy perspectives, major events, collaborations and significant achievements, resulting in extensive media visibility and widespread coverage. • Media Interactions and Major Stories: Extensive one-on-one media engagement was undertaken through interviews, interactions, authored articles, expert quotes and Q&A sessions across The Economic Times, Mint, The Hindu BusinessLine, Financial Express, Times of India, Business Standard, Indian Express, Deccan Herald, NDTV, Zee Business, CNBC TV18, ET Now, Aaj Tak, Dainik Bhaskar, Dainik Jagran, Hindustan, Moneycontrol.com, Print, PTI, UNI, IANS, Informist and many more. • Television Coverage: Special interviews and live shows across leading television channels such as ET Now Swadesh, NDTV Profit, Zee Business, CNBC TV18, CNBC Awaaz, ET Now and Doordarshan ensured wide national reach and electronic media presence. • Podcasts: ICAI leveraged the growing popularity of podcasts for deeper stakeholder engagement; the podcast series garnered lakhs of views across digital platforms, significantly enhancing the Institute's thought leadership visibility among a wider and younger audience. • Press Releases: 46 Press Releases sharing updates on various important announcements were issued during the period, garnering 2,000+ coverages.

(IV) ICAI Media Meet The Committee organised a Media Workshop in Goa on 11th–12th October 2025. The two-day workshop served as a dynamic platform for interaction between ICAI leadership and senior journalists on various technical matters. The workshop received enthusiastic participation from 23 senior professionals and resulted in 150+ positive coverages across print, electronic and online media.

(V) ICAI Stands with the Nation Following Operation Sindoor, the President, ICAI, addressed the CA fraternity on 9th May 2025, calling on members and students to stand in solidarity with the Armed Forces and stay vigilant, and encouraging them to organise blood donation drives, set up shelter camps for displaced civilians, provide ambulance services or extend financial assistance. Over 50,000 members and students joined the live address, which subsequently garnered over 2 million impressions across various social media platforms.

(VI) Publicity and Promotion of Major Events The Committee undertook extensive publicity and promotional activities for ICAI's major events and initiatives, ensuring wide visibility and branding across print, electronic, digital, radio, outdoor and social media platforms. Key events supported through integrated communication campaigns included Accounting Standards Day, the 11th International Day of Yoga, the 77th Chartered Accountants' Day Celebrations, ICAI Convocation Ceremonies, the World Forum of Accountants (WOFA 2.0), RESOLVE 2025, the Digital Transformation Finance Summit (DxFS) 2025 and the AI Innovation Summit (AIS 2025). These efforts generated extensive media coverage, enhanced public awareness and strengthened ICAI's brand visibility. • VISHWASNIYA AV: An engaging audio-visual emphasising the key priority areas was developed and played at all events throughout the year. • Cooperative Chintan Shivir AV: A special AV highlighting ICAI's contribution towards the cooperative sector was developed and played during the ICAI Cooperative Chintan Shivir. • WOFA Promotional AVs and Creatives: Various promotional AVs and creatives publicising WOFA

2.0 were developed to enhance visibility and awareness about the event. • Advertorials publicising ICAI's branding initiatives were published in the Hindustan Times (17th December 2025), The Economic Times (19th December 2025) and the Hindu Business Line (23rd December 2025). • To promote the CA Course, a quarter-page advertisement was developed and published in leading national and regional publications and magazines across the country in multiple languages.

Various short videos publicising the CA Course were also posted on social media platforms.

8.10 RESEARCH COMMITTEE Established in 1955, the Research Committee is one of the oldest technical committees of ICAI, committed to promoting a robust research culture to enhance professional services. Its primary objective is to undertake research and strengthen institutional capacity in accounting and allied disciplines for the benefit of the profession and the nation. The key activities of the Committee during the period under report are as under:

(I) Research Projects Completed The following research projects were completed during the year: • Accounting for Digital Assets • An Exploratory Analysis of Pradhan Mantri MUDRA Yojana (PMMY) in Fostering Growth of Micro Enterprises • Corporate Reporting Practices of Listed Companies in India • Revitalizing India's Legal Landscape: Addressing Obsolete Economic and Commercial Laws for a Viksit Bharat @ 2047 • Reprioritizing Environmental Claims Under the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code • Study Material on Public Procurement • Liquidation Accounting • REITs and Their Emerging Significance in India: A Regulatory, Market and Professional Perspective • Mahua Flowers: Regulatory, Economic and Social Opportunities

(II) Research Projects – Work in Progress • The Rise of Green Investment: Investors' Intention and the Mediating Role of Theory of Planned Behavior on Sustainable Investment Propensity. • Socio-Economic Impact of Digital Finance on Rural Women in India: An Analysis of the Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (PMJDY) and Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT) Schemes. • Exploring the Intellectual Property Landscape of Indian Healthcare and Life Science Start-ups. • The Start-up Ecosystem in Northeast India: A Critical Analysis of its Dynamics, Challenges and Potential as a Catalyst for Sustainable Development and Inclusive Economic Transformation. • Sustainable Financial Reporting in Indian Smart Cities: An Evaluation of Disclosure Practices against Qualitative Characteristics for Municipal Bond Issuance and Urban Challenge Fund Access. • Demystifying the Natural Resources–Capital Flight Nexus: Insights on Money Laundering in India. • Study of Current Socio-Economic Affairs in Sehore District of Madhya Pradesh and the Way Forward.

(III) ICAI Awards for Excellence in Financial Reporting 2024-25 The Research Committee has been organising the ICAI Awards for Excellence in Financial Reporting since 1958 to recognise excellence in the preparation and presentation of financial information. For the year 2024-25, a total of 19 awards – comprising 9 Gold Shields and 10 Silver Shields – were presented on 31st January 2026 during WOFA 2.0.

(IV) ICAI International Research Award 2025 Since 2020, the Committee has been organising the "ICAI International Research Awards" to recognise and honour exemplary research work in Accounting, Auditing, Economics, Finance and Taxation. The Award Ceremony was organised on 14th October 2025 in Goa, during which 16 research papers were recognised with awards, comprising 5 Gold Shields, 6 Silver Shields and 5 Bronze Shields presented to the researchers.

(V) Research Schemes • ICAI Doctoral Scholarship Scheme 2025: This scheme provides monthly scholarship support to eligible candidates undertaking research work, with details available on the ICAI website. • ICAI Research Projects Scheme 2025: This scheme encourages researchers to undertake insightful studies on contemporary and emerging issues, with details available on the ICAI website. • ICAI Short-Term Research Studies Scheme 2025: This scheme provides a platform for sharing new ideas, innovative research findings and thought-provoking insights on various contemporary topics.

(VI) Memorandum of Understanding The Committee successfully signed an MoU with Chanakya National Law University, Patna to enhance academic and research capabilities in various areas.

(VII) Capacity Building Initiatives The Research Committee organises webinars, seminars, Residential Refresher Courses and virtual live panel discussions on contemporary topics, with the objective of promoting continuous learning and professional development, fostering innovative thinking and creating new research opportunities.

8.11 STRATEGY, PERSPECTIVE PLANNING AND MONITORING COMMITTEE (SPP&MC) The Strategy Perspective Planning and Monitoring Committee is a non-standing committee of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) serves a critical role in shaping long-term ICAI Vision and objectives. The primary purpose of this committee is to engage in strategic planning, ensuring that the ICAI develops and implements effective strategies to meet its goal. The committee is committed to effective risk management, identifying potential challenges, and adapting strategies to navigate a dynamic business environment. By analysing various perspective, trends and factors, the committee aim to provide a comprehensive outlook for the organization's future. Additionally, it plays a critical role in monitoring the execution of strategic initiatives, assessing their impact, and making adjustments as necessary.

Preparation of Research Report ICAI Vision 2049 Collaboration with IIMA In pursuit of a forward-looking strategy for the Institute, the ICAI initiated the development of ICAI Vision 2049 through a collaborative engagement with the Indian Institute of Management Ahmedabad

(IIMA). This collaboration aimed to outline a comprehensive roadmap that aligns the Institute's longterm goals with emerging global, technological, and regulatory trends and Research Report has been submitted by IIM Ahmedabad.

8.12 ICAI CORPORATE MITRA COMMITTEE The ICAI Corporate Mitra Committee has been constituted for the Corporate Mitra Project of the Government of India to undertake coordination, outreach, awareness creation and other activities for its effective implementation, ensure access to training through the Regions, Branches, Chapters and offices of ICAI, including periodic continuing training, capacity building and skill enrichment for Corporate Mitra, enlist human, logistic and other resources required for the initiative, and constitute working groups or invite experts, academicians and industry leaders for its implementation, while contributing to the formulation of the overall scheme for the Project. The ICAI Corporate Mitra Committee continued its efforts towards supporting Government initiatives aimed at enhancing employability, skill development and ease of doing business. During the year, the Committee actively engaged with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) in connection with the Prime Minister Internship Scheme and the proposed Corporate Mitra Scheme.

(I) Prime Minister Internship Scheme (PMIS) The Prime Minister Internship Scheme (PMIS), launched by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs under the Union Budget 2024-25, aims to provide structured, paid and industry-linked internship opportunities to youth across India. Recognising the importance of experiential learning and practical training, ICAI extended its support to the initiative and was invited by MCA to participate in Pilot Phase III of the Scheme.

Key features of the Scheme include: • Internship allowance of ₹9,000 per month, comprising ₹900 by the participating firm and ₹8,100 by MCA. • One-time financial assistance of ₹6,000 by MCA. • Internship duration of 9 months. • Eligibility for graduates aged between 18 and 25 years, subject to prescribed criteria. • Implementation through a dedicated single-window portal administered by MCA.

The Committee continued its efforts to support wider outreach and participation, particularly in Tier-II and Tier-III cities, thereby contributing towards the development of industry-ready professionals.

(II) Corporate Mitra Scheme The Corporate Mitra Scheme, announced by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs in the Union Budget 202627, seeks to strengthen the MSME ecosystem by creating a pool of trained and certified paraprofessionals capable of assisting enterprises in regulatory, financial and governance matters.

Key features of the Scheme include:  The Scheme envisages six months of structured online academic learning followed by six months of on-the-job training.  ICAI developed the module on Accounting, Taxation, Banking and Financial Management and submitted the same to MCA for consideration.  The Scheme is proposed to commence with 2,000 participants, including 200 participants from the North-Eastern Region.  Successful candidates will be certified as "Corporate Mitra" upon completion of assessments and on-the-job training and will be equipped to function as MSME compliance facilitators.  Academic content is proposed to be made available in multiple Indian languages to promote wider accessibility and inclusiveness.

The Committee contributed towards the development of the academic framework of the Scheme. The module on Accounting, Taxation, Banking and Financial Management was developed by the subject faculties of the Board of Studies (Academic), ICAI and submitted to MCA for consideration. The Scheme is expected to strengthen ease of doing business, support MSME compliance requirements and create structured employment opportunities for youth across the country.

8.13 CENTRAL COUNCIL LIBRARY The Central Council Library of the Institute caters to the information requirements of its stakeholders.

Its aim is to provide a comprehensive and up-to-date collection of primary and secondary print and non-print material to present and prospective members, students, research scholars and officials of ICAI. The Library has assumed greater responsibilities in serving Committees and Departments by imparting knowledge and valuable information through books, e-books, journals, magazines, online databases, print newspapers as well as e-newspapers, and is responsible for updating and providing the journals and books required for the work of various Committees.

The Central Council Library is fully computerised and operates through the Liberty library management software. Library material, including the database of books, journals and articles, can be searched subject-wise, author-wise, title-wise, topic-wise, keyword-wise and publisher-wise. These records are available through the online search OPAC (Liberty) under "Central Council Library" on the ICAI website (www.icai.org).

Under the column "Accountants' Browser", an index of articles relevant to the accounting profession is published every month in the journal 'The Chartered Accountant'. The "Accountants' Browser" is an index of important professional articles, with archives of past articles. Reference services are also provided by the Library to researchers, scholars, faculties, students and members.

A number of online databases have been acquired by the Library, the details of which are available on www.icai.org under "Central Council Library". These online knowledge databases have been installed in the Central Council Library and can be accessed in-house to facilitate the search for required material by members, faculties and research scholars. Several e-journals have also been subscribed to by the Library. The details of the new resources added in the Central Council Library at the Head Office and the Noida Library during the year (1st April 2025 to 31st March 2026) are as under:

8.14 RIGHT TO INFORMATION ACT, 2005 The basic objective of the Right to Information Act, 2005 is to empower citizens and promote transparency and accountability in the working of every public authority. The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), a statutory body set up by the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, is a public authority as envisaged under Section 2(h) of the RTI Act, 2005. In compliance with the provisions of the RTI Act, 2005 and the directions of the Central Information Commission, officers of the Institute have been designated as the Central Public Information Officer (CPIO), Central Assistant Public Information Officer, and First Appellate Authority (FAA).

A large number of RTI applications are received by the RTI Cell, ICAI from students, members, other stakeholders and citizens, and are responded to in a timely manner. In terms of Section 4(1)(b) of the RTI Act, 2005, all necessary disclosures have been made by hosting them on the Institute's website (www.icai.org), which are updated from time to time.

During the period 2025-26, a total of 2,29,758 applications were received. The large majority comprise requests for certified copies of answer sheets for the CA Foundation, Intermediate and Final examinations. In addition, a significant number of RTI applications pertaining to Disciplinary functions, HRD, Examinations and Administration were also received. The category-wise break-up is as under:

Period Foundation Intermediate Final Others Total June to December 20,292 73,717 58,815 1,53,398 January to March 10,007 37,581 28,560 76,360 Total 2025-26 30,299 1,11,298 87,375 2,29,758 Further, 11 hearings of the Central Information Commission were attended, and a significant number of orders of the First Appellate Authority were responded to during the year.

9. DIRECTORATES

9.1 BENEFICIARY SCHEMES DIRECTORATE The Beneficiary Schemes Directorate is responsible for facilitating beneficiary products and services for the Members and Students of ICAI and other beneficiaries, if any; assessing stakeholder requirements; exploring strategic tie-ups; reviewing existing schemes; evaluating proposals; drafting and vetting documentation for implementation of beneficiary schemes; establishing governance and service frameworks; creating awareness through communication and outreach initiatives; developing technology-enabled platforms to facilitate access to beneficiary schemes; promoting well-being, work life balance and environmentally sustainable habits and practices; implementing a centralized framework for the promotion and utilization of beneficiary schemes across the ICAI network; and promoting the adoption of Practice Management Software (PMS).

The Beneficiary Schemes Directorate continues its efforts towards enhancing member welfare by providing a comprehensive ecosystem of benefits across insurance, healthcare, banking, technology, professional tools and value-added services through its dedicated platform, bs.icai.org.

(I) Health, Wellness and Fitness Initiatives With a focus on promoting physical fitness, preventive healthcare and holistic well-being among Members and Students, the Directorate undertook various wellness initiatives during the year: Supported ICAI's participation in World Bicycle Day 2026 under the Fit India Movement and encouraged Members, Students, Branches and Regional Councils to participate in the nationwide "Sundays on Cycle" campaign.  Conducted more than 160 Yoga Programmes across Branches and Regional Councils jointly with the Public Relations Committee in connection with International Yoga Day 2026.  Organised 45 live webcasts up to 30 June 2026 covering health, wellness, work-life balance, leadership, financial planning and professional development for Members.

(II) Member Facilitation and Professional Development Initiatives To support members in their professional and career aspirations, the Directorate facilitated various learning and development opportunities: Disseminated information relating to the Samkalp IAS Facilitation Initiative for Chartered Accountant Members aspiring to pursue Civil Services.  Continued to provide members access to learning, mentoring and knowledge-sharing opportunities through webinars and professional outreach initiatives.

(III) Digital Platform for Member Benefits To provide seamless access to member services and benefits, the Directorate strengthened its dedicated digital platform: Continued to provide integrated access to insurance, healthcare, banking, technology solutions and value-added services through the Beneficiary Schemes Portal (bs.icai.org), enabling Members and Students to access various schemes and benefits through a unified digital platform.

(IV) Banking and Financial Services The Directorate continued to facilitate banking and financial solutions for Members through collaborations with leading financial institutions: Banking facilities through AU Small Finance Bank, including Current Account, Savings Account and Credit Card facilities.  Term Loan and Dropline Overdraft facilities through HDFC Bank.  Credit Card facilities through Bank of Baroda.  Online PSB Loan Facility for streamlined access to credit.

(V) Insurance and Risk Protection Services The Directorate continued to provide a range of insurance solutions aimed at strengthening financial security and risk protection for Members and their families: HDFC Group Poorn Suraksha Term Insurance Scheme.  LIC Term Insurance Scheme.  Medical Insurance Scheme through New India Assurance.  Professional Indemnity Insurance Scheme.  Motor Vehicle Insurance Scheme.  Personal Accident Insurance Policy.  Householder Insurance Scheme.  Office Protection Shield Scheme.

(VI) Healthcare Services Recognising the importance of preventive healthcare and access to quality medical services, the Directorate facilitated healthcare benefits through reputed healthcare partners: Healthcare and diagnostic services through Apollo Diagnostics and Apollo ProHealth.  Healthcare facilities through Fortis Healthcare, Dr. Lal PathLabs, Max Healthcare Services and Medanta.  Health insurance and wellness services through NIA Health Insurance, Trueworth Wellness and Tata 1mg.

(VII) Technology, Software and Professional Tools To support digital transformation and improve professional efficiency, the Directorate facilitated access to a wide range of technology solutions and professional tools: Practice Management, Audit and Compliance Solutions including PMS, AnyAudit, NESL IU Audit, Firmway, Click2Confirm and Simplify Practice Management Software.  Accounting, Taxation and GST Solutions including Tally, GST Software KDK, TDS Software KDK, CA-TDSMAN, Speqta GST, AccuReco, Suvit and Wraptax.  Business Productivity and Automation Solutions including Zoho, Easy Office, FYNONE, My Bill Book, Bill Mantra and Razorpay.  Artificial Intelligence, Legal Research and Analytics Tools including VIDUR, Assure AI, Probe42, CLAonline and GovEVA.  Digital Infrastructure and Security Solutions including Bharath Cloud, My Meet, Quick Heal Antivirus and Screenza.

(VIII) Strategic Tie-ups and Value-added Services The Directorate expanded the basket of member benefits through strategic collaborations with reputed organisations: Knowledge and research support through Commercial Publication, CLAonline and CA/CS Proedge.  Technology and digital solutions through Samsung, Google Pixel (Shivaami), GovEVA, VIDUR and AccuReco.  Banking and financial service tie-ups with AU Small Finance Bank, HDFC Bank and Bank of Baroda.  Healthcare partnerships with Apollo Diagnostics and other leading healthcare and diagnostic service providers.  Strengthened the member benefit ecosystem through strategic collaborations across banking, healthcare, technology, professional services and knowledge solutions, providing Members and Students with enhanced access to value-added products and services.

9.2 CENTRE OF EXCELLENEC DIRECTORATE (CoED) The Centre of Excellence Directorate (CoED) of ICAI functions as a key knowledge and thought leadership arm of the Institute, with a focus on capacity building, research, and policy-oriented initiatives. Guided by its vision to strengthen the professional competencies of members and contribute meaningfully to national economic priorities, the Directorate has undertaken several initiatives integrating advanced learning, structured knowledge dissemination and evidence-based policy advocacy. Through its research initiatives, flagship capacity-building programmes and knowledge series webcasts, CoED has further strengthened ICAI's position as a credible and trusted knowledge partner for policymakers and other stakeholders and remains committed to expanding collaborative networks and developing forward-looking insights for sustainable economic development.

Major Achievements/Milestones of the Directorate for the year 2025-26 • Knowledge Series Live Webcasts with Thought Leaders: The Directorate organised 3 live webcasts with eminent thought leaders during the year, on the topics "Jamsetji Tata's Blueprint for Corporate Success", "Economic and Fiscal Implications of the Proposed 'One Nation, One Election' Initiative", and "State Capacity, Fiscal Federalism and the Future of Public Finances in India". • Research & Policy Advocacy Initiatives: The Directorate continues to reinforce ICAI's position as a thought leader in policy discourse through well-researched and high-impact reports addressing emerging economic priorities and governance challenges, providing actionable inputs for policymakers while enabling members to gain deeper insights into the evolving business and regulatory environment. • Major Research Achievements: The Directorate completed five significant research studies during the year, each contributing meaningfully to national policy discourse and development – o From Policy to Profit: Amplifying MSME Revenues in J&K through Targeted Government Initiatives o Synchronising Democracy: The Economics of One Nation, One Election o Hope in the Hills: Strengthening MSMEs in J&K through Comparative Policy Innovation o Rise in Family Business Offices in Singapore o Effective Logistics and Supply Chain Management for the Northeastern Region's Flower Industry The report "From Policy to Profit" was handed over to Hon'ble Chief Minister of Jammu & Kashmir, Shri Omar Abdullah, during the 445th Council Meeting held at Pahalgam, J&K, on 12th August

2025. • Advanced Flagship Programs: Batch 2 of the Advanced Flagship Program "Empowering Trusted Advisors – Unlocking the Potential of Family Enterprises" was conducted at the ICAI Centre of Excellence, Hyderabad, continuing the Directorate's commitment to equipping members with deep domain expertise and practical skills for evolving business environments. • Ongoing Research Projects: Intensive research work is underway on five further projects, namely an assessment of the accounting, auditing and legal framework of Panchayati Raj Institutions in Andhra Pradesh; Ten Years of the Women Director Mandate – Insights for Strengthening Gender-Inclusive Governance; the strategic role of MSMEs in nation-building and their contribution to Viksit Bharat 2047; and fostering India-Sri Lanka and India-Cambodia economic and sectoral collaboration. • Enhancing Research Facilities: The Directorate continues to strengthen its research infrastructure by investing in advanced research software and access to renowned journal databases such as Prime Database, Prowess IQ, Prowess Dx, STATA and EBSCO, equipping members and researchers with the tools required for high-quality academic and professional research aligned with global standards.

9.3 CONTINUING PROFESSIONAL EDUCATION DIRECTORATE The Continuing Professional Education Committee (CPE) (earlier named as Continuing Professional Education Committee) is a non-standing Committee of the Council of the ICAI entrusted with the task of setting strategic directions and overseeing CPE activities of POUs, members, etc. under the directions of the Council. During the year under review, the erstwhile Continuing Professional Education Committee (CPEC) was restructured and redesignated as the "CPE Directorate (CPED)" of ICAI for the Council Year 2026-27. The Directorate has been entrusted with strengthening the Continuing Professional Education framework of the Institute through structured learning, policy reforms, digital transformation and enhanced outreach initiatives aimed at ensuring the continuous professional development of members.

The CPE Directorate continued to promote lifelong learning and professional excellence by organising and facilitating high-quality educational programmes across the country and abroad through its extensive network of Programme Organising Units (POUs).During the period under review, the Directorate held seven meetings, including meetings of the erstwhile CPEC and the newly constituted CPED, at New Delhi and Jaipur for the deliberation and implementation of key policy initiatives and reforms.

(I) Expansion of the CPE Network One of the major achievements of the Directorate was the expansion of the CPE network in remote and underserved regions. A comprehensive exercise undertaken during the year identified 851 prospective locations, comprising 260 potential Study Chapters and 591 Study Groups at the district and tehsil levels. Consequent to this initiative, 17 new CPE POUs, including Study Circles, Study Chapters and Study Groups, were established, significantly enhancing the geographical reach and accessibility of professional education initiatives.

(II) Reform-Oriented Initiatives The CPE Directorate undertook several reform-oriented initiatives approved by the Council to strengthen the CPE framework and improve operational efficiency. These included: (a) revision in the minimum fee structure for CPE programmes; (b) enhancement in the fixed asset purchase limits for Study Chapters; (c) rationalisation of seminar limits for Central Committees; (d) reduction in the minimum duration of technical sessions for flagship events; (e) revision in the CPE hours requirements effective from Calendar Year 2026; and (f) simplification of exemption norms for members. Further, the Directorate facilitated voluntary contributions towards the Chartered Accountants' Benevolent Fund

(CABF) through the new DigiCA CPE Portal and reviewed the grant guidelines for Study Chapters to ensure improved accountability and the regular conduct of programmes.

(III) DigiCA CPE Portal In order to modernise and streamline the CPE ecosystem, the Directorate progressed significantly towards the implementation of the DigiCA CPE Portal, a comprehensive digital platform aimed at ensuring automation, transparency, real-time attendance tracking, efficient programme approvals and a seamless experience for members and Programme Organising Units. The portal is expected to significantly improve programme management, monitoring, compliance and the accessibility of CPE activities.

(IV) Knowledge Dissemination and Programmes • The Directorate released the Annual CPE Calendar for 2025-26 and published the 23rd Edition of the "CPE Bulletin", highlighting major initiatives, developments and policy decisions undertaken during the year. • The Directorate organised 136 programmes directly for members, including National Conferences, Residential Refresher Courses, Seminars, Workshops and Webinars, covering diversified and contemporary professional subjects. • Through its strong network of 709 CPE Programme Organising Units across India and abroad, the Directorate facilitated large-scale professional learning activities. During the period from 1st April 2025 to 30th April 2026, a total of 9,127 physical and virtual CPE events were organised, with the participation of 8,59,447 members. These programmes resulted in the grant of 49,157 CPE hours and the generation of 50,79,228 Structured CPE hours. • The Directorate also facilitated the organisation of several national-level educational initiatives by various POUs and Central Committees, including 1,228 live webcasts/webinars, 652 Certificate Course batches, 72 Post-Qualification Course batches, foreign language learning programmes in Japanese, Spanish and French, and Pre-registration Education Course batches conducted by ICAI RVO and IIIPI.

(V) Supporting Government Endeavours through Topic-specific CPE Programmes ICAI supports various Government endeavours through its strong network of 709 CPE POUs by organising topic-specific CPE programmes across the country in both physical and virtual modes, as under:

9.4 DIGITAL RE-ENGINEERING AND TRANSFORMATION DIRECTORATE (DRTD) The Digital Re-Engineering and Transformation Directorate (DRTD) continued to drive ICAI's digital transformation agenda during the year, strengthening digital governance, automation and online service delivery for members, students and internal stakeholders. The key initiatives undertaken during the period under report are as under:

(I) Enterprise Document Management System (DMS) Implementation across Regional Offices ICAI successfully completed a large-scale Document Management System (DMS) project for all five Regional Offices on a war footing, resulting in the digitisation and archival of over 8 crore documents.

The initiative has significantly strengthened centralised record management, secure document retrieval, preservation of legacy records, operational efficiency and digital governance across the Institute. The project marks a major milestone towards ICAI's vision of a paperless, technology-driven and efficiently managed institutional ecosystem.

(II) Digital Credentials on DigiLocker ICAI enabled the issuance of Membership Identity Cards and Examination Marksheets through DigiLocker, strengthening secure digital access and paperless service delivery for members and students. The initiative facilitates anytime, anywhere availability of authenticated academic and professional credentials, aligned with the Government of India's Digital India vision and an enhanced digital governance framework.

(III) Common Website Layout and Standardised Digital Identity for ICAI Branches through Centralised CMS ICAI continued its digital transformation initiative by implementing a centralised Content Management System (CMS) for Branch websites, enabling a common layout, a standardised design framework and a unified digital identity across the ICAI network. As on 30th April 2026, applications from 48 Branches were received, out of which 36 Branch websites were successfully developed and shared. The initiative ensures consistency in branding, improved accessibility, enhanced security, ease of content management and compliance with ICAI web policies. To support Branches in strengthening their digital presence, ICAI Headquarters bore the major cost towards website development, AWS cloud hosting and onboarding support, thereby enabling Branches to establish a professional and cost-effective digital ecosystem.

(IV) Launch of the ICAI Snapshots Portal for Centralised Digital Record Management ICAI launched the "Snapshots" Portal as a centralised digital repository and document management platform for Committees, Boards, Departments, Regional Councils, Branches and various ICAI portals/applications. The portal has been designed to facilitate the secure storage, systematic organisation, easy retrieval and efficient sharing of official documents, reports, circulars, meeting records, selected photographs and other institutional data. The platform also serves as a dedicated repository for MoU/MRA agreements executed with Governments, trade organisations, universities, professional bodies and technology partners, along with the list of ICAI portals and applications and Non-Disclosure Agreements (NDAs) with vendors. The initiative aims to strengthen digital governance, preserve institutional memory, improve operational efficiency and promote a paperless working environment across ICAI, while enhancing collaboration, accessibility, secure archival and the longterm preservation of important organisational records, strategic partnerships and key milestones of the Institute.

(V) ENGAGE Platform – An Integrated Centralised Mass Communication Mechanism for ICAI's Regional Councils and Branches The DRT Directorate developed 'ENGAGE', a centralised communication platform enabling ICAI Regional Councils and Branches to send mass emails and SMS to members and students within their jurisdiction in a secure and efficient manner. The platform has been rolled out to all Regional Councils and Branches after a round of demonstrations and training for the officers and staff of all five Regional Councils and Branches, in coordination with the RBA Directorate of ICAI. As on 30th April 2026, ENGAGE login credentials have been shared with all 186 Branches of ICAI, out of which 55 Branches are using the mass e-mail services and 26 Branches are using the SMS services through the platform. The DRTD Secretariat is organising periodic training sessions on the ENGAGE platform for all Regional Councils and Branches.

(VI) ICAI Digital Learning Hub The ICAI Digital Learning Hub is an initiative aimed at leveraging technology to provide enhanced learning opportunities and resources to members, students and other stakeholders. By centralising educational materials and resources in a digital format, the Hub facilitates easy access to a wide range of learning content, including e-books, webinars, courses and interactive modules, promoting continuous professional development and ensuring that users can access high-quality educational content anytime and anywhere. The Hub has evolved over the years, with various customisations and alignment of ICAI processes enabled for the conduct of virtual and physical courses. It is currently being used by the Central Committees for Certificate Courses, Post-Qualification Courses, Refresher Courses, CPE events and online self-paced courses, and has been extended to ICAI Branches and Regional Councils for conducting their virtual CPE events and web lectures. The Digital Learning Hub broadly provides the following facilities to its stakeholders: • Knowledge repository for professional and academic learning, with content delivery providing access to courses, lectures and other learning materials. • Learning content in multiple formats (documents, PPTs, video, audio, SCORM) tailored to suit every learner. • Leader board feature showcasing credits achieved, and badges to members based on CPE hours earned. • Structured and Unstructured CPE hours credits for ICAI members. • Communities to share ideas. • Course access to Government officials, bank officials and MRA/MoU/SAFA members, along with Skill India courses for non-members. • Certificates generated for learners on successful completion of examinations.

(VII) Online Mode of Service Delivery for Members, Students and Internal ICAI Stakeholders • Centralised Online Portal: Maintenance of the centralised online portal of ICAI (https://icai.org/) to serve as a one-stop solution for various services and resources for members, students and internal stakeholders. • Virtual Meetings and Conferences: Implemented platforms for virtual meetings, conferences and events, ensuring seamless communication and engagement across different ICAI Committees/Departments and Regional Offices. • Helpdesk and Support: Established online helpdesk and support services to provide real-time assistance and resolution of queries for members and students. • Mobile Applications: Developed mobile applications to provide on-the-go access to ICAI services, resources and updates, enhancing user convenience. • Cloud-Based Solutions: Adopted cloud-based solutions to ensure scalability, data security and remote access to ICAI's digital infrastructure and services. • Remote Work Facilitation: Implemented technologies and platforms to support remote work for ICAI officials and Committees/Departments, ensuring uninterrupted administrative and operational activities. • Data Analytics and Reporting: Leveraged data analytics tools to monitor service usage, gather feedback and generate reports to continuously improve service delivery and user experience. • Compliance and Security: Ensured that all online services comply with regulatory standards and incorporated robust cybersecurity measures to protect user data and maintain the integrity of ICAI's digital platforms.

(VIII) Technological Support Provided to Other Departments/Committees of ICAI • GeM Portal: Successfully created and is maintaining the administrative setup of the GeM Buyer account for ICAI on the Government e-Marketplace (GeM) Portal. • Statistical Dashboard: Developed and is maintaining the dashboard for members- and students-related statistical information. • Tracker.icai.org: The payment tracking portal has been developed and is maintained by the DRTD.

9.5 ESTATE DEVELOPMENT DIRECTORATE (EDD) The Estate Development Directorate (EDD) administers matters relating to the release of Capital Grants to Units of ICAI, including Regional Councils, Branches, DCOs, and Centres of Excellence (COEs), for repair and renovation works, additions, internal modifications, and small construction activities up to ₹200 lakhs. The several key initiatives towards infrastructure standardisation, sustainability, and digital governance, are given below:-

(I) Infrastructure Standardisation Under the theme "My Estate, My Pride," EDD launched Project SHIELD – the Sustainable Infrastructure Initiative – to promote sustainable and environmentally responsible infrastructure management across ICAI establishments nationwide. The project lays down a unified framework for branding, signage, and the upkeep of ICAI premises, ensuring that every Unit reflects a consistent and dignified institutional identity. As part of the brand building exercise, EDD continued the installation of uniform glow signage and standardised painting of ICAI buildings across the country, reinforcing visual consistency and strengthening the Institute's public image.

(II) Installation of Solar Power Plants In furtherance of the Institute's commitment to clean energy and sustainability, EDD continued the installation of solar power plants at various ICAI establishments. The initiative aims to reduce the Institute's carbon footprint, lower energy costs, and demonstrate ICAI's leadership in adopting renewable energy solutions across its nationwide network of offices.

(III) Review of Safety and Accessibility Infrastructure EDD conducted a comprehensive review of firefighting systems, lift facilities, accessibility provisions for specially-abled persons, and rainwater harvesting systems across 129 ICAI Units. This exercise was undertaken to assess the current state of safety, accessibility, and environmental compliance infrastructure, and to identify Units requiring upgradation. The review forms the basis for a structured action plan to bring all Units up to uniform safety and accessibility standards.

(IV) Automation of Directorate Processes EDD continued the automation of its processes through dedicated software for Capital Budget management, asset tracking, and workflow monitoring. The automation initiative has significantly enhanced efficiency, transparency, and governance in the processing and disbursement of Capital Grants, enabling real-time tracking of project status and financial utilisation across ICAI Units.

(V) ICAI Reading Room Initiatives EDD undertook detailed surveys and collected status reports from ICAI Units to assess existing Reading Room infrastructure, student usage patterns, and operational feasibility. Based on the findings of this exercise, the ICAI Reading Room Policy 2025 was formulated and approved in August 2025. The policy introduces simplified norms, defines infrastructure standards, and streamlines approval procedures for the establishment and management of Reading Rooms across the country, ensuring a consistent and student-friendly experience at all locations.

To support implementation of the policy, EDD launched Project SHABD – Students Hub for Aspirations and Building Dreams – for the creation of modern Reading Hubs equipped with improved study facilities and student-centric amenities. EDD also organised orientation sessions and webinars for Regional Councils, Branches, and student representatives on the provisions of the policy and the associated funding mechanisms, ensuring smooth and uniform adoption across the Institute's network.

9.6 FINANCIAL AND TAX LITERACY DIRECTORATE In line with the Government of India's Vittiya Saksharta Abhiyaan, the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), through its Financial and Tax Literacy Directorate (now merged with Financial Markets and Investors' Awareness Committee (FM&IAC), continued its efforts during 2025– 26 to promote financial and tax literacy among the public. Guided by the motto 'Har Jan, Har Man, Vittiyagyan se Sampann', the Directorate expanded its outreach through strategic collaborations with Government Departments, State Rural Livelihood Missions, educational institutions, and other organisations, while also leveraging digital and mass media platforms to enhance financial awareness across diverse sections of society, including students, teachers, government officials, women self-help groups, agriculturists, senior citizens, enforcement personnel, scientists, scholars, housewives, and local communities.

(I) Key Initiatives • Financial Literacy for Government Functionaries The Directorate conducted specialised financial literacy workshops for administrative officials at the Chief Minister's Secretariat, Meghalaya, and organised sessions at the Governor's Houses of Uttarakhand and Meghalaya to strengthen financial awareness among government functionaries. • Financial Inclusion at the Grassroots Level To promote financial inclusion, the Directorate organised dedicated financial literacy programmes for Women Self-Help Groups across Meghalaya, Mizoram, Tripura, Arunachal Pradesh, Sikkim, and the Andaman & Nicobar Islands in collaboration with the respective State Rural Development Departments and Rural Livelihood Missions, reaching over 1,000 SHGs. • Citizen Engagement through MyGov Platform The Directorate leveraged the Government of India's MyGov platform to deepen citizen engagement, reaching over 46,950 individuals through quizzes, slogan-writing, poster-making, reel-making, and poetry competitions on financial literacy themes. • Nationwide Outreach Campaign The Directorate launched the nationwide 'Vittiyagyan – ICAI Ka Abhiyan', comprising financial literacy workshops and practical sessions organised through ICAI Regional Councils and Branches, along with a multilingual radio awareness campaign in 10 Indian languages that reached over 3.93 crore listeners by simplifying complex financial concepts for the general public. • Financial Literacy Week 2025 Financial Literacy Week was observed from 17–23 August 2025 with the support of ICAI's five Regional Councils and 185 Branches, during which awareness programmes were organised across the country, reaching over 3,000 participants.

(II) Major Achievements World Record Recognition The Financial and Tax Literacy Directorate established a world record by conducting over 190 Financial and Tax Literacy programmes across the country within nine months (March–November 2025), earning recognition from the World Book of Records, London.

(III) Institutional Outreach Programmes The Directorate conducted Financial and Tax Literacy workshops at various government institutions, educational establishments, and uniformed services organisations, including the National Institute of Immunology, Indian National Science Academy, Regional Centre for Biotechnology, National Cadet Corps, Bharat Scouts and Guides, leading colleges, the Central Reserve Police Force, and the Central Bureau of Investigation, thereby further expanding the reach and impact of its financial literacy initiatives nationwide.

9.7 GRIEVANCE RESOLUTION DIRECTORATE Grievance Resolution Directorate (GRD) (now merged with Members & Students Services Directorate) was a centralized department of ICAI formed to oversee the support system for our students, members and other stakeholders for their engagement with institute's various activities which is delivered through multiple departments, committees dealing in respective areas. The GRD facilitates timely and effective grievance redressal through multiple channels, including Call Sahayata, E-Sahayata, dedicated email support, and social media. The Directorate focuses on active process monitoring, timeliness, root cause analysis, coordination with all stakeholders ensuring transparency, trust, and overall stakeholder satisfaction.

Call Sahayata ICAI Call Sahayata (9997599975) operates from 09:00 AM To 09:00 PM, Monday to Saturday, except on gazetted holidays, enabling students, members and stakeholders to reach ICAI primarily through inbound calls for their queries. Calls received during operational hours are routed through and Interactive Voice Response(IVR) system, ensuring effective inbound calls management. Queries received outside operational hours are captured through the IVR and are addressed during working hours through callbacks. The helpline handles both general information and service-related queries in an efficient and systematic manner.

Multilingual Support, Operational KPIs and Statistical Analysis Call Sahayata continued to provide stakeholder support in Hindi, English and nine regional languages, ensuring wider accessibility across India. The language-wise call handling pattern reflected Hindi as the major medium of communication at 53.85%, followed by English at 33.21% and regional languages at

12.94%. Regional language support was available in Tamil, Telugu, Marathi, Kannada, Assamese, Kashmiri, Punjabi, Odia and Bengali, enabling stakeholders from different parts of the country to seek assistance in their preferred language and improving the overall inclusiveness of the grievance redressal mechanism.

During the year, Call Sahayata performance was monitored through defined operational KPIs to ensure service quality, responsiveness and effective stakeholder support. The facility handled a total call volume of over 10.34 lakh calls with support from 84 agents. Of the total call activity, 76.86% related to inbound calls and 23.14% to outbound calls. The average talk time for inbound calls was 4.70 minutes, with more than 2,500 calls handled per day. Peak call traffic was generally observed between 10:00 AM and 5:00 PM. Around 82% of calls were from students, followed by members and other stakeholders. The highest inbound call volume was recorded on 7th July 2025, coinciding with the CA course result announcement, while the highest outbound call activity was recorded on 3rd April 2025.

E-Sahayata (e-Tickets) It is an online grievance redressal platform accessible at https://eservices.icai.org/through which students and members can raise tickets directly. In cases where stakeholders are not satisfied with the support received or where resolution is delayed, tickets may also be raised through Call Sahayata agents on their behalf. It functions based on ICAI activities and the officers responsible for those activities, referred to as the escalation matrix, which is critical for effective grievance resolution Performance Highlights Summary E-Sahayata continued to serve as an effective online grievance redressal platform for students and members, ensuring timely support and structured escalation through the ICAI activity-based escalation matrix. During February 2025 to January 2026, the platform handled 30,874 grievances, out of which 30,791 tickets were successfully closed, achieving an impressive 99% resolution rate. The platform also demonstrated strong operational efficiency, with 85.51% of tickets resolved within one week, reflecting prompt response, effective coordination, and commitment to stakeholder satisfaction.

Comprehensive Training for Enhanced Grievance Support (2025–2026) During 2025–2026, the Grievance Redressal Department (GRD) conducted 21 comprehensive training sessions for the Call Sahayata Team to enhance grievance handling. Designed using trend analysis and recurring queries, these sessions involved close collaboration with key departments, including CA Exam, Articleship, UDIN, CPE, SSP, Campus Placement, BOS Scholarship, PDC, and Member Training.

Additionally, GRD proactively coordinated with these departments to update FAQs, ensuring stakeholders always receive the most accurate and up-to-date information.

Key Service Enhancements and Digital Initiatives  Rationalization of Call Categories: Call categories (dispositions and sub-dispositions) were comprehensively reviewed to improve relevance, simplify call classification, and generate meaningful insights. The categories were rationalized from over 170 sub-disposition options to 7 primary dispositions (Student, Examination, Articles, CDS, Member, Firm, and Committee) with 85 streamlined sub-dispositions, enabling more accurate categorization and efficient handling of stakeholder queries.  Enhancement of E-Sahayata System: The E-Sahayata escalation matrix was comprehensively reviewed to simplify activities, standardize categories, and ensure correct officer mapping for faster grievance resolution. The matrix was updated across Regions, Committees, Departments, Boards, and Directorates, with query options rationalized from 377 to 122. Additionally, a self-service password reset facility, along with a user guide, was introduced for Department/Committee users, reducing login issues and improving grievance resolution timelines.  Optimization of Interactive Voice Response (IVR) System: The Interactive Voice Response (IVR) system, an automated telephony system that guides callers through voice prompts and keypad inputs, was optimized by simplifying the menu structure and eliminating redundant options to reduce call hang-ups and enable faster connectivity for stakeholders. The revised IVR framework was implemented. Additionally, regular follow-up with the call service provider reduced the average call connection time from 7–8 seconds to 4–5 seconds, further enhancing the stakeholder experience.  SMS Integration in Call Sahayata: Earlier, SMS alerts for grievance tickets generated through Call Sahayata were not available. During the year, SMS notifications were introduced, enabling stakeholders to receive instant ticket confirmations after calls and facilitating easier tracking of grievances, thereby enhancing transparency and communication.  Initiative for Updated FAQs: The Directorate proactively followed up with Departments, Committees, Boards, and Directorates to update and standardize FAQs, with the objective of providing concise, accurate, and up-to-date information through the ICAI Call Sahayata Portal, ensuring easier access, an improved user experience, and enhanced self-service for stakeholders.

9.8 HUMAN RESOURCES DIRECTORATE The Human Resources Directorate of ICAI (now Human Resource Department) focused on HR-related matters across all offices of ICAI, such as recruitment, transfer and rotation, performance management, and training and development. HRD continued to strengthen the Institute's talent acquisition efforts during the year, wherein recruitment for middle and senior-level positions was carried out through advertisements and interviews. 43 new employees joined various branches across India under the Branch Human Resource Scheme (BHRS) 2022. With ICAI's expanding footprint and evolving responsibilities, the Human Resources Department remains committed to building an agile and futureready workforce through strategic HR initiatives, continuous capacity building, and a culture of professionalism, inclusivity, and high performance.

The development of a Human Resource Management System (HRMS) and a mobile-based Employee Self-Service (ESS) application was initiated to integrate various HR processes and enable employees to access HR services more efficiently. Group Term Life Insurance coverage was also introduced for employees to provide financial support to their families in the event of any unforeseen circumstances The key initiatives during the year are as under: • Recruitment: Recruitment under the Branch Employees Human Resource Scheme, 2022 was undertaken to augment resources at various Branch offices. Additionally, the recruitment process for key positions at the Head Office was also initiated. • Employee Welfare: Initiatives such as insurance coverage for regular employees through a Group Term Life Insurance policy and a Directors' & Officers' Liability policy were taken to provide coverage. • HR Automation: It was decided to implement a comprehensive Human Resource Management System (HRMS) to enable the delivery of all HR processes on a single platform; accordingly, the automation of HR data through the HRMS package has been initiated.

9.9 LEGAL DIRECTORATE Legal Directorate is responsible for the enforcement and compliance with the provisions of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 and Rules & Regulations made thereunder. It takes necessary action to protect and defend the interest of the Institute in the cases filed against it, provides legal opinions to various Departments within ICAI on adherence to the statutory mandate, defends regulatory actions in various courts, collaborates with other Departments to enforce regulations and penalize violations as per the statutory mandate.

The significant legal developments concerning the Institute and the profession during the year are as under:

(I) Supreme Court Rules in Favour of Chartered Accountants – Parity with Advocates for Appointment as Member (Accountant), ITAT The Hon'ble Supreme Court restored parity between Chartered Accountants and Advocates in the matter of the minimum experience criteria for appointment as Members of the Income Tax Appellate Tribunal (ITAT). In a landmark judgement pronounced on 20th November 2025, a Bench of the Hon'ble Supreme Court comprising the Chief Justice of India and Justice K. Vinod Chandran invalidated the rule requiring 25 years of experience for the post of Member (Accountant) of the ITAT. The ruling places Chartered Accountants on a par with Advocates regarding the minimum experience criteria and sets right the injustice to Chartered Accountants under the Rules, which prescribed a minimum experience of 25 years for a Chartered Accountant to be considered for the post of Member (Accountant) as against only 10 years for Advocates to be considered for the post of Member (Judicial).

Historically, the minimum experience requirements for both Chartered Accountants and Advocates were the same, before the new Rules disturbed this parity to the disadvantage of Chartered Accountants.

The judgement restores the status quo between Advocates and Chartered Accountants in the matter of the minimum experience requirement, which had occupied the field for many years.

(II) Directions of the Hon'ble Supreme Court in Himanshu Singh vs. UOI (the "Supertech Case") The Hon'ble Supreme Court of India, on 29th April 2025, in the case of Himanshu Singh vs. Union of India (popularly known as the Supertech Case), issued various directions to the Government of India, the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs, RERA, the RBI, the CBI and ICAI. More than 170 petitions were filed by over 1,200 home buyers in the Supreme Court alleging the systematic failure of statutory and government authorities to discharge their statutory functions, the circumvention of the regulatory framework by banks and housing finance corporations, and the resultant illicit benefits said to have been drawn by builders/developers, with home buyers bearing the brunt of such failures.

In so far as ICAI is concerned, the Hon'ble Court directed as follows:

"19. The President, Institute of Chartered Accountants of India is directed to deploy three Chartered Accountants, who are well conversant with the process of forensic audit and place them at the disposal of CBI to assist the Bureau/SIT in the matter of subject enquiry/investigations. The details of the remuneration to be paid to these Chartered Accountants be forwarded to this Court so that an appropriate order in that regard can be passed on the next date of hearing."

9.10 MEMBERS & STUDENTS SERVICES DIRECTORATE (M&SS) The Members & Students Services (M&SS) Directorate is one of the key functional Directorates of the Institute entrusted with administering the regulatory, operational and service-related functions concerning members, students and firms in accordance with the provisions of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, the Chartered Accountants Regulations, 1988, and the Guidelines, decisions and directions issued by the Council, the Executive Committee and the Management Committee from time to time.

The Directorate serves as the principal interface for delivering a wide range of services relating to enrolment, admission to fellowship, grant and restoration of Certificate of Practice, registration and regulation of firms, practical and industrial training, student registration and allied services, maintenance of statutory records and registers, issuance of various certificates and identity cards, organisation of convocations, publication of statutory notifications, and grievance redressal.

With the Institute's growing membership and student base and the increasing emphasis on technologyenabled governance, the Directorate continues to strengthen service delivery by simplifying procedures, ensuring effective implementation of regulatory policies, enhancing digital services through the SelfService Portal (SSP), promoting paperless processes, developing efficient monitoring and management information systems, and improving coordination across the Head Office and Regional Offices.

The Directorate also operates a dedicated grievance redressal mechanism through E-Sahaayataa and Call Sahaayataa, ensuring timely, transparent and responsive resolution of concerns raised by members, students and firms. Through these initiatives, the Directorate remains committed to providing efficient, accountable and stakeholder-centric services while supporting the Institute's objective of excellence in professional administration.

(I) Membership During the year ended 31st March 2026, 29,720 new members were enrolled by ICAI, bringing the total membership to 4,52,825 as on 1st April 2026. During the year, 3,761 Associate members were admitted as Fellows, in comparison to the figure of 3,677 in the previous year.

These events were held at 15 locations across India, including Ahmedabad, Bengaluru, Chandigarh, Chennai, Ghaziabad, Hyderabad, Vijayawada, Indore, Jaipur, Kolkata, Ludhiana, Mumbai, Delhi and Pune, where a total of 33,686 newly enrolled Chartered Accountants received their certificates.

9.11 PUBLICATION DIRECTORATE The Publication Directorate (erstwhile Publication & CDS Directorate) plays a pivotal role in ensuring the availability of ICAI's educational and professional publications to students, members, and other stakeholders. The Directorate oversees the printing, inventory management, distribution, and accounting of study materials, member publications, and other official publications of the Institute. The Directorate manages the Institute's online Publication Portal (https://cds.icai.org/), which enables stakeholders to purchase publications and artefacts through a seamless digital platform. The portal supports online ordering, payment processing, and delivery through the Centralized Distribution System

(CDS), thereby enhancing accessibility and service efficiency.

To maintain an efficient supply chain, the Directorate administers inventory across designated storage locations and undertakes periodic physical stock verification. Stock valuation and inventory records are maintained in coordination with the Accounts Department to support accurate financial reporting and effective inventory control. The Directorate also undertakes reconciliation of publication sales with payment gateway collections and facilitates the integration of sales and dispatch information with the Institute's ERP system. This ensures transparency, accuracy, and timely accounting of publicationrelated transactions. Through these functions, the Publication Directorate contributes significantly to the Institute's objective of providing timely access to quality educational resources while maintaining robust inventory management, financial discipline, and operational transparency.

(I) New Publications Brought Out During the period from 1st April 2025 to 31st March 2026, the Publication Directorate printed various new publications on behalf of the Board of Studies (Academic) and other Committees, which are also hosted on the Publication Portal.

(II) Centralised Distribution System (CDS) Since July 2017, all publications of ICAI, including study materials, Revision Test Papers, memberrelated publications and various mementoes such as ties, tie pins, cuff links and lapel pins, are dispatched centrally through the CDS in the Publication Portal (https://cds.icai.org/) to students, members and other stakeholders based on orders received through the portal.

(III) Dispatch Statistics (1st April 2025 to 31st March 2026) Category Types of Items Quantity Dispatched Student publications dispatched against registration 46 types 15,86,268 books Student publications dispatched against sale 113 types 28,019 books Member publications dispatched against sale 324 types 13,185 books Mementoes dispatched against sale 26 types 1,134 units

(IV) Future Endeavours The Directorate is at the final stage of developing a new Publication Portal. The new portal is expected to go live shortly and will provide an enhanced user experience, improved operational efficiency, better monitoring of publication orders, and reduced turnaround time for processing and delivery to students, members and other stakeholders.

9.12 REGION & BRANCH AFFAIRS (RBA) DIRECTORATE As ICAI continues to strengthen its presence and serve a vibrant community of over 4 lakh members and more than 9 lakh students, the Region & Branch Affairs (RBA) Directorate plays a vital role in ensuring the seamless and effective functioning of the Institute's extensive pan-India network of 5 Regional Councils and 186 Branches. Acting as the central coordinating bridge between the Headquarters and regional formations, the Directorate provides strategic guidance, administrative support, and operational oversight to foster consistency, efficiency, and excellence across the organization.

Through enhanced institutional coordination, process optimization, knowledge dissemination, and capacity-building initiatives, the Directorate enables regional and branch-level units to effectively deliver ICAI's educational, professional, and member-focused programmes. By promoting collaboration, strengthening stakeholder engagement, and supporting continuous improvement, the RBA Directorate significantly contributes to advancing ICAI's mission of upholding excellence, integrity, professionalism, and public trust, while embracing technology, transformation, and innovation in the service of the profession and nation-building.

(I) Regional Councils and their Branches The Institute has five Regional Councils – Western India Regional Council (WIRC), Southern India Regional Council (SIRC), Eastern India Regional Council (EIRC), Central India Regional Council (CIRC) and Northern India Regional Council (NIRC) – with their Headquarters at Mumbai, Chennai, Kolkata, Kanpur and New Delhi respectively. The total number of Branches of Regional Councils is 186. With a view to actively involving students of the CA course in developing a spirit of fellowship and promoting social, cultural, academic and intellectual development, the Council has encouraged the setting up of Branches of Chartered Accountants Students' Association (CASA). So far, 145 Branches of CASA have been set up.

(II) Awards for Best Regional Council, Best Branch and Best Students' Association (2025) These awards are given by the Institute every year on the basis of overall performance and established norms. The awards for 2025 were presented at the Annual Function held on 1st February 2026, on the sidelines of the World Forum of Accountants (WOFA 2.0), to the following winners:

Category Prize Winner(s) Best Regional Council 1st Prize WIRC Best Regional Council 2nd Prize SIRC Best Students' Association 1st Prize EICASA & NICASA (Jointly) Best Students' Association 2nd Prize WICASA Best Branch – Mega Category 1st Prize Ahmedabad Best Branch – Mega Category 2nd Prize Pune Best Branch – Large Category 1st Prize Nagpur Best Branch – Large Category 2nd Prize Vasai Best Branch – Medium Category 1st Prize Vijayawada & Bhopal (Jointly) Best Branch – Medium Category 2nd Prize Guwahati & Ludhiana (Jointly) Best Branch – Small Category 1st Prize Tirupur & Thiruvananthapuram (Jointly) Best Branch – Small Category 2nd Prize Jammu & Kashmir & Bhilai (Jointly) Best Branch – Micro Category 1st Prize Bilaspur Best Branch – Micro Category 2nd Prize Ratlam Best Students' Assoc. Branch – Mega 1st Prize Ahmedabad & Bengaluru (Jointly) Best Students' Assoc. Branch – Mega 2nd Prize Pune & Vasai (Jointly) Best Students' Assoc.
