(III) Managing Committee Members Meet (MCMM) 2026 – Regional Level The RBA Directorate successfully conducted the Regional Level Managing Committee Members Meet 2026 across all five regions, with the objective of providing structured orientation to elected Managing Committee Members of Branches and Regional Council Members. These programmes were conducted as per the following schedule:

Region Dates Venue Northern Region 11th–12th March 2026 Chandigarh Western Region 13th–14th March 2026 Mumbai Central Region 16th–17th March 2026 Ghaziabad Eastern Region 21st–22nd March 2026 Kolkata Southern Region 23rd–24th March 2026 Hyderabad A standardised programme structure was designed and implemented uniformly across all regions to ensure consistency in guidance and approach. The sessions, delivered by esteemed Central Council Members in their respective regions, covered key operational and governance areas including: Conduct of Meetings & Events; Accounting, Audit & Statutory Compliances; Council Directions, Advisories & RBA Grant Management Framework; ICAI Policy Framework; Students' Activities & Grants; Performance Management of Branches & Students' Associations; and the CPE Framework.

A key feature of these programmes was an interactive session with the leadership, wherein Managing Committee Members shared their suggestions, ideas and practical challenges faced at the branch level.

A total of 1,095 Managing Committee Members (out of 1,206) and 62 Regional Council Members (out of 64) participated, reflecting an encouraging response. Feedback from participants indicated great appreciation for the content, structure and practical relevance of the orientation programmes.

9.13 SPECIFIC FUND MANAGEMENT DIRECTORATE The Specific Fund Management Directorate was constituted by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) to ensure the dedicated, transparent, and efficient administration of funds established for social welfare, educational advancement, and charitable purposes. The Directorate is responsible for the effective management, continuous monitoring, and transparent reporting of these funds, ensuring that their resources are deployed strictly in accordance with the objectives for which they have been constituted. Through a robust governance framework, the Directorate seeks to maximize the social and professional impact of these funds while upholding the principles of accountability, integrity, and good governance.

In line with ICAI's enduring commitment to the welfare of its members, students, and the larger society, the Directorate administers the Chartered Accountants Benevolent Fund (CABF), Chartered Accountants Students Benevolent Fund (CASBF) and S. Vaidyanath Aiyar Memorial Fund (SVAMF). By institutionalizing a comprehensive framework for fund governance, financial management, monitoring, compliance, and reporting, the Specific Fund Management Directorate reinforces ICAI's core values of compassion, integrity, transparency, and trustworthiness Chartered Accountants' Benevolent Fund The Chartered Accountants Benevolent Fund (CABF) has been established to provide financial assistance for maintenance, education, medical support, and other similar purposes to necessitous persons, including members or former members of the Institute irrespective of whether they were subscribers to the Fund, the wives and children of such members, widows and children of deceased members, and relatives or other dependants who relied upon a deceased member for support where the member has passed away without leaving behind a widow or child. Through timely financial assistance, the CABF serves as a vital social security mechanism, extending support to members and their families during periods of financial hardship and reinforcing the profession's spirit of solidarity and mutual care.

(SVAMF), established to commemorate the memory and enduring contributions of Late CA S.

Vaidyanath Aiyar, a former President of ICAI. The Fund has been created through voluntary contributions from members of the Institute with the objective of recognizing professional excellence, promoting professional academic through lectures and research activities, and supporting deserving students.

The number of life membership of the Fund as on 31st March 2026 is 11,089. The balance in the Corpus Fund was Rs. 81,85,150/- as on 31st March 2026 as against Rs. 81,25,150/- as on 31st March,

2025.

Chartered Accountants Student's Benevolent Fund (CASBF) The Directorate also manages the Chartered Accountants Students Benevolent Fund (CASBF), which has been established to provide financial assistance to economically weaker Chartered Accountancy students, enabling them to pursue their professional education without undue financial constraints. The Fund provides medical financial assistance to deserving students facing medical emergencies, reimburses course and examination fees of economically weaker students whose parent or guardian is receiving assistance under the Chartered Accountants Benevolent Fund, and grant scholarships to financially distressed students who have lost both parents. Through these initiatives, the CASBF seeks to ensure that financial adversity does not become an impediment to pursuing the Chartered Accountancy course and supports deserving students in successfully completing their professional education.

The Fund was established in August, 2008 with the aim and objective to provide financial assistance to the students registered with ICAI. The balance in the credit of the general fund was Rs.16,37,29,559/as on 31st March, 2026 as against Rs. 17,13,64,481/- as on 31st March, 2025.

9.14 TENDER MONITORING DIRECTORATE In order to monitor, analyze and streamline tendering for professional services of Chartered Accountants, a Directorate namely "Tender Monitoring Directorate" was constituted by the Council during the year 2023-24 which has now merged with PDC. The Directorate has been endeavouring to streamline the process of tendering and to monitor the bids quoted by CAs in all the permissible tenders and analyse them. The Directorate further refers the deviations at appropriate level.

Major activities of the Directorate The Directorate has its own Portal for tenders on professional services – www.tmdicai.org. which offers the following features:  Tender opportunities  Latest information for members  Council Guidelines on tenders  Details of minimum recommended scale of fees  Format of cost sheet to be submitted by members  Extract from relevant provisions of Code of Ethics  Representations made by Directorate to the tenderers  Answers to Frequently Asked Questions  Grievance posting mechanism, with or without disclosing identity.  Fee calculator for the ease of calculating fee for audit and other assignments.

(I) Representations to Tendering Organisations Representations are being made to tenderers where minimum fees is not prescribed in respect of audit & attestation services, or where eligibility conditions appear to be unreasonably restrictive or where the services tendered create "independence" issues. During the functional year 2025-26, 147 such representations were sent to the tendering organisations.

(II) Review and Hosting of Tenders TMD also collects tenders for professional services from all over India and hosts the same in its TMD Portal. During the functional year 2025-26, 2,112 tenders were studied, out of which 1,937 tenders were hosted in the TMD portal as these were found appropriate in all respects.

(III) Examination of Bids Submitted by Members Bids submitted by members are studied and cost sheets are called for wherever it is felt that the bid is for low amount. During the functional year 2025-26, notices were sent to 209 CA firms in respect of 38 tenders. During the year 2025-26, cases of 102 CA firms were referred to Director (Discipline) for investigation. Peer review has been ordered in respect of 10 CA firms. Cases relating to 17 CA firms have been closed as the cost sheet was found to be satisfactory. Examination of rest of the cases is on.

9.15 UDIN DIRECTORATE The Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN), introduced by ICAI, continues to play a pivotal role in ensuring the authenticity, reliability and transparency of documents certified by Chartered Accountants. By assigning a system-generated unique identification number to every certificate, audit report and attestation, UDIN serves as a robust mechanism to prevent document forgery and misuse.

Over the years, UDIN has evolved into a critical component of the regulatory ecosystem, facilitating seamless verification of documents by regulators, government authorities, financial institutions and other users. During the year under review, UDIN witnessed significant advancements in technological transformation, regulatory integration and functional enhancements.

(I) UDIN as a Decision-Making Parameter for Regulators, Banks and Other Stakeholders • MoU with the Reserve Bank of India: ICAI signed an MoU with the RBI on 6th February 2026 at ICAI Bhawan, New Delhi, during the 448th Council Meeting, to facilitate real-time verification of UDINs through secure API integration. The initiative aligns with RBI's end-to-end digital transformation framework and reflects ICAI's commitment to technology-driven governance. • MoU with the Indian Army (DGST): An MoU was signed on 6th November 2025 with the Indian Army, Directorate General of Supplies and Transport, for integration of UDIN verification within the vendor registration process of the Army Purchase Organisation (APO), enabling automated authentication of CA-certified documents. The MoU was executed in the presence of Lieutenant General Mukesh Chadha, AVSM, SM, VSM, Director General of Supplies and Transport. • Ministry of Home Affairs (Project FCRA 2.0): The Ministry of Home Affairs approached the UDIN Directorate for API-based integration to enable real-time verification of certificates and audit reports submitted under the Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Act, 2010. A meeting was held on 21st April 2026 at the FCRA Wing, New Delhi, to deliberate on the requirements for enabling API-based UDIN verification. • Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology (MeitY): Continued engagement for integration of UDIN with the Entity Locker platform, a secure digital repository for document storage and sharing. Two meetings were held in 2025 (17th March and 28th July) to discuss APIbased integration requirements. • C&AG Empanelment: The C&AG used UDIN data as one of the elements for empanelment of CA firms/LLPs for the first time in 2023-24. For 2026-27, points are awarded based on UDINs generated during 2022-23, 2023-24 and 2024-25, with the window to be scaled to 5 years by 2026-27.

(II) Technology Transformation and Digital Initiatives • Migration to DigiCA Platform: The UDIN Portal was successfully migrated to the DigiCA Platform on 20th December 2025, providing an enhanced user interface, improved system performance and advanced functionalities. Post-migration, the portal witnessed substantial improvements in system stability, responsiveness and user experience, with better load handling, improved uptime and enhanced data security. • Mobile Application (iOS and Android): A mobile application was launched in a phased manner, enabling members to generate, view, download and revoke UDINs in real time, enhancing accessibility and operational efficiency during urgent and time-sensitive professional engagements. • Security Audit by C-DAC: The Vulnerability Assessment and Penetration Testing

(VAPT)/Security Audit of the UDIN web portal under the DigiCA Platform was entrusted to CDAC. The Security Audit Certificate for the web portal was received on 17th December 2025, and the Security Audit Certificate for the iOS Mobile Application was received on 19th January 2026.

(III) Functional Enhancements for Members • PAN Validation for GST & Tax Audit UDINs: Pursuant to the 417th Council Meeting decision, the PAN of the assessee has been introduced as a mandatory additional field during UDIN generation under the GST & Tax Audit category for validation at the e-Filing Portal, applicable for UDINs generated from 20th December 2025 onwards. • Preceding Year Audit Details: Pursuant to the 442nd Council Meeting decision, a provision has been enabled on the UDIN Portal to capture preceding year audit details during UDIN generation under the GST & Tax Audit and Audit & Assurance Function categories, made operational with effect from 20th December 2025. • Ceiling on Tax Audit UDINs: Pursuant to the 442nd Council Meeting decision, a provision introducing a ceiling on the maximum number of UDINs that may be generated under Tax Audit assignments, in alignment with the prescribed limit of 60 tax audits, is under development.

Counting of Tax Audit assignments has been made effective from 1st April 2026. Field-Level Validation was implemented on 11th February 2026 for all sub-categories under Section 44AB. • Auditor's Opinion Functionality: Pursuant to the 446th Council Meeting decision, the functionality to capture "Auditor's Opinion" during UDIN generation was implemented – for Audit & Assurance Functions from midnight of 20th June 2025, and for GST & Tax Audit from midnight of 28th June 2025. • Inclusion of New Forms: Form 26AANX was enabled on the UDIN Portal on 22nd October 2025 on the request of CBDT; Form 146 under the Income-tax Act, 2025 was incorporated from 1st April 2026.

(IV) Milestone Achievement The total number of UDINs generated crossed 10 Crore in December 2025. As on 7th May 2026, more than 1.70 lakh Chartered Accountants have logged on the UDIN Portal and have generated more than

10.65 Crore UDINs across three categories – Audit & Assurance Functions, Certificates and GST & Tax Audit. More than 3.69 Crore UDINs have been verified and validated by authorities, regulators, banks and other stakeholders. The total number of UDIN generation since its inception is given below:Year Total No. of UDIN generated 76,89,789 91,52,414 1,16,41,268 1,65,51,874 1,69,97,473 1,78,07,644 2,17,20,798 75,83,709 Grant Total 10,91,44,969 Total Number of UDINs Generated (Since Inception to 15.07.2026) 1,65,51,874 1,69,97,473 1,78,07,644 2,17,20,798 1,16,41,268 76,89,789 91,52,414 75,83,709

(V) Branding and Identity The UDIN logo, launched during the previous year, has further strengthened the identity of the initiative as a symbol of authenticity, trust and professionalism. Applications for registration under Copyright, Intellectual Property and Trademark (CIPT) were filed on 1st September 2025.

(VI) Capacity Building and Knowledge Dissemination • Webinar on "Overview of the UDIN Portal on DigiCA Platform" – 8th January 2026. • Webinar on "Clarifications on Auditor's Opinion Functionality: Q&A Session" – 14th October 2025. • Webinar on "Preliminary Insights on UDIN for Bank Audits" – 7th April 2025. • Release of Addenda to FAQs (5th Edition) addressing new functionalities and system changes.

(VII) Publications  UDIN Report 2025-26 (providing insights into the growth, adoption and statistical trends of the UDIN system)  6th Edition of FAQs on Unique Document Identification Number (UDIN) (Revised 2026), addressing common member queries and enhancing clarity and compliance.

9.16 ICAI PLACEMENT DIRECTORATE The ICAI Placement Directorate of The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India was formed in the year 2026 with the objective of creating a dedicated and focused platform for facilitating career opportunities for Chartered Accountants and strengthening industry-profession engagement. The Directorate acts as a bridge between industry and the Chartered Accountancy profession by providing structured, transparent, and technology-driven placement and career support services for Newly Qualified Chartered Accountants (NQCAs), Experienced Chartered Accountants, semi-qualified professionals, and organizations across diverse sectors and geographies.

With a vision to empower finance professionals and support organizations in accessing quality talent, the ICAI Placement Directorate undertakes various initiatives including Campus Placement Programmes, ICAI Campus Placement+, Career Ascent Programmes, HR Meets, Overseas Placement Initiatives, CA Career Catalyst Programmes, Orientation Programmes, and Management Development Programmes (MDPs) for CA Rank Holders. The Directorate also offers continuous recruitment and networking support through its digital platforms, enabling organizations to connect with highly skilled finance professionals throughout the year while helping Chartered Accountants explore opportunities across domains The major initiatives are as under:

(I) CA Career Catalyst Programme 2026 The Directorate launched the CA Career Catalyst Programme 2026, a comprehensive virtual careerreadiness initiative designed for newly qualified Chartered Accountants and CA Final qualified candidates, conducted from 15th to 22nd March 2026 in a phased manner. During the period from 15th to 20th March 2026, domain-focused webinar sessions were organised daily covering important sectors and emerging career opportunities for Chartered Accountants, including taxation, mergers and acquisitions, finance, investment banking, statutory audit, internal audit, financial due diligence, forensic accounting, consultancy, advisory, FP&A, costing, budgeting and offshore opportunities.

Further, on 21st and 22nd March 2026, structured sessions on Group Discussion, HR Training, Mock Interviews and One-to-One Mentoring were organised in batches to equip participants with placement readiness, sector-selection guidance and interview preparedness. The programme received an encouraging response and served as a structured bridge between the declaration of the CA Final results and the commencement of placement activities.

(II) 64th ICAI Campus Placement Programme The 64th ICAI Campus Placement Programme was organised across 29 centres (9 major and 20 smaller centres) during April–May 2026 for Chartered Accountants who qualified in the January 2026 CA Final Examination, marking the third campus placement opportunity within a year for recently qualified members. The programme witnessed the registration of 5,834 candidates (out of 7,592 newly qualified CAs) and participation of over 136 organisations, offering 9,000+ vacancies, reflecting continued trust in ICAI's Campus Placement Programme and strong employer confidence.

Participation from a wide spectrum of reputed organisations, including large MNCs, technology companies, consulting firms, professional services networks, Global Capability Centres, start-ups and leading Indian corporates, further underscored the industry's confidence in ICAI's talent pool. The campus cycle also recorded robust demand across both traditional and emerging domains, including finance, audit, consulting, data analytics, AI workflow automation, business intelligence, investment banking, private equity, risk management, compliance and digital transformation.

The programme witnessed strong hiring demand despite global geopolitical uncertainties, restructuring activity and layoffs reported in certain sectors. Annual CTCs offered ranged from ₹9 lakh to ₹27.50 lakh, and a standardised orientation programme was conducted across all centres to familiarise candidates with the placement process, career opportunities, employer expectations and interview readiness.

(III) CA Jobs Portal In addition to campus placement initiatives, the Directorate continued to strengthen the CA Jobs Portal (https://cajobs.icai.org/), which provides 24x7, 365-day employment opportunities for Chartered Accountants across sectors and experience levels. Through the portal, the Institute facilitates hiring through virtual interviews and immediate vacancy fulfilment at various levels, offering opportunities for senior-level CA positions, women-focused placements, work-from-home opportunities, lateral placements for experienced Chartered Accountants and Director-level opportunities. Employers with urgent hiring requirements can directly source qualified CA talent through the portal or write to icai.placement@icai.in for assistance and support.

(IV) Management Development Programme for CA Rank Holders The Directorate conducted the Management Development Programme for CA Rank Holders of the January 2026 batch in collaboration with the Indian Institute of Management Kozhikode. The programme was designed to provide structured management exposure to rank holders in areas such as strategic thinking, leadership, communication, AI for finance professionals, governance, decisionmaking and emerging business leadership perspectives.

Out of 157 eligible rank holders, 133 participated in the programme, reflecting a highly encouraging response; considering the number of participants and residential logistics, the programme was organised in two separate batches to ensure effective delivery, focused learning and smooth participant management. The programme received highly encouraging feedback from both the participating rank holders, who appreciated the quality of academic inputs, practical exposure, peer learning and the leadership-oriented design, and the Management Institute, which acknowledged the enthusiasm, discipline and active engagement of the CA rank holders throughout the sessions.

Students related Boards/Committee

10.1 BOARD OF STUDIES – ACADEMICS The Board of Studies- Academics, a non-standing Committee of ICAI, formulates and implements policies and programmes relating to education and training of students. The mission of the Board of Studies is to provide a strong foundation of professional knowledge, skills and values, ethics and attitudes that enable students of chartered accountancy course to evolve as competent professionals and adapt to changes throughout their professional career. The Board of Studies (Academic) significantly contributes to the academic and professional growth of aspiring CA students by offering comprehensive educational support, continuous guidance and mentorship throughout their journey towards becoming Chartered Accountants.

To strengthen students' professional knowledge and values, the BoS (A) offers quality Study Materials, Revision Test Papers, Mock Test Papers, Suggested Answers, Saransh – Last Mile Referencer and Case Scenario Booklets for all subjects of the CA course. It also promotes effective learning through digital initiatives such as Live Virtual Classes, Live Virtual Revisionary Classes, the ICAI Digital Learning Campus and the MCQ Paper Practice Assessment Dashboard.

The key initiatives of the year include:

(I) Student-Centred Learning Resources • Value-Added Study Material: In a step to enhance academic support, the Board released Value-Added Study Material for the Foundation, Intermediate and Final levels for the May 2026 Examinations. This comprehensive material also covers all Self-Paced Online Modules – Sets A, B, C and D – and has been made accessible on the BoS Knowledge Portal. • Saransh – Last Mile Referencer: Saransh, a comprehensive booklet summarising core concepts, provisions and standards through visual summaries, pictorial diagrams, concise flowcharts and tables, was prepared and released for the Final level: Paper 2 – Advanced Financial Management and Paper 3 – Advanced Auditing, Assurance and Professional Ethics. Further, Saransh for the Taxation papers of the Intermediate level (Paper 3A: Income-tax Law; Paper 3B:

Goods and Services Tax) and the Final level (Paper 4 – Direct Tax Laws and International Taxation; Paper 5 – Indirect Tax Laws) was also updated. • Case Study Digest of Integrated Business Solutions, Volume II: The Case Study Digest Volume II for the Integrated Business Solutions paper of the CA Final course was released, comprising 35 immersive case studies drawn from a wide range of sectors. It offers a boardroomlevel learning experience, encouraging students to step into the shoes of key decision-makers such as CFOs, Finance Directors, CEOs and Auditors. • Revision Test Papers (RTPs): RTPs incorporating a variety of questions in each subject, including questions on amendments, were released for the May 2025, September 2025, January 2026 and May 2026 Examinations, benefiting students across all levels. • Suggested Answers: For the first time in ICAI's history, Suggested Answers for the May 2025, September 2025 and January 2026 Examinations were web-hosted within 24 hours of the declaration of results.

(II) Comprehensive Student Support Services • Live Virtual Classes (LVCs)/Live Virtual Revisionary Classes (LVRCs): LVCs were conducted for the Foundation, Intermediate and Final courses for each examination cycle, and LVRCs were conducted for the Intermediate and Final courses. The viewership statistics are as under:

LVC/LVRC Batches Viewership Statistics LVC Foundation 21,39,612 LVC Intermediate 32,09,100 LVC Final 3,62,468 LVC/LVRC Batches Viewership Statistics LVRC Intermediate 3,12,224 LVRC Final 1,83,356 • Special Sessions on CA Final Paper 6 – Integrated Business Solutions: Special sessions on CA Final Paper 6 (IBS) were launched, and 35 such sessions were conducted during the year. • Open House Query Sessions for the Final Course: Launched "BoS Believes in You: Let's Conquer CA Final Together", a series of subject-wise live Open House query sessions for CA Final students. • Mock Test Papers (MTPs): Two series of MTPs were conducted for each paper of the Foundation, Intermediate and Final courses before each examination. During the year, 59,157 students participated in these mock test series, which were conducted through the Regional Councils and Branches of ICAI. • Mock Test Registration Portal:

A centralised Mock Test Registration Portal (https://bosmtp.icai.org/) was launched to streamline the conduct of Mock Test Papers (Series I and II) for the Foundation, Intermediate and Final courses across all ICAI Branches and Regional Offices. • Subject-specific "Success at CA Exam" Sessions: Subject-specific sessions were conducted by BoS faculty after the MTPs to clarify doubts, strengthen understanding and streamline preparation. • Fortnight Programme – "Unlock Success through Clarity, Strategy & Confidence": Two Fortnight Programmes were conducted in virtual mode for Intermediate and Final students in November 2025 and March 2026, covering one paper of the Intermediate/Final course every day through expert-driven sessions aimed at preparing students effectively for the upcoming examinations, with insights on course structure, section-wise weightage, preparation tips and common mistakes to avoid.

(III) Student Handholding Initiatives • Induction Programme for Newly Registered Foundation Students: Two Induction Programmes were conducted in May 2025 and October 2025 for newly registered Foundation students to ensure a strong start, with around 10,000 and 8,000 students participating respectively. The programmes provided students with a comprehensive understanding of ICAI's legacy, vision, structure and available services. • "Charcha Ka Vishay: CA Pariksha – Let's Talk: The CA Exam": Organised in April 2025, August 2025 and December 2025 in hybrid mode, with approximately 7,000 students participating. The initiative offered students a direct platform to interact with experts, clarify queries and overcome examination-related concerns. • Mentoring Programme for CA Students – "CA Saarthi": Launched on 1st July 2025, the programme is designed to guide, support and handhold CA students in their academic journey by connecting experienced Chartered Accountants as mentors with students as mentees. An exclusive Mentorship Portal (https://bosmentor.icai.org/) has been built wherein mentors and mentees can register themselves. • Sessions on "Insight into Audit – Bridging Theory with Practice": A series of online sessions was initiated to provide students with practical exposure to real-world audit procedures; 15 sessions have been conducted in this regard.

(IV) Digital Support Services • Digital Learning Campus Portal – SPOM: For the Self-Paced Online Modules (SPOM) on the Digital Learning Campus Portal, 8 expert-led video lecture series of 20 hours each, covering 8 different subjects from Sets C and D, were released. • MCQ Paper Practice Assessment Dashboard: The portal offers subject-specific MCQs to aid students in refining their examination readiness, with features including a user-friendly platform, paper-wise and chapter-wise performance tracking and MCQs of varying difficulty levels. As of now, a total of 3,28,529 students have accessed the dashboard. • ICAI-BoS Mobile App and BoS Knowledge Portal: The ICAI-BoS Mobile App and Knowledge Portal (https://boslive.icai.org/) serve as a holistic platform offering a comprehensive package of educational resources to students. As of now, 8,61,529 students have accessed the App/Portal and 37,387 subject-specific queries have been answered.

(V) The Chartered Accountant Student (Students' Journal) The Chartered Accountant Student, a monthly publication of the Board of Studies (Academic) specifically for CA students, was released regularly during the year. It provides educational content, academic updates, study aids and professional guidance relevant to the CA curriculum.

(VI) Towards Partner in Nation Building • Global Education Summit on Commerce and Accountancy (GES-CA) 2026: A two-day Global Education Summit on Commerce and Accountancy was successfully organised in New Delhi, bringing together Vice-Chancellors, policymakers, industry leaders, Directors, Principals, Deans, Professors and distinguished professionals from 24 States and 2 Union Territories. The discussions, led by eminent domain experts, traversed pivotal areas, and a booklet on the "ICAI Model Curriculum for Commerce and Accountancy Education" was launched at the Summit, proposing a unified, forward-looking curriculum at the Secondary, Senior Secondary and Undergraduate levels. • Memoranda of Understanding (MoUs) for Academic Collaboration: ICAI, through the BoS (A), has entered into strategic MoUs with several prestigious State and Central Universities.

As of this year, ICAI has successfully signed 22 MoUs with Universities and Institutions across the country, nurturing partnerships that will enhance the quality of education and provide students with enriched learning opportunities. • Faculty Development Programmes and Joint Seminars: To equip faculty members with advanced knowledge and modern pedagogical approaches, Faculty Development Programmes

(FDPs) were organised on contemporary and highly relevant themes. The Board also collaborated with Universities and Colleges to host Joint Seminars on pertinent topics, facilitating knowledgesharing and professional growth among faculty members.

(VII) Brainstorming Meet for Academic Excellence The Board of Studies (Academic) undertook an initiative through the Brainstorming Meet for Academic Excellence, aimed at strengthening the academic framework of the CA profession by evaluating the skill sets of newly qualified Chartered Accountants, identifying academic gaps and improving educational resources to develop future-ready professionals. The Meet was organised in 11 cities across the country.

10.2 BOARD OF STUDIES – OPERATIONS The Board of Studies – Operations, a non-standing committee of the Institute, is continuously focusing on providing world-class training to Chartered Accountancy students. It conducts regulatory training programmes for Chartered Accountancy students, including Orientation, Information Technology, Advanced Information Technology, and Management & Communication Skills courses through its IT and Soft Skills Centres across India, as well as four-week residential programmes at the Centres of Excellence in Hyderabad and Jaipur.

The Board also organises various student-focused events, including International, National, State and Branch-level conferences, seminars, workshops, sports competitions, festivals, counselling programmes, industrial visits, talent contests and provides scholarships to CA students. These initiatives are aimed at equipping students with professional competencies, managerial, communication, presentation, and soft skills to meet the evolving requirements of the accountancy profession and the changing business and regulatory environment.

(I) Implementation of the Revised Syllabus of Advanced IT Training The Revised Syllabus of Advanced Information Technology Training was implemented during the year, and batches under the revised syllabus are now being conducted by IT labs across the country.

(II) Faculty Development and Train the Trainer Programmes • Four Train the Trainer Programmes were conducted at Jaipur, New Delhi, Kolkata and Mumbai on the new topics of the Revised Syllabus of IT & Soft Skills Training. • Online Faculty Development Programmes were conducted for the faculties of IT & Advanced IT Training. • A live webinar was conducted on the occasion of the Teachers' Day Celebration held on 5th September 2025, wherein the President, ICAI virtually addressed the students and faculty members of IT & Soft Skills Training. • Three Interactive Meetings were held with the faculties of IT and Soft Skills Training, and an online meeting of the Chairman and Vice-Chairman, BoS – Operations with the faculties was also conducted.

(III) IT & Soft Skills Training and Quality Monitoring • A total of 4,452 batches of IT and Soft Skills Training were conducted during the year, and 1,61,299 students were trained. • 465 Smart Classroom equipment sets were installed at the IT and Soft Skills Centres across the country. • Regular online monitoring of batches of IT & Soft Skills Training was carried out through Zoom links from the Head Office, supplemented by the observation of batches by faculties/members appointed as Observers, to improve the quality of training imparted to students across the country.

(IV) Student Engagement and Skill Development • Two-Day Workshop on Artificial Intelligence – AURA: A Two-Day Workshop on Artificial Intelligence for CA students was introduced under the banner of AI Understanding for Rising Achievers (AURA). A total of 100 physical batches were conducted through Regional Councils and Branches, training 3823 students, while 7,910 students were trained in AI through the online mode. • Live Webinar Series – RUN@100: 90 live webinars were conducted under the series RUN@100, focused on Reskilling, Upskilling and Acquiring New Skills for CA students, which were viewed by 5.35 lakh students. • Interactive Sessions with Students: Fifteen online Interactive Sessions were conducted with students undergoing IT & Soft Skills Training through the Smart Classroom Boards installed at the IT & Soft Skills Centres across the country. These sessions were addressed by the President, ICAI, the Chairman and Vice-Chairman, BoS – Operations, and Central Council Members. • E-Diary: An E-Diary was introduced for students commencing practical training on or after 1st January 2026.

(V) CA Students Conferences and Activities • A total of 63 CA Students Conferences were organised during the year, comprising 1 International Conference, 1 All India Excellence Conference, 23 National Conferences, 21 Mega Conferences, 15 State-Level Conferences and 2 Branch-Level Conferences. Topics on Soft Skills Development were included in the programme structure of the National Conferences of CA Students. • CA Students National Talent Search: Twelve competitions – Pitch Deck, Debate, Elocution, Quiz, Essay, Chess, Drama by Students' Teams, Sketching, Instrumental Music, Poetry, Extempore and Aurathon (AI) – were conducted for CA students under the banner of the CA Students National Talent Search. These activities were conducted first at the Branch level, then at the Regional level and finally at the National level by the Board. • In addition, 1,184 student activities were organised during the year, comprising 249 One-Day Seminars, 431 Half-Day Seminars, 232 Workshops, 142 Sports Competitions, 62 CA Students Festivals and 68 Special Counselling Programmes.

(VI) Scholarships for CA Students A new Scholarship Scheme was approved by the Council, and revised rates of scholarship became applicable from January 2026. The Scholarship Corpus Fund was increased from ₹100 crore to ₹500 crore. A scholarship grant for Foundation Course students was also introduced. The revised rates of scholarship grant payable under different categories are as under:

Category Revised Rate Merit Award for Final Course students (Rank holders of Intermediate Examinations up to 10th rank) ₹5,000/- per month Economically Needy Students Scholarship for Final Course students – Merit-based (Rank holders of Intermediate Examinations from 11th to 50th rank) ₹4,000/- per month Economically Needy Students Scholarship for Final & Intermediate Course students ₹2,500/- per month Economically Needy Students Scholarship for Foundation Course students ₹1,500/- per month

(VII) Coordination with Regional Councils and Branches • Five Interactive Meetings were held with the Managing Committee Members of Regional Councils and Branches, wherein issues relating to students' activities were discussed.

10.3 COMMITTEE ON CAREER COUNSELLING The Career Counselling Committee of ICAI, constituted in 2015, serves as the outreach arm of the Institute, dedicated to creating awareness among students and parents about Chartered Accountancy and career opportunities in the commerce domain. In alignment with the national vision of a Viksit Bharat and sustained economic development, the Committee recognizes that a strong foundation in commerce education is essential to build a financially literate, ethical, and competent workforce. During 2025-26, the Committee intensified its efforts to reach students in schools, colleges, and Tier-2/Tier-3 cities through career seminars, mentorship drives, and digital outreach. By equipping students with information on academic pathways, skill requirements, and career prospects, the Committee is committed to building a robust pipeline of future professionals who will contribute to nation-building and the vision of Viksit Bharat.

Key activities Project Vanijya - The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), launched "Project Vanijya - Shaping the Future of Students for Bharat as a Developed Country" on 19th December 2025 at New Delhi. The initiative aims to strengthen commerce education from the school level in line with the Hon'ble Prime Minister's vision of Viksit Bharat @2047. The Representatives of Education Boards from 15 States across India were presented. The programme focuses on guiding students towards commerce education and professional opportunities, contributing to India's journey towards becoming a developed nation.

Exposure Visits of CBSE School Principals- The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India signed an MoU with CBSE to promote commerce education and career awareness among students. As part of this initiative, the Committee on Career Counselling organised Exposure Visits for CBSE School Principals at the Centres of Excellence in Jaipur and Hyderabad, as well as at Mumbai, Indore, and Guwahati.

These visits familiarised principals with the Chartered Accountancy (CA) Course, new education scheme, and diverse career opportunities in the profession. The initiative received an encouraging response, with principals expressing keen interest in conducting career counselling programmes in their schools.

Some participants and their family members also enrolled for the CA Course, reflecting the growing impact of the initiative.

Faculty Development Programmes (FDP)- Committee organised a series of Faculty Development Programmes (FDPs) titled "Career Counsellors Empowerment Meet" to enhance awareness about the New Scheme of Education and Training among career counsellors across India. The initiative aimed to equip counsellors with the necessary knowledge and skills to guide and motivate students towards pursuing the Chartered Accountancy (CA) Course as a promising career option. During the year, the Committee successfully conducted 11 FDPs across Pan India, benefiting approximately 632 career counsellors and strengthening ICAI's outreach efforts nationwide.

Career Counselling Programmes- The Committee has organized 3,386 Career Counselling Programmes benefiting approximately 6,13,179 students across the country. Additionally, the Committee participated in 24 career/education fairs across India, reaching more than 16,840 students.

Awards & Recognition Guinness World Record- The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India achieved a Guinness World Record for conducting the "Largest Careers Advice Lesson" with an objective to help students to choose the right career path for their future in different streams held in Mumbai on 27th November, 2025. The event witnessed participation from over 7,400 students, teachers, principals, and stakeholders, with an eligible record count of 6,166 students. The Guinness World Records jury officially recognised and felicitated ICAI for this remarkable achievement.

World Book of Records: The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) achieved a World Book of Records recognition for successfully organizing the Career in Accounting and Finance for Youth (CAFY 4.0) and Super Mega Career Counselling Programme simultaneously across 87 branches on a single day, i.e., 27th November 2025, benefiting over 1.20 lakh students nationwide. The initiative provided comprehensive career guidance in the field of Commerce, empowering students to make informed decisions and choose the right career path for their future.

11. Section – 8 Companies

11.1 XBRL INDIA For the effective participation of regulators, technologists, corporates and academicians in the Institute's endeavour to promote XBRL in India, the Institute facilitated the incorporation of XBRL India as a Section 8 Company (formerly Section 25 under the Companies Act, 1956), with the main objective of promoting and encouraging the adoption of XBRL as the standard for electronic business reporting in India, through the development of taxonomies and facilitation of education and training. XBRL India is an established Indian jurisdiction of XBRL International.

(I) Development and Maintenance of Taxonomies for MCA XBRL India is currently developing and maintaining XBRL taxonomies for the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA). Two Taxonomies are presently applicable to companies: the Ind AS Taxonomy and the Commercial and Industrial (C&I) Taxonomy. XBRL India submitted samples of Instance Documents for testing purposes of taxonomies to MCA (initially submitted in 2022), and meetings and discussions were held during the year with MCA officials regarding the updation of taxonomies. Queries and issues faced by filers with regard to taxonomies while converting Financial Statements to XBRL formats have also been resolved.

(II) Development of New Taxonomies Apart from the Ind AS and C&I taxonomies, XBRL India has proposed to MCA the development of taxonomies for the following sectors (currently under review by MCA): Non-Banking Finance Companies

(NBFCs); Insurance Entities (both life and non-life insurance companies, separately).

(III) Participation in International Activities As a member of XBRL International, the contribution of XBRL India extends beyond India. Board members of XBRL India regularly represent India at various meetings and events of XBRL International.

During the year, Board members participated in the XBRL International Member Assembly and in Data Amplified 2025. As a Board member, Immediate Past President attended the Board of Directors meeting of XBRL International held on 28th-29th April, 2025.

11.2 ICAI- Accounting Research Foundation (ARF) ICAI Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF) was established in January, 1999 to be developed as an academy for imparting, spreading and promoting knowledge, learning, education and understanding in the various fields related to the profession of accountancy. The following is the detail of projects undertaken by ICAI ARF:  Indian Railways: Indian Railways, the fourth largest railway network in the world after the United States, Russia and China, is undertaking several initiatives towards Accounting Reforms in line with generally accepted accounting principles. In its journey of accounting reforms, the Indian Railways had initiated exercise of preparation of its Accrual Based Financial Statements in 2014, marking a major shift towards enhanced financial transparency and accountability.

Since the inception of this initiative, the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) and its Accounting Research Foundation (ICAI ARF) have played a pivotal role in supporting these reforms.

ICAI ARF has been instrumental in guiding Indian Railways through the migration to an accrualbased accounting system. ICAI ARF have successfully prepared the Accrual Based Financial Statements for all Zonal Railways and Production Units from the financial year 2015-16 to 202223 under the grafting method.

Initiatives during the year

1. For the first time, Accrual Based Financial Statements (ABFS) of Indian Railways under transactional method for the financial year 2023-24 prepared and submitted. Several initiatives towards environment Sustainability undertaken by the Indian Railways have been reflected in the financial statements. ABFS for the year 2023-24 under grafting method is also under development for comparison with new transactional method.

2. ABFS of Indian Railways for the year 2024-25 under transactional method is under progress.

3. Proposal to review Accounts/Finance Codes, relevance of various account heads and allocation/classification of expenditure and earnings on Indian Railways (RAFC) has been initiated and is currently under progress. .  Treasuries and Accounts Department, Government of Tamil Nadu: ICAI ARF has undertaken the task of Re-writing of Tamil Nadu Financial Code, Volume I & II. The ICAI has submitted the Report during the year to the department.  Impact Study of GST reforms 2.0 on Scheme of Budgetary Support under Goods and Services Tax Regime - The Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade (DPIIT) awarded the project to ICAI ARF for undertaking the study of GST reforms 2.0 on Scheme of Budgetary Support under Goods and Services Tax Regime. The project is under progress.  Indian Ports Association (IPA): ICAI ARF had formulated and submitted the Report on Common Framework for the preparation of annual accounts for Major Ports and Significant Accounting Policies for all ports to the IPA. Both the documents were under Ministry of Shipping Port and Waterways (MoPSW) for notifying in consultation with CAG for implementation in all Major Ports. In this regard, various meetings were held with the officials of C&AG and Ministry of Shipping Port and Waterways during the year.  Certificate Course for Accountants of Panchayats & Municipal Bodies: ICAI ARF executed the project of Certificate Courses for Accountants of Panchayats and Municipal Bodies through Board for Local Bodies Accountants Certification (i.e., BLoAC established under ICAI ARF) in collaboration with the Committee on Public and Government Financial Management

(CPGFM) of ICAI. The courses were formally launched on 16th November 2023 (Audit Diwas) and the registrations of these courses were initiated on 1st December 2023 through a dedicated website https://lba.icaiarf.org.in/. Approx. 5000 candidates have registered for this course till date. As per structure of course, four Screening Exams and four Main Exams have already been held till date.

11.3 ICAI REGISTERED VALUERS ORGANISATION (ICAI RVO) The ICAI Registered Valuers Organisation (ICAI RVO) is a Section 8 company established by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI). It was incorporated on 23rd February, 2018 to enrol and regulate Registered Valuers as its members in accordance with the Companies (Registered Valuers and Valuation) Rules, 2017. ICAI RVO functions as a Registered Valuers Organisation under the authority of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India (IBBI).

The key activities and achievements:

(I) Enrolment of Members (FY 2025-26) • Total Primary Members enrolled: 749 • Total Registered Valuer Members enrolled: 83

(II) Empowering Members through Education and Training Educational Course – Asset-class-wise batches conducted:

Asset Class Batches Conducted Participants Trained Securities or Financial Assets Land and Building Plant and Machinery Continuous Educational Programmes (CEP) – Training conducted:

Asset Class Trainings Conducted Hours Imparted Participants Trained Securities or Financial Assets (SFA) 2,056 Plant and Machinery (P&M) L&B and SFA L&B and P&M L&B, P&M and SFA 1,133 Total 3,362 • Master Class on Valuation: Master Classes were conducted, imparting 20 hours of training to 433 participants. • Certificate of Practice (COP) Training: 8 COP trainings were conducted, training 77 members.

(III) Capacity Building and Technological Advancements • Learning Management System (LMS): ICAI RVO's online Learning Management System provides a holistic and easily accessible learning platform for its members and is updated on a regular basis. • Course Material for New Asset Classes: ICAI RVO released the First Edition of the Educational Course Material for Land & Building and Plant & Machinery in June 2025, following the syllabus mandated by the IBBI for the Valuation Examination for these respective asset classes.

(IV) Events The 3rd edition of RESOLVE-2025, an international convention on insolvency and resolution themed

"Enabling Resolution, Maximising Value", was organised on 1st –2nd December 2025 at Mumbai, by ICAI in association with the IBBI, IICA, IIIPI and ICAI RVO.

(V) Monitoring of Members • Monitoring: The Half-Yearly Report (HYR) for the period up to 30th September, 2025 was successfully concluded and reported to the IBBI, while submission for the March 2026 period is underway. • Peer Review: 38 valuation assignments relating to 15 Registered Valuers and 4 Registered Valuer Entities were covered. • Inspection: 15 valuation reports of 15 Registered Valuers were inspected. The findings of both exercises have been finalised and are proposed to be placed before the Monitoring Committee for consideration and approval.

(VI) Publications Four quarterly journals of ICAI RVO were released during the year – Volume 1, Issue 5 (April 2025);

Issue 6 (July 2025); Issue 7 (October 2025); and Issue 8 (January 2026).

11.4 INDIAN INSTITUTE OF INSOLVENCY PROFESSIONALS OF ICAI (IIIPI) The Indian Institute of Insolvency professionals of ICAI 'IIIPI', a wholly owned subsidiary of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India 'ICAI', is a section 8 Company promoted by ICAI to enrol and regulate insolvency professionals as its members in accordance with the Insolvency and Bankruptcy code 2016 and read with regulations. IIIPI has been awarded with the registration certificate as the First Insolvency Professional Agency of India by Hon'ble Union Finance Minister on 28th November 2016 at Delhi. IIIPI is the largest IPA with nearly two-third of members in India, has attracted members from a diverse stream including Chartered Accountant, Company Secretary, Cost Accountant, Advocates and Management Professionals. It has networked with other national and international bodies in furtherance of the capacity and knowledge building of stakeholders on the one hand and providing value-added policy inputs for the evolving law and practice of the IBC on the other.

IIIPI is one of the key pillars in the insolvency and bankruptcy regime. It is a front-line regulator required to discharge multiple statutory responsibilities. It is responsible for the development and regulation of insolvency profession and professionals, besides building knowledge and capacity of stakeholders. In a parlance, it is a mini-State, last in the hierarchy of parliamentary delegation of authority with regulatory, executive and quasi-judicial responsibilities.

Out of the total 4,643 Insolvency Professionals registered with the IBBI as on 30th April 2026, 2,907, i.e. 62.61%, are from IIIPI. IIIPI's members have so far handled approximately 75% of the overall assignments of CIRP/Liquidation/Voluntary Liquidation, including many of the largest cases.

(II) Capacity Building Initiatives Dialogues and deliberations are crucial to the capacity building of professional members in any profession, and even more so in the context of the insolvency profession, which impacts multiple stakeholders and is considered one of the most complex professions. The performance of the IBC ecosystem is, to a great extent, directly linked to the capacity building of IPs. IIIPI's endeavour has been not only to sustain the momentum but also to keep launching capacity-building initiatives to meet stakeholders' expectations. The key initiatives during the year include:

S. No.

Programme / Activity 18 Executive Development Programmes (EDPs) across five domains 44 Webinars on contemporary topics (3-hour format) 23 One-Day Workshops on multiple topics 7 Roundtables on IBBI Discussion Papers and Emerging Issues 9 LIE Examination Preparation Classes (5-day format) 3 Virtual Half-Day Workshops on Common Issues in association with IBBI and other IPAs 2 Physical Half-Day Workshops on Common Issues in association with IBBI and other IPAs National Research Conclave in association with NALSAR Hyderabad and IICA Manesar Residential Workshop on the IBC in association with IBBI 10 3rd International Convention on Insolvency Resolution and Valuation (RESOLVE-2025) 11 2 Conferences on IBC-related topics in association with PHDCCI 12 5 Three-Day Training Programmes organised by IBBI and other IPAs S. No.

Programme / Activity 13 Training Programme on Limited Insolvency Examination (LIE Course) for PGIP Students 14 Revival and Resilience: India Turnaround Summit 2026 15 Seminar on the IBC (Amendment) Bill, 2026 16 Webinar Series – IBC Reform Dialogue 2026 – IBC Amendment Act and IBBI Discussion Paper

(III) Research and Studies Conducted • During FY 2025-26, the following study reports/research documents/internal study reports were developed:

(a) Developing the Market for Distressed Assets in India; and

(b) Taxation/Accounting/Company Compliances required under the IBC – Best Practices. • 5 research projects have been approved so far by IIIPI under its Research Project Scheme, in the areas of real estate resolutions, the PPIRP framework, insolvency laws in India vis-à-vis internationally, and resolving insolvency in FPCs in East India.

(IV) Monitoring of Insolvency Professionals As per the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India (Model Bye-laws and Governing Board of Insolvency Professional Agencies) Regulations, 2016, IIIPI is required to monitor the professional activities and conduct of its professional members for their adherence to the provisions of the Code, Rules, Regulations and Guidelines issued thereunder, the Bye-laws, the Code of Conduct and the directions given by the Governing Board. The monitoring, including inspections, is continuous and covers assignments undertaken by IPs, particularly Corporate Insolvency Resolution Processes (CIRP), Liquidation Processes, Voluntary Liquidations and individual insolvency pertaining to Personal Guarantors to Corporate Debtors.

It involves a review of records, reports, filings, timelines and decision-making, to assess whether the IP has acted with integrity, independence and due diligence. Any deviations, lapses or non-compliances identified during the monitoring process are examined and appropriate actions are initiated in accordance with the applicable framework, which may include seeking explanations, issuing warnings, imposing penalties or referring the matter for disciplinary proceedings, thereby ensuring accountability and maintaining high standards of professional practice.

(V) Grievance Redressal and Disciplinary Mechanism • During the period, IIIPI received 78 grievances and disposed of 97 grievances (including grievances pertaining to the previous year). Since inception, the Grievance Redressal Committee has disposed of 507 cases, with 32 matters remaining pending. • IIIPI initiated 15 disciplinary proceedings during the period and disposed of 23 matters (including matters pertaining to previous years). A total of 235 cases have been disposed of, while 9 matters remained pending. • Further, one appeal was received and disposed of during the period. 4 appeals have been disposed of since inception, with no cases pending.

11.5

THE INSTITUTE OF SOCIAL AUDITORS OF INDIA (ISAI) The Institute of Social Auditors of India (ISAI) was incorporated under Section 8 of the Companies Act, 2013 on 7th October, 2022, under the aegis of ICAI, as a Self-Regulatory Organisation with the prime objective of being a leading institution for developing independent, ethical and world-class Social Impact Assessors (earlier Social Auditors) responding to the needs and expectations of stakeholders. ISAI enrols, regulates and develops Social Impact Assessors in an independent and transparent manner and also works on the capacity building of Social Impact Assessors through continuous professional education and training.

(I) Governing Board of ISAI ISAI functions through its Governing Board, which comprises a minimum of twelve Directors, namely:

(a) a Chairperson having rich experience in the social sector/impact assessment, or from a renowned academic institution; (b) five Central Council Members of ICAI/the Sustainability Reporting Standards Board with relevant experience; (c) one person nominated by the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI); (d) one person nominated by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA)/Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA)/Ministry of Social Justice & Empowerment; (e) one person nominated by the Capacity Building Fund; (f) one person nominated by the Social Stock Exchange; (g) one member from the thematic areas of the social sector; and (h) one member having cross-sectoral experience in social sectors.

For the period from 1st April, 2025 to 31st March, 2026, two Governing Board meetings were convened at New Delhi – the 10th Governing Board Meeting on 24th June, 2025 and the 11th Governing Board Meeting on 2nd September 2025. The 3rd Annual General Meeting (AGM) of the Company was held on 18th September 2025, and the Annual Return and financials have been filed with the MCA.

(II) Sub-Committees of ISAI Seven Sub-Committees have been constituted in terms of the Bye-laws of the Company to regulate its working in the most transparent and efficient manner, comprising members of the Governing Board, namely the Advisory Committee, Membership Committee, Monitoring Committee, Grievance Redressal Committee, Appellate Committee, Disciplinary Committee and Editorial Board. During the financial year 2025-26, two Membership Committee meetings were convened, on 6th November, 2025 and 19th January, 2026, through virtual/physical mode, wherein important decisions related to the allotment of membership of ISAI were taken.

(III) Registration of Social Impact Assessors ISAI has registered 765 Social Impact Assessors, the details of which are available on the website of the Company.

(IV) Online Training Course on the NISM Series XXIII: Social Impact Assessor Certification Examination ISAI conducts an online training course to facilitate preparation for the "NISM Series XXIII: Social Impact Assessor Certification Examination". The online classes cover all 11 chapters of the NISM study material across 21 study hours with expert faculties, MCQs for each chapter, case studies and illustrative examples. ISAI conducted one batch of the online training course during the financial year 2025-26 and trained 30 participants for the examination.

(V) Webinars ISAI organised 5 webinars during the year for capacity building and to create awareness about the Social Stock Exchange, Social Audit and Social Impact Assessment amongst stakeholders and the public at large:

11.6 ICAI INTERNATIONAL ADR CENTRE (IIAC) The ICAI International ADR Centre (IIAC), a Section 8 Company established by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India, was formally launched on 30th July, 2025 by Hon'ble Minister of State (Independent Charge) for Law & Justice and Minister of State for Parliamentary Affairs, Government of India, at New Delhi and incorporated on 8th August, 2025. The IIAC was set up with the objective of promoting and institutionalising Alternative Dispute Resolution (ADR) mechanisms through arbitration, mediation, conciliation and other dispute resolution processes. The Centre seeks to provide efficient, transparent, technology-enabled and cost-effective dispute resolution services in commercial, financial, contractual, corporate and economic matters while upholding the principles of fairness, neutrality, confidentiality and procedural efficiency.

Major developments:  The IIAC marked ICAI's entry into the institutional Alternative Dispute Resolution ecosystem and reaffirmed its commitment towards supporting efficient dispute resolution mechanisms and ease of doing business.  Statutory and regulatory compliances, including filings with the Ministry of Corporate Affairs, were undertaken for establishing the institutional framework of the Centre.  Drafting and finalisation of Arbitration Rules and Mediation Frameworks were initiated, drawing upon international best practices and aligned with the Indian legal and regulatory framework.  A Governing Board comprising representatives of ICAI, eminent legal professionals, former institutional leaders and experienced practitioners was constituted to provide strategic direction and strengthen the Centre's institutional governance and credibility.  Going forward, IIAC aims to emerge as a premier ADR institution by fostering capacity building, promoting awareness of ADR mechanisms, facilitating domestic and international dispute resolution, and contributing to the development of a robust, efficient and trusted dispute resolution framework.

QUALITY REVIEW BOARD (QRB) The Quality Review Board (QRB), established by the Central Government of India under Section 28A of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 (The 'Act'), operates as an independent body to review the quality of services rendered by Chartered Accountants in India. The constitution of the Board consists of a Chairperson, five members appointed by the Central Government, and five members nominated by the Council of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), ensuring a balanced governance structure.

While statutorily empowered to examine quality of all professional services under Sec. 28B of the Act, the QRB is presently engaged in the review of quality of statutory audit services. In accordance with Section 28B, its principal functions consist of:  To make recommendations to the Council with regard to the quality of services provided by the members of the Institute.  To review the quality of services provided by the members of the Institute including audit services.  To guide the members of the Institute to improve the quality of services and adherence to the various statutory and other regulatory requirements.  To forward cases of non-compliance with various statutory and regulatory requirements by the members of the Institute or firms, noticed by it during the course of its reviews, to the Disciplinary Directorate for its examination.

Subsequent to the establishment of National Financial Reporting Authority (NFRA) by the Central Government in 2018, the jurisdiction of QRB has been correspondingly defined. The QRB is now mandated to undertake reviews of audit quality in respect of entities falling outside the ambit of Rule 3 of the NFRA Rules, 2018, as well as those referred to QRB by NFRA under Rule 9(4) of NFRA Rules,

2018.

(I) Quality Review Status of Selected Audit Firms:

(II) Capacity Building – Webinar for Empanelled Technical Reviewers The Board organised a webinar for empanelled Technical Reviewers to share experiences, update them on recent developments and communicate the Board's expectations, with a focus on quality enhancement, transparency and consistency in the review process. The programme was attended by 145 Reviewers along with their assistants and was well appreciated. The Board resolved that similar capacity-building initiatives would continue in the future.

(III) Recent Amendments and Developments by QRB for Quality Assurance • The "Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) for Conducting Quality Reviews" has been modified and updated, and the revised SOP has been hosted on the Board's website (https://www.qrbca.in/). • The rating criteria for Technical Reviewers has been revised to ensure the selection of highquality Reviewers, to facilitate the identification and management of underperformance, and thereby contribute to overall quality enhancement.

FINANCE AND ACCOUNTS As per the Section 18(5B) of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 , the Council shall cause to be published in the Gazette of India not later than the 30th day of September of the year next following, a copy of the audited accounts and the Report of the Council for that year duly approved by the Council. Accordingly, the Balance Sheet as on 31st March, 2026 and the Income & Expenditure Account for the year ended on that date as approved by the Council duly audited along with auditors' report are enclosed.

APPRECIATION The Council is grateful to members of the profession who functioned as co-opted members on its Committees, persons nominated on the Boards/Committees constituted under the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949, the Regional Councils, its branches, and their members, and to the special invitees and non-members who assisted the Council during the year 2025-26 in the conduct of its educational, technical and other developmental activities and in its examinations.

The Council wishes to place on record its appreciation of the continued assistance and support given by the Central Government and its nominees on the Council during the year 2025-26.

The Council also acknowledges its appreciation of the sincere interest evinced by various Central and State Governments in the numerous initiatives taken by the ICAI and the steps already/being initiated by them, pursuant to such initiatives.

The Council also acknowledges its appreciation of the sincere and devoted efforts put in during the year 2025-26 and thereafter by all officers and employees of the ICAI.

(ICLS), Regional Director, WR-II Shri Naveen Singhvi New Delhi PD (Com.II), C&AG (C&AG Nominee) New Delhi Shri Sanjay Sharan Indian Postal Service Group A (1989) Prayagraj Justice (Former) Shashi Kant Gupta Allahabad High Court Shri Mukhmeet Singh Bhatia, IAS-1990, (Retd) New Delhi Ghaziabad Shri Vinod Kumar Jindal Indian Cost Accounts Service (1988) ASA & Associates LLP Chartered Accountants Aurobindo Tower 81/1, Third Floor, Adchini Aurobindo Marg, New Delhi -110017 Jagdish Chand & Co Chartered Accountants H-20, LGF Green Park (Main) New Delhi-110016 INDEPENDENT AUDITOR'S REPORT To the Council of The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India Report on the Audit of the Financial Statements Opinion We have audited the accompanying financial statements of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India ("the Institute"), which comprise the Balance Sheet as at March 31, 2026, the Statement of Income and Expenditure, the Statement of Cash Flows for the year then ended, and notes to the financial statements, including a summary of significant accounting policies and other explanatory information (hereinafter referred to as "financial statements") in which are incorporated the returns/financial statements audited by the branch auditors of Institute's Decentralized offices, Overseas offices, Centers of Excellence, Regional Councils and their Branches.

In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to the explanations given to us, the aforesaid financial statements give the information required by the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 (as amended up to date) and give a true and fair view in conformity with the accounting principles generally accepted in India, of the state of affairs of the Institute as at March 31, 2026, and its surplus and its cash flows for the year ended on that date.

Basis for Opinion We conducted our audit in accordance with the Standards on Auditing (SAs) issued by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India. Our responsibilities under those Standards are further described in the 'Auditor's Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements' section of our report. We are independent of the Institute in accordance with the Code of Ethics issued by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) together with the ethical requirements that are relevant to our audit of the financial statements and we have fulfilled our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with these requirements. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion on the financial statements.

Responsibilities of Management and those charged with governance for the Financial Statements The Institute's Management and those charged with governance are responsible for the preparation of these financial statements that give a true and fair view of the financial position, financial performance, and cash flows of the Institute in accordance with the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949 ("the Act") This responsibility also includes maintenance of adequate accounting records in accordance with the provisions of the Act for safeguarding the assets of the Institute and for preventing and detecting frauds and other irregularities; selection and application of appropriate accounting policies; making judgments and estimates that are reasonable and prudent; and design, implementation and maintenance of adequate internal financial controls, that were operating effectively for ensuring the accuracy and completeness of the accounting records, relevant to the preparation and presentation of the financial statements that give a true and fair view and are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.

In preparing the financial statements, the management and those charged with governance are responsible for assessing the Institute's ability to continue as a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and using the going concern basis of accounting unless the management and those charged with governance either intends to liquidate the Institute or to cease operations, or has no realistic alternative but to do so.

Those charged with governance are also responsible for overseeing the Institute's financial reporting process.

Auditor's Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the financial statements as a whole are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor's report that includes our opinion. Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance, but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance with SAs will always detect a material misstatement when it exists.

Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are considered material if, individually or in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence the economic decisions of users taken on the basis of these financial statements.

As part of an audit in accordance with SAs, we exercise professional judgment and maintain professional skepticism throughout the audit. We also: • Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the financial statements, whether due to fraud or error, design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of internal controls. • Obtain an understanding of internal controls relevant to the audit in order to design audit procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the Institute's internal controls. • Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates and related disclosures made by management. • Conclude on the appropriateness of the management and those charged with governance's use of the going concern basis of accounting and, based on the audit evidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty exists related to events or conditions that may cast significant doubt on the Institute's ability to continue as a going concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty exists, we are required to draw attention in our auditor's report to the related disclosures in the financial statements or, if such disclosures are inadequate, to modify our opinion. Our conclusions are based on the audit evidence obtained up to the date of our auditor's report. However, future events or conditions may cause the Institute to cease to continue as a going concern. • Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and content of the financial statements, including the disclosures, and whether the financial statements represent the underlying transactions and events in a manner that achieves fair presentation. • Materiality is the magnitude of misstatements in the financial statements that, individually or in aggregate, makes it probable that the economic decisions of a reasonably knowledgeable user of the financial statements may be influenced. We consider quantitative materiality and qualitative factors in

(i) planning the scope of our audit work and in evaluating the results of our work; and (ii) to evaluate the effect of any identified misstatements in the financial statements.

We communicate with those charged with governance regarding, among other matters, the planned scope and timing of the audit and significant audit findings, including any significant deficiencies in internal controls that we identify during our audit.

We also provide those charged with governance with a statement that we have complied with relevant ethical requirements regarding independence, and to communicate with them all relationships and other matters that may reasonably be thought to bear on our independence, and where applicable, related safeguards.

Other Matters a) There are number of study circles, study chapters authorized by the Institute to facilitate continuous professional education activities for members in India and abroad. The Institute has represented that these study circles/chapters are separate entities and accordingly their financial statements are not required to be consolidated. We have relied on this representation. b) We did not audit the financial statements/information of the Institute's Decentralized Offices, Overseas Offices, Centers of Excellence, Regional Councils and their branches (collectively known as Branches) whose financial statements reflect total assets of ₹ 1,54,981 Lakhs and net assets of ₹ 34,385 Lakhs as at March 31, 2026, total revenue of ₹ 35,553 Lakhs and net cash outflows of ₹ 328 Lakhs for the year ended March 31, 2026, as considered in the financial statements. The Financial Statements/information of these Branches have been audited by the Branch Auditors whose reports have been furnished to us by the management and our opinion in so far as it relates to the amounts and disclosures included in respect of such Branches, is solely based on the reports of Branch Auditors. c) We did not audit the financial statements/information of the Institute's Singapore Overseas Office, Jhunjhunu & Korba Branch of Central India Regional Council of the Institute, Khammam of Southern India Regional Council of the Institute and Gondia & Morbi Branch of Western India Regional Council of the Institute, which reflect total assets of ₹ 3 Lakhs and net assets of ₹ (65) Lakhs as at March 31, 2026, total revenue of ₹ NIL Lakhs and net cash inflows of ₹NIL Lakhs as at March 31, 2026, as considered in the financial statements. Such financial statements/information are unaudited and have been furnished to us by the management and our report, in so far as it relates to the amount and disclosures, is based solely on the unaudited financial statement/information. According to the information and explanation given to us, these financial statements/information are not material to the Institute. d) The comparative financial statements of the Institute for the year ended March 31, 2025, were audited by predecessor auditors who expressed unmodified opinion on those financial statements in their audit report dated July 30, 2025.

Our opinion is not modified in respect of above matters.

Report on other Regulatory Requirements Further, we report that:

(a) We have sought and obtained all the information and explanations which to the best of our knowledge and belief were necessary for the purposes of our audit;

(b) In our opinion, proper books of account have been kept by the Institute so far as appears from our examination of those books and proper returns adequate for the purpose of our audit have been received from the branches not visited by us;

(c) The Reports on the accounts of the branches of the Institute audited by branch auditors have been sent to us and have been properly dealt with by us in preparing this report; and

(d) The Balance Sheet, the Statement of Income and Expenditure, and the Statement of Cash Flows dealt with by this Report are in agreement with the books of account and with the returns received from the branches not visited by us.
